These were similar in type to the monstrosities of the jungle; but they were more sparse and scattered; and somehow there was a difference. When Deming and Roverton approached them the nature of the difference became manifest. The ophidian branches dropped and trailed on the ground, and were strangely still and unmoving. Seen closer at hand, they were withered and mummified. It was evident to these scientists that the trees had long been dead.

===Chinese===
水汽在斜阳里散开。前方不远处，罗弗顿和戴明此时看见一处低矮的、桌面般的高地。乍看之下，它便像一座岛屿；待他们走近，它所显露的特征更表明它确曾是一座岛，而它周围的平原，在不很久远的年代，曾是一片浅海的海床。它山脚的土里有波浪的印痕；与平原的一毛不拔截然不同，它那长长的、起伏的坡上散布着巨石和树形之物；在它宽阔平坦的顶上，还看得见几段倾颓的墙垣和独石柱，属一种异界的建筑。

"且看一些仙女座的考古，"罗弗顿指着废墟评论道。

"还不止，还有更多植物学，"戴明补了一句。

两人都怀着相当的小心与不安，打量着最前面的那些树木与草木。它们与丛林里的那些怪物同类；可更为稀疏、更为零落；而且，总有些不一样。待戴明和罗弗顿走近，那不一样的性质便显了出来。那些蛇形的枝条垂落到地上拖曳着，出奇地静止不动。凑近一看，它们都干枯了，皱缩如木乃伊。这两位科学家一看便知，这些树早已死去多时。

===Original===
Not without repulsion, Roverton broke off the end of one of the hanging tentacles. It snapped easily; and he found that he could crumble it into fine powder between his fingers. Realizing that there was nothing to be feared, he and Deming began to climb the slope toward the fantastic ruins.

The soil of the hill, a sort of grey and purple marl, was firm beneath their feet. They reached the summit as the sinking sun began to disappear behind a far-off line of cliffs that rose like the core of a continent from the plain.

Circled about with rows of the dead plant-monsters, there stood in the center of the summit the strange ruins that Roverton and Deming had descried from below. They gleamed in the light with a dull luster, and appeared to be made of some foreign stone that was heavily impregnated with metal. They were apparently the remnants of several immense buildings, and bore the marks of some awful cataclysm that had carried away their superstructures and even much of the floor-work and foundations. One of the walls retained a doorway that was oddly high and narrow and was wider at top than at bottom. Also, there were some queer windows close to the ground. The men wondered at the physical characteristics of the race that had reared such edifices. From a human standpoint everything about the ruins was architecturally abnormal.

===Chinese===
罗弗顿怀着厌恶，折断了其中一条下垂触手的末端。它很容易便"啪"地一声断了；他发觉自己能用手指把它捻成细粉。明白这里没什么可惧的了，他和戴明便朝那一片奇异的废墟爬上坡去。

那山的土，是一种灰紫色的泥灰岩，踩在脚下十分坚实。他们登上山顶时，那轮将沉的太阳，正开始隐到一线远崖之后——那一线远崖，如同一片大陆的脊梁，从平原上升起。

在被一圈圈死去的草木怪物所环绕之中，山顶正中立着罗弗顿和戴明从下面望见的那一片奇异废墟。它们在光线里泛着暗淡的光泽，似乎是用某种饱含金属成分的异石所造。它们显然是几座巨大建筑的残骸，带着某场可怖浩劫的痕迹——那场浩劫卷走了它们的上部结构，甚至连许多地面与地基也被带走。其中一堵墙还留着一道门，门高而窄，殊为古怪，上宽而下窄。此外，紧贴地面处还有几个怪模怪样的窗。几人对营建这等建筑的种族的体貌特征，啧啧称奇。从人的角度来看，这废墟的一切，在建筑上都反常得很。

===Original===
Roverton approached one of the monoliths. It was square in shape, was forty feet high by seven in diameter, and had manifestly been taller at one time, for the top was riven and jagged where it had been broken off abruptly. It was wrought of the same material as the walls. A series of bas-reliefs, intermingled with columns of hieroglyphic letter-forms, had been carved about the base. The bas-reliefs depicted beings of a curious type, with long thin trunks terminating at each end in a multitude of many-jointed limbs. The heads of these creatures, or what appeared to be such, were at the nether extremity of the trunks, and had two mouths that were set above a double row of eyes. Ear-like appendages drooped from the chins. The lower limbs ended in birdlike claws and the upper in broad, umbrella-shaped webs whose use was beyond conjecture. Roverton exclaimed with amazement as he called Deming's attention to these figures. Whether such beings represented an extinct race, or whether their prototypes were still to be found in this outré world, was of course an irresolvable problem. But the men felt that this mystery too would soon be solved. It would be solved whether they liked it or not.

The men were too worn out with their herculean ordeals to devote much time and energy to speculation of this order. They found a sheltered place in the angle of one of the walls, and sat down. Perforce they had eaten nothing since the food provided by the pygmies at early dawn; and seemingly there was no immediate prospect of finding any. They were desperate and there seemed no hope to lighten the depression that closed in about these doomed men.

===Chinese===
罗弗顿走到其中一根独石柱前。它呈方形，高四十英尺，直径七英尺，先前显然更高，因为顶端在骤然折断之处，断裂参差、犬牙交错。它是用与那些墙垣相同的材料所造。柱身上绕着一系列浮雕，其间夹杂着一栏栏象形文字的字形。浮雕所刻的，是一种形貌古怪的生物，生着又长又细的躯干，两端各以许多多节的肢体作结。这些生物的头——或貌似头之物——长在躯干的下端，生着两张嘴，位于一双成排的眼睛之上。耳状的附肢从下巴垂下。下肢末端是鸟爪，上肢末端则是宽宽的、伞形的蹼膜，其用途无从揣测。罗弗顿招呼戴明注意这些图形，惊叹不已。这些生物究竟代表一个业已灭绝的种族，还是它们原型仍可在这一古怪的世界里寻得，自是一个无解的问题。可几人觉得，这一谜团也很快就会解开。它会被解开——不管他们乐不乐意。

几人被那些九死一生的磨难折腾得太疲乏了，已无多少时间与精力来作这等推测。他们在一堵墙的拐角处寻得一个避风之处，坐了下来。自黎明时分侏儒所给的食物之后，他们便没吃过东西；而眼前，似乎也没有立即找到食物的希望。他们绝望了，那笼罩着这几个在劫难逃之人的沮丧，仿佛再也无望驱散。

## VII / VII

Volmar Again! / 沃尔马重现！

===Original===
The sun had gone down, leaving an erubescent twilight that stained the soil, the ruins and the dead trees as with a deepening tide of blood. A preternatural silence prevailed—a silence fraught with the sense of foreign mystery, the burden of ultramundane antiquity that clung to those strange ruins. The men lay down and began to doze.

They awoke simultaneously, without knowing for a brief moment what it was that had aroused them. The twilight had turned to a rich violet, though the walls and trees were still clearly distinguishable. Somewhere in this twilight, there was a shrill, strident humming that grew louder momentarily.

All at once the humming was near at hand, in midair. It soared to a deafening clamor. Roverton and Deming saw that a swarm of giant insects with curving five-inch bills were hovering about them as if uncertain whether or not to attack. There seemed to be hundreds of these formidable-looking creatures. One of them, bolder than the others, darted forward and stung Deming on the back of his left hand till its beak almost protruded from his palm. He cried out with the pain, and struck the insect with his other fist. It squashed beneath the blow and fell to the ground, emitting a nauseous stench.

Roverton sprang to his feet and broke off a bough from one of the trees. This he waved at the swarm, which retreated a little but did not disperse. An idea came to him, and he thrust the bough into Deming's hand, saying:

"If you can keep them off, I'll try to build a fire."

While Deming waved his ineffectual weapon at the hesitating army Roverton broke off more of the dead, tentacle-like boughs, piled them, and crushed others into a heap of fine dust with his heel. Then, in the twilight, he found two small fragments of the metallic stone from which the buildings had been wrought; and striking the fragments together, he obtained a spark which fell into the dust-pile and ignited it. The stuff was highly combustible, for in less than a minute the heap of boughs was burning brightly. Terrified by the blaze, the insects fell back; and their stridulation soon diminished and sank away in the distance.

===Chinese===
太阳已经落了下去，留下一抹赤红的暮光，把土壤、废墟和枯死的树木都染了，如同一片愈染愈深的血潮。一片超乎寻常的寂静弥漫着——那寂静里满是一种异域的神秘之感，满是一种超乎尘世的古老，那古老附着在这一片奇异的废墟之上。几人躺下，开始打起盹来。

他们同时醒了过来，一时间竟不知是什么把他们惊醒的。暮光已转为浓郁的紫罗兰色，可墙垣和树木仍清晰可辨。在这片暮光里某处，有一阵尖厉刺耳的嗡嗡声，一刻比一刻响。

蓦地，那嗡嗡声已到了近前，半空之中。它升作一阵震耳欲聋的喧嚣。罗弗顿和戴明看见一大群生着五英寸长弯喙的巨虫，正围着他们盘旋，似乎在踌躇要不要进攻。这些面目可憎的家伙，看来足有数百只之多。其中一只比别的胆大，一个俯冲，在戴明左手背上蜇了一下，那喙几乎从他掌心透了出来。他痛得叫出声，用另一只拳头朝那虫子一击。那虫子在那一击下被拍烂，掉到地上，散发出一股恶臭。

罗弗顿一跃而起，从一棵树上折下一根树枝。他把树枝朝那虫群挥舞，虫群退开了一点，却并未散去。他心生一计，把树枝塞到戴明手里，说道：

"你要是能挡住它们，我就设法生一堆火。"

戴明挥着那根无济于事的武器，对着那支踟蹰的大军；罗弗顿则又折下更多枯死的、触手似的枝条，堆成一堆，又用脚跟把另一些碾成一堆细粉。随后，在暮光里，他寻得两小块金属质的石片——那些建筑便是用这种石头所造；他把两片石片一击，便得了一点火星，落进粉堆，把它引燃了。那东西极易燃，不出一分钟，那堆枝条便熊熊烧了起来。虫群被火光吓退；它们的嗡鸣很快便小了下去，消失在远方。

===Original===
Deming's hand was now painfully swollen and throbbing from the sting he had received.

"Those brutes would have finished us if they had been nervy enough to attack in force," he observed.

Roverton piled more fuel on the fire, in case the swarm should return.

"What a world!" he ejaculated. "I wish Volmar were here, confound him!"

As he spoke, there was a far-off droning in the crepuscular sky. For a moment, the men thought that the insect swarm was coming back to assail them again. Then the droning deepened to a great roar. The roar was somehow familiar, though neither could determine at first the memory which it tended to evoke. Then, where stars were beginning to pierce the vague heavens, they saw the indistinct bulk that descended toward them.

"My God! Is that the space-flier?" cried Deming.

===Chinese===
戴明挨蜇的那只手，此刻又肿又痛，突突地跳。

"那些畜生要是胆子够大、一齐扑上来，咱们早完了，"他说。

罗弗顿往火里又添了些柴，以防那虫群卷土重来。

"这是什么鬼世道！"他脱口叹道，"沃尔马要是在这儿就好了，可恶！"

他正说着，暮色苍茫的天空中传来一阵遥远的嗡鸣。一时间，几人还以为那虫群又回来袭击他们了。随即那嗡鸣加深，化作一片巨大的轰响。那轰响竟有些耳熟，可两人一时间都辨不清它唤起的是什么记忆。随后，在群星开始穿透苍茫天穹之处，他们看见一个模糊的庞然身躯朝他们降落下来。

"老天！那是飞船吗？"戴明喊道。

===Original===
With a final roaring and screeching of its propellers, the bulk came to rest within fifty feet of the fire. The light flickered on its metal sides and revealed the well-known ladder down which the three mutineers had climbed in an alien darkness.

A figure descended the ladder and came toward the fire. It was Captain Volmar. His face was drawn and livid in the firelight, and looked older than the two men remembered it. He greeted them stiffly, with an odd trace of embarrassment in his manner.

"I'm certainly glad to have located you," he announced, without waiting for Roverton or Deming to return too his salutation. "I've been flying around this damn planet all day, hoping there was one chance in a trillion of finding you again. I didn't take any bearings when I put you off in the night, so of course I had no idea where to look. I was about to give it up, when I saw the fire and decided to investigate."

===Chinese===
随着螺旋桨最后一声轰鸣与尖叫，那庞然大物在离火堆不到五十英尺之处停了下来。火光在它金属的两侧闪动，照出了那道三人熟悉的梯子——他们当初便是在一片异界的黑暗里，顺着它爬下来的。

一个人影下了梯子，朝火堆走来。那是沃尔马船长。他在火光中面容憔悴、青惨，看上去比两人记忆中更为苍老。他生硬地同他们打了招呼，举止间带着一丝古怪的尴尬。

"能找到你们，我确实很高兴，"他不等罗弗顿或戴明回礼便说道，"我在这该死的行星上飞了一整天，只盼着能有亿万分之一的机会再找到你们。我夜里把你们扔下去时没记下方位，自然也就不知该往哪儿找。我正打算放弃，却看见了火光，便决定过来看看。"

===Original===
"If you'll come back with me," he continued, "we'll let bygones be bygones. I'm short-handed now, and am going to give up the trip and start back for the solar system. We began to develop engine-trouble not long after we put you off; and two of the men were electrocuted by a short-circuit before the trouble was remedied. Their bodies are floating somewhere in mid-ether now—I gave them a space-burial. Then Jasper fell ill, and I've been running the flier single-handed for the past twenty-four hours. I'm sorry I was so hasty with you—I certainly put you off on an impossible sort of world. I've been all over it today, and there's nothing anywhere but seas, deserts, marshes, mud-flats, jungles of crazy-looking vegetation, a lot of equally desolate ruins, and no life except overgrown insects, reptiles, and a few cliff-dwelling pygmies in the sub-polar regions. It's a wonder that even two of you have managed to survive. Come on—you can tell me your story when we're aboard the flier."

Roverton and Deming followed him as he turned and re-ascended the ladder. The manhole closed behind them with a clang that was more grateful to their ears than music. A minute more, and the flier was climbing the heavens along the crepuscular curve of the planet, till it soared into the daylight of Delta Andromedae. Then it rushed on through the sidereal gulfs, till the great sun became a star and began to resume its wonted place in an ever-receding constellation.

===Chinese===
"要是你们肯跟我回去，"他接着说，"过去的事就既往不咎了。我眼下人手短缺，打算放弃这次远航，掉头返回太阳系。把你们扔下去之后不久，我们的引擎就出了毛病；毛病修好之前，有两个船员被短路电死了。他们的尸体此刻正漂浮在以太之中某处——我给他们行了太空葬。后来贾斯珀又病倒了，过去二十四小时一直是我一个人操持着飞船。对不起，我对你们太急躁了——我把你们扔到的，确是个没法待的世界。我今天把它跑了个遍，到处只有海洋、沙漠、沼泽、泥滩、一片片形如疯癫的草木丛林，还有许多同样荒凉的废墟，除了些过分硕大的虫豸、爬虫，以及亚极地一带少数穴居崖洞的侏儒之外，再无生命。你们两个竟能活下来，已是奇迹。来吧——上了飞船，你们再把你们的故事讲给我听。"

沃尔马转身重新攀上梯子，罗弗顿和戴明跟在他身后。舱口在他们身后"哐"地一声合上，那声响在他们耳中，比任何乐声都更悦耳。又过了一分钟，飞船便沿着这行星暮色苍茫的弧线，攀上苍穹，直冲进仙女座δ的日光之中。随后它一头扎进星辰的深渊，向前飞驰，直到那轮巨日化作一颗星，在一片不断退远的星座之中，重新归到它素来的位置。

## Sadastor / 萨达斯托

===Original===
Listen, for this is the tale that was told to a fair lamia by the demon Charnadis as they sat together on the top of Mophi, above the sources of the Nile, in those years when the sphinx was young. Now the lamia was vexed, for her beauty was grown an evil legend in both Thebais and Elephantine; so that men were become fearful of her lips and cautious of her embrace, and she had no lover for almost a fortnight. She lashed her serpentine tail on the ground, and moaned softly, and wept those mythical tears which a serpent weeps. And the demon told this tale for her comforting:

===Chinese===
且听我言——以下这则故事，乃恶魔沙尔纳迪斯（Charnadis）对一位姣美的拉弥亚（lamia）所讲述。其时斯芬克斯尚在年幼，二人同坐于莫菲（Mophi）山巅、尼罗河诸源之上。那拉弥亚正满腹郁恼，因她的美貌早已在底比斯地区（Thebais）与厄勒弗廷（Elephantine）两境俱成了不祥的传说；故而世人对她的朱唇心存畏惧，对她的拥抱满怀戒备，她已近半月不曾有一个情人。她以蛇形之尾在地上抽打，幽幽哀鸣，落下那只有蛇才会落下的、属乎神话的泪。恶魔便讲了下面这则故事，以作宽慰：

===Original===
Long, long ago, in the red cycles of my youth (said Charnadis), I was like all young demons, and was prone to use the agility of my wings in fantastic flights; to hover and poise like a gier-eagle above Tartarus and the pits of Python; or to lift the broad blackness of my vans on the orbit of stars. I have followed the moon from evening twilight to morning twilight; and I have gazed on the secrets of that Medusean face which she averts eternally from the earth. I have read through filming ice the ithyphallic runes on columns yet extant in her deserts; and I know the hieroglyphs which solve forgotten riddles, or hint eonian histories, on the walls of her cities taken by ineluctable snow. I have flown through the triple ring of Saturn, and have mated with lovely basilisks, on isles towering league-high from stupendous oceans where each wave is like the rise and fall of Himalayas. I have dared the clouds of Jupiter, and the black and freezing abysses of Neptune, which are crowned with eternal starlight; and I have sailed beyond to incommensurable suns, compared with which the sun that thou knowest is a corpse-candle in a stinted vault. There, in tremendous planets, I have furled my flight on the terraced mountains, large as fallen asteroids, where, with a thousand names and a thousand images, undreamt-of Evil is served and worshipt in unsurmisable ways. Or, perched in the flesh-colored lips of columnar blossoms, whose perfume was an ecstasy of incommunicable dreams, I have mocked the wiving monsters, and have lured their females, that sang and fawned at the base of my hiding-place.

===Chinese===
很久很久以前，在我那血色的青春轮回之中（沙尔纳迪斯说道），我正如一切年轻的恶魔，惯于借双翼之矫捷，作种种荒诞奇诡的飞翔：时而如兀鹫一般，盘旋于塔耳塔洛斯（Tartarus）与皮同（Python）诸巨渊之上；时而将我双翼那一片广袤的漆黑，托举于群星的轨道之间。我曾追随月亮，自黄昏的暮霭直追到清晨的曙光；我曾窥见她那美杜莎式容颜上的秘密——那张面容，她永远避开人间，从不肯转向大地。我透过蒙着翳膜的寒冰，读出她荒漠中尚存的石柱上那些阳具图腾的符文；我识得那些象形文字——在她被无可抵御的大雪所攻陷的城池的墙垣之上，或能解破已被遗忘的谜语，或可暗示亘古绵长的史事。我曾穿过萨图恩的三重光环，曾与妩媚的蛇怪（basilisk）相好于诸岛之上——那些岛屿自浩瀚无垠的汪洋中拔起，高逾数里格，海上每一重浪潮的起落，皆如喜马拉雅山的隆升与崩塌。我曾犯朱庇特的云海，曾入尼普顿那些幽黑冰寒、唯以永恒星光为冠冕的深渊；我更驶向更远之处，直抵那些不可度量的太阳——与之相较，你所熟知的那轮日头，不过是燃于逼仄墓穴中的一支尸烛。在那些骇人的星球之上，我曾在层叠如阶、大若坠落陨星的群山之间收拢飞翔——在那里，世所未梦的恶，以千种名号、千般形象，在无可揣度的方式里被人供奉、受人膜拜。又或我栖于柱状巨花那肉色的花唇之间，其芬芳乃是一种无可传达的迷梦之极欢，我便在其上嘲弄那些寻觅配偶的妖物，诱引它们的雌类，使之在我藏身之处的根脚下吟唱、献媚。

===Original===
Now, in my indefatigable questing among the remoter galaxies, I came one day to that forgotten and dying planet which in the language of its unrecorded peoples was called Sadastor. Immense and drear and gray beneath a waning sun, far-fissured with enormous chasms, and covered from pole to pole with the never-ebbing tides of the desert sand, it hung in space without moon or satellite, an abomination and a token of doom to fairer and younger worlds. Checking the speed of my interstellar flight, I followed its equator with a poised and level wing, above the peaks of cyclopean volcanoes, and bare, terrific ridges of elder hills, and deserts pale with the ghastliness of salt, that were manifestly the beds of former oceans.

===Chinese===
后来，在我那不知疲倦的、对远方星系的追寻之中，有一日我来到了那颗被遗忘的、垂死的行星——在它那未曾被载入史册的族群的语言里，它被唤作萨达斯托（Sadastor）。在一轮正趋熄灭的太阳之下，它广袤、阴沉、灰败，遍布深不可测的巨大裂罅，自一极至另一极，皆被永不退潮的流沙之潮所覆盖；它悬于太虚之中，无月、无卫星，对那些更为姣美、更为年幼的世界而言，它是一桩可憎之物，一个覆亡的凶兆。我敛起星际飞驰之速，以平稳端凝的双翼沿着它的赤道飞行，飞越独眼巨人般的火山之巅，飞越那些古老山岭光秃而可怖的山脊，飞越那些因盐分的惨白而死气沉沉的荒漠——那分明是往昔汪洋的海床。

===Original===
In the very center of one of these ocean-beds, beyond sight of the mountains that formed its primeval shoreline, and leagues below their level, I found a vast and winding valley that plunged even deeplier into the abysses of this dreadful world. It was walled with perpendicular cliffs and buttresses and pinnacles of a rusty-red stone, that were fretted into a million bizarrely sinister forms by the sinking of the olden seas. I flew slowly among these cliffs as they wound ever downward in tortuous spirals for mile on mile of utter and irredeemable desolation, and the light grew dimmer above me as ledge on ledge and battlement on battlement of that strange red stone upreared themselves between my wings and the heavens. Here, when I rounded a sudden turn of the precipice, in the profoundest depth where the rays of the sun fell only for a brief while at noon, and the rocks were purple with everlasting shadow, I found a pool of dark-green water﻿—the last remnant of the former ocean, ebbing still amid steep, insuperable walls. And from this pool there cried a voice, in accents that were subtly sweet as mortal wine of the mandragora, and faint as the murmuring of shells. And the voice said:

===Chinese===
在其中一处海床的正中央，已望不见当初构成它太古海岸的群山，比那些山岭的水准低下去不知多少里格，我发现了一道广阔而蜿蜒的峡谷，它更深地没入这可怖世界的诸渊之中。它以锈红色的岩石为壁——危崖、扶壁与尖峰林立，皆被古海的退却侵蚀成百万种诡谲可怖的形状。我在这些危崖之间缓缓飞行，只见它们以曲折的螺旋盘旋而下，一英里接着一英里，尽是彻头彻尾、无可救赎的荒凉；头顶的光线愈发昏暗，因那一层又一层、一垛又一垛奇异红岩的岩脊与雉堞，在我双翼与苍穹之间层层拔起。就在这里，当我绕过悬崖一处急转的弯角，在最幽深的渊底——那里阳光仅在正午短暂地落下一瞬，岩石因永恒的阴影而呈紫黑——我发现了一汪墨绿色的水潭：那是往昔汪洋仅存的残液，仍在陡峻、不可逾越的崖壁之间缓缓退落。自这汪水潭之中，传出一缕呼喊，其声如曼陀罗（mandragora）所酿的人间之酒一般幽微甘美，又如贝壳的絮语一般若有若无。那声音说道：

===Original===
"Pause and remain, I pray, and tell me who thou art, who comest thus to the accursed solitude wherein I die."

Then, pausing on the brink of the pool, I peered into its gulf of shadow, and saw the pallid glimmering of a female form that upreared itself from the waters. And the form was that of a siren, with hair the color of ocean-kelp, and berylline eyes, and a dolphin-shapen tail. And I said to her:

"I am the demon Charnadis. But who art thou, who lingerest thus in this ultimate pit of abomination, in the depth of a dying world?"

===Chinese===
“请驻足留下吧，我求你，告诉我你是谁——竟这样来到这受诅咒的孤寂之中，而我，正于此间赴死。”

于是我在潭边驻足，向它那阴影的深渊中凝望，看见一个女子身影的惨白微光，自水中缓缓立起。那身影，乃是一位塞壬（siren）：她的头发是海藻的颜色，双眸如绿玉，生着一条海豚形状的尾。我对她说道：

“我是恶魔沙尔纳迪斯。可你又是谁，竟这样流连于这终极的可憎之渊，在这垂死世界的深处？”

===Original===
She answered: "I am a siren, and my name is Lyspial. Of the seas wherein I saw and sported at leisure many centuries ago, and whose gallant mariners I drew to to an enchanted death on the shores of my disastrous isle, there remains only this fallen pool. Alas! For the pool dwindles daily, and when it is wholly gone I too must perish."

She began to weep, and her briny tears fell down and were added to the briny waters.

===Chinese===
她答道：“我是塞壬，我的名字叫利斯皮尔（Lyspial）。昔日我曾悠然徜徉其间的那些海洋——那些我在数百年前所见、所嬉的海洋，那些被我诱引至我那不祥岛屿的海岸、以蛊惑之术赐以迷死的高贵舟子——如今只剩这一汪残落的水潭。唉！这潭水日渐枯竭，待它彻底干涸之日，我也必随之而亡。”

她开始啜泣，她那咸涩的泪珠纷纷落下，融入那咸涩的潭水之中。

===Original===
Fain would I have comforted her, and I said:

"Weep not, for I will lift thee upon my wings and bear thee to some newer world, where the sky-blue waters of abounding seas are shattered to intricate webs of wannest foam, on low shores that are green and aureate with pristine spring. There, perchance for eons, thou shalt have thine abode, and galleys with painted oars and great barges purpureal-sailed shall be drawn upon thy rocks in the red light of sunsets domed with storm, and shall mingle the crash of their figured prows with the sweet sorcery of thy mortal singing."

===Chinese===
我本欲慰藉她，便说道：

“莫哭，因我将把你托上我的双翼，带你飞往某一个更为年幼的世界。在那里，浩瀚汪洋那天蓝色的水面，在低低的、碧绿而闪烁着初春金黄之色的海岸上，碎裂成最苍白的浪沫所织就的繁复之网。在那里，或可历经万古，你将得一处栖身之所；彩桨的大划船、张着紫红风帆的庞大驳船，将在风暴穹顶笼罩下那红彤彤的落日余光里，被牵引上你的礁石，它们那雕花船首的撞击之声，将与你那致命歌声中甘美的巫术交织在一起。”

===Original===
But still she wept, and would not be comforted, crying:

"Thou art kind, but this would avail me not, for I was born of the waters of this world, and with its waters I must die. Alas! my lovely seas, that ran in unbroken sapphire from shores of perennial blossoms to shores of everlasting snow! Alas! the sea-winds, with their mingled perfumes of brine and weed, and scents of ocean flowers and flowers of the land, and far-blown exotic balsams! Alas! the quinquiremes of cycle-ended wars, and the heavy-laden argosies with sails and cordage of byssus, that plied between barbaric isles with their cargoes of topaz or garnet-coloured wines and jade and ivory idols, in the antique summers that now are less than legend! Alas! the dead captains, the beautiful dead sailors that were borne by the ebbing tide to my couches of amber seaweed, in my caverns underneath a cedared promontory! Alas! the kisses that I laid on their cold and hueless lips, on their sealed marmorean eyelids!"

===Chinese===
可她仍旧哭泣，不肯受慰，哭喊道：

“你心肠是好的，可这于我无益，因我生于这世界的水，也必随它的水一同死去。唉！我那可爱的诸海，它们以一脉不辍的蓝宝石之色，自四季常花的海岸奔涌向万古不化之雪的海岸！唉！那海上的风，夹着咸水与海藻交织的芬芳，夹着洋中花卉与陆上花卉的气息，还有远方吹来的异域香脂！唉！那些纪元已尽的战事中的五列桨大船，那些满载的、张着海丝（byssus）风帆与缆索的大商船，它们在蛮荒的岛屿之间往返，载着一舱舱黄玉色或石榴红的葡萄酒、玉石与象牙的神像——在那如今已比一则传说更为渺远的古老盛夏里！唉！那些已死的船长，那些美丽的、死去的水手，他们被退潮带至我琥珀色海藻的卧榻之上，在我那长满雪松的岬角之下的洞穴里！唉！我曾印在他们冰冷而无色的双唇上的、印在他们紧闭的大理石般的眼睑上的吻！”

===Original===
And sorrow and pity seized me at her words, for I knew that she spoke the lamentable truth, that her doom was in the lessening of the bitter waters. So, after many proffered condolences, no less vague than vain, I bade her a melancholy farewell and flew heavily away between the spiral cliffs where I had come, and clomb the somber skies till the world Sadastor was only a darkling mote far down in space. But the tragic shadow of the siren's fate, and her sorrow, lay grievously upon me for hours, and only in the kisses of a beautiful fierce vampire, in a far-off and young and exuberant world, was I able to forget it. And I tell the now the tale thereof, that haply thou mayest be consoled by the contemplation of a plight that was infinitely more dolorous and irremediable than thine own."

===Chinese===
闻她此言，哀伤与怜悯攫住了我，因我知道她所说的，乃是可悲的实话——她的厄运，正系于那苦涩之水的日渐干涸。于是，在献上诸多慰问之后——那些慰问既朦胧又徒然——我凄然与她道别，沉重地飞离我当初来时所经的螺旋危崖之间，攀上那阴沉的天空，直至萨达斯托这颗世界，已不过是远远沉在太虚下方的一粒幽暗微尘。然而那塞壬命运的悲剧阴影，连同她的哀愁，沉重地压在我心头上达数个时辰；唯有在一个遥远、年轻而生机勃发的世界里，在一位美丽而凶悍的吸血女妖的亲吻之中，我才得以将它忘却。如今我把这故事讲给你听，愿你或可因思忖一段远比你自身更为可哀、更无可挽回的困境，而得到些许宽慰。”

## The Abominations of Yondo / 雍多的可憎之物

===Original===
The sand of the desert of Yondo is not as the sand of other deserts; for Yondo lies nearest of all to the world's rim; and strange winds, blowing from a pit no astronomer may hope to fathom, have sown its ruinous fields with the gray dust of corroding planets, the black ashes of extinguished suns. The dark, orb-like mountains which rise from its wrinkled and pitted plain are not all its own, for some are fallen asteroids half-buried in that abysmal sand. Things have crept in from nether space, whose incursion is forbid by the gods of all proper and well-ordered lands; but there are no such gods in Yondo, where live the hoary genii of stars abolished and decrepit demons left homeless by the destruction of antiquated hells.

===Chinese===
雍多（Yondo）荒漠的沙，不同于他处荒漠的沙；因为雍多在一切地域之中，距大地的边缘最近；自那连天文学家也无从测度的深渊里吹来的怪风，将朽蚀行星的灰败尘埃、熄灭恒星的黑色余烬，遍播于它那倾败的原野之上。自它那皱缩、坑洼的平原上耸起的暗色球状群山，并非尽是本土所生，其中有些，乃是半埋于那无底荒沙之中的坠落陨星。有种种异物自幽冥的太虚潜入此地，它们的入侵，为一切端正有序的疆土上的诸神所不容；然而雍多并无此等神祇，栖居其间的，乃是那些被废黜的星辰的苍老巨灵，以及因陈旧地狱之毁灭而无家可归的衰颓恶魔。

===Original===
It was noon of a vernal day when I came forth from that interminable cactus-forest in which the Inquisitors of Ong had left me, and saw at my feet the gray beginnings of Yondo. I repeat, it was noon of a vernal day; but in that fantastic wood I had found no token or memory of a spring; and the swollen, fulvous, dying and half-rotten growths through which I had pushed my way, were like no other cacti, but bore shapes of abomination scarcely to be described. The very air was heavy with stagnant odors of decay; and leprous lichens mottled the black soil and russet vegetation with increasing frequency. Pale-green vipers lifted their heads from prostrate cactus-boles and watched me with eyes of bright ochre that had no lids or pupils. These things had disquieted me for hours past; and I did not like the monstrous fungi, with hueless stems and nodding heads of poisonous mauve, which grew from the sodden lips of fetid tarns; and the sinister ripples spreading and fading on the yellow water at my approach were not reassuring to one whose nerves were still taut from unmentionable tortures. Then, when even the blotched and sickly cacti became more sparse and stunted, and rills of ashen sand crept in among them, I began to suspect how great was the hatred my heresy had aroused in the priests of Ong and to guess the ultimate malignancy of their vengeance.

===Chinese===
那是一个春日的正午，我从昂（Ong）的审判官们所弃置我的那片连绵无际的仙人掌森林中走出，望见雍多那灰败的起始，就在我脚下。我再说一遍，那是一个春日的正午；可是在那诡异的林莽之中，我寻不见任何春天的征兆或记忆；我一路推开那些肿胀、黄褐、垂死而半腐的草木向前行去，它们与一切别的仙人掌都判然不同，却生着几乎难以描摹的可憎形状。连空气都沉滞着腐臭的、停滞的气息；而那癞病般的苔藓，愈来愈频繁地将黑土与赭色的草木斑驳地点染。淡绿的小蝰蛇自倾倒的仙人掌躯干上抬起头来，以那既无眼睑亦无瞳孔的明亮赭石色眸子凝视着我。这些物事已使我惴惴不安了数个时辰；我更不喜那些畸怪的蕈类，它们生着无色的菌柄与低垂的毒紫色菌冠，自恶臭山潭那浸饱水汽的唇边长出；而当我走近时，那在黄水上扩散又消褪的阴森涟漪，对一个神经尚因不可言说的酷刑而紧绷如弦的人而言，也并无半分宽慰。后来，当连那些斑驳而病态的仙人掌也变得愈发稀疏、愈发矮小，一缕缕灰烬般的细沙悄悄潜入它们之间，我才开始揣度我的异端在那昂的祭司心中唤起了何等深切的仇恨，并揣测出他们报复之中那终极的恶毒。

===Original===
I will not detail the indiscretions which had led me, a careless stranger from far-off lands, into the power of those dreadful magicians and mysteriarchs who serve the lion-headed Ong. These indiscretions, and the particulars of my arrest, are painful to remember; and least of all do I like to remember the racks of dragon-gut strewn with powdered adamant, on which men are stretched naked; or that unlit room with six-inch windows near the sill, where bloated corpse worms crawled in by hundreds from a neighboring catacomb. Sufficient to say that, after expending the resources of their frightful fantasy, my inquisitors had borne me blindfolded on camel-back for incomputable hours, to leave me at morning twilight in that sinister forest. I was free, they told me, to go whither I would; and in token of the clemency of Ong, they gave me a loaf of coarse bread and a leathern bottle of rank water by way of provision. It was at noon of the same day that I came to the desert of Yondo.

===Chinese===
我无意详述那些轻率的行径——正是它们使我这来自远方、不慎疏虞的异乡人，落入那些侍奉狮首之神昂的骇人术士与秘仪教长（mysteriarchs）之手。这些行径，连同我被捕的种种详情，回想起来都痛楚难堪；而我最不愿忆起的，莫过于那些以龙筋编成、撒满金刚砂的刑架——人被赤身裸体地张开其上；或是那间六英寸小窗紧贴窗台的幽暗囚室，成百上千臃肿的尸虫自邻近的地下墓穴爬入其中。只消说，我的审判官们在穷尽了他们那可怖想象的一切手段之后，将我蒙上双眼、缚于驼背，驮行了不可胜计的时辰，于晨曦微明之时将我弃于那片不祥的森林之中。他们对我说，我自由了，可往任何想去之处；作为昂之宽宥的凭证，他们给了我一块粗面包、一只盛着腥臭脏水的皮瓶，权作路资。正是同一天的正午，我来到了雍多的荒漠。

===Original===
So far, I had not thought of turning back, for all the horror of those rotting cacti, or the evil things that dwelt among them. Now, I paused knowing the abominable legend of the land to which I had come; for Yondo is a place where few have ventured wittingly and of their own accord. Fewer still have returned—babbling of unknown horrors and strange treasure; and the lifelong palsy which shakes their withered limbs, together with the mad gleam in their starting eyes beneath whitened brows and lashes, is not an incentive for others to follow. So it was that I hesitated on the verge of those ashen sands, and felt the tremor of a new fear in my wrenched vitals. It was dreadful to go on, and dreadful to go back, for I felt sure that the priests had made provision against the latter contingency. So after a little I went forward, singing at each step in loathly softness, and followed by certain long-legged insects that I had met among the cacti. These insects were the color of a week-old corpse and were as large as tarantulas; but when I turned and trod upon the foremost, a mephitic stench arose that was more nauseous even than their color. So, for the nonce, I ignored them as much as possible.

===Chinese===
在此之前，尽管那些腐烂的仙人掌何等可怖，其间栖居的恶物何等可憎，我都不曾起过折返之念。而如今我却踌躇了，因为我知道自己所至之地有着怎样可憎的传说；雍多这地方，少有人明知而甘愿涉足。归来者更是寥寥——他们语无伦次地念叨着未知的恐怖与奇异的珍宝；而那终生不愈、震颤他们枯萎四肢的瘫疾，以及斑白眉睫之下那双暴凸眼眸中狂乱的光芒，绝非引诱他人效尤的榜样。于是我在那片灰烬之沙的边缘踌躇，在我那被折磨得衰败的脏腑中，感到一种新的恐惧的颤栗。向前是可怖，折返亦是可怖，因为我深知，祭司们对后一种情形必已预作防备。过了一会儿，我向前走去，每一步都发出令人作呕的、轻柔的鸣响，几只我在仙人掌间遇见的长腿昆虫尾随其后。这些昆虫的颜色如同死去一周的尸首，体型则与狼蛛相仿；可当我转身一脚踩住最前面那只时，一股恶臭腾起，比它们的颜色更令人作呕。于是，暂且之间，我只得尽可能不去理会它们。

===Original===
Indeed, such things were minor horrors in my predicament. Before me, under a huge sun of sickly scarlet, Yondo reached interminable as the land of a hashish-dream against the black heavens. Far-off, on the utmost rim, were those orb-like mountains of which I have told; but in between were awful blanks of gray desolation, and low, treeless hills like the backs of half-buried monsters. Struggling on, I saw great pits where meteors had sunk from sight; and divers-colored jewels that I could not name glared or glistened from the dust. There were fallen cypresses that rotted by crumbling mausoleums, on whose lichen blotted marble fat chameleons crept with royal pearls in their mouths. Hidden by the low ridges, were cities of which no stela remained unbroken—immense and immemorial cities lapsing shard by shard, atom by atom, to feed infinities of desolation. I dragged my torture-weakened limbs over vast rubbish-heaps that had once been mighty temples; and fallen gods frowned in rotting psammite or leered in riven porphyry at my feet. Over all was an evil silence, broken only by the satanic laughter of hyenas, and the rustling of adders in thickets of dead thorn or antique gardens given to the perishing nettle and fumitory.

===Chinese===
诚然，这些于我的困境而言，不过是些微末的可怖。在我面前，一轮病态猩红的巨日之下，雍多向着黑色的苍穹无尽地延展，宛如一场哈希什迷梦（hashish-dream）中的疆域。极远之处，在最外的边缘，便是我所说过的那些球状群山；可两者之间，却是一处处骇人的、灰败荒芜的空白，以及低矮、无树的丘陵，形似半埋于地的怪物的脊背。我艰难前行，望见一个个巨坑，那是陨星沉没消失之处；我无以名之的各色宝石，在尘埃中灼灼生光或熠熠闪烁。有倾倒的柏树在倾圮的陵墓旁腐烂，那生满苔藓斑迹的大理石上，肥硕的变色龙口衔皇室珍珠缓缓爬行。隐于低矮山脊之后的，是一座座无一碑完整的城池——广袤而无从追忆的古城，正一碎片一碎片、一微粒一微粒地崩颓，去喂养那无尽的荒凉。我拖曳着因酷刑而衰弱的肢体，攀过一堆堆曾为雄伟神庙的浩瀚瓦砾；陨落的诸神，或于腐朽的砂质岩（psammite）中蹙眉，或于我脚下破裂的斑岩（porphyry）中狞笑。笼罩一切的，是一片邪祟的寂静，唯有鬣狗那魔鬼般的狞笑，以及蝰蛇在枯荆棘丛中、在任由将死的荨麻与烟堇（fumitory）侵占的古旧园林里的窸窣爬行，偶尔将它打破。

===Original===
Topping one of the many mound-like ridges, I saw the waters of a weird lake, unfathomably dark and green as malachite, and set with bars of profulgent salt. These waters lay far beneath me in a cup-like hollow; but almost at my feet on the wave-worn slopes were heaps of that ancient salt; and I knew that the lake was only the bitter and ebbing dregs of some former sea. Climbing down, I came to the dark waters, and began to lave my hands; but there was a sharp and corrosive sting in that immemorial brine, and I desisted quickly, preferring the desert dust that had wrapped me about like a slow shroud.

===Chinese===
翻过那许许多多坟冢般的山脊之一，我望见了一片怪湖的水面——它幽深难测、暗而绿如孔雀石，其间横亘着一道道辉耀的盐带。那片水域远远地卧在我脚下的一个杯状洼地之中；可几乎就在我脚边、那些被波浪侵蚀的斜坡上，堆着一簇簇那古老的盐；我便明白，这湖不过是某片往昔之海那苦涩而退落的残液。我攀援而下，来到那暗水之畔，开始濯洗双手；可那太古的盐水里有一种尖锐而腐蚀的蜇刺，我连忙缩手，宁可任由那早已如一袭缓缓裹尸的殓衣般裹缠我的荒漠尘埃。

===Original===
Here I decided to rest for a little; and hunger forced me to consume part of the meager and mocking fare with which I had been provided by the priests. It was my intention to push on if my strength would allow and reach the lands that lie to the north of Yondo. These lands are desolate, indeed, but their desolation is of a more usual order than that of Yondo; and certain tribes of nomads have been known to visit them occasionally. If fortune favored me, I might fall in with one of these tribes.

===Chinese===
我决意在此稍作歇息；饥饿迫使我吞下祭司们所给我的那份少得可怜、形同嘲弄的口粮之一部分。我本打算，若体力尚可，便继续北行，抵达雍多以北的那些疆土。那些疆土诚然也是荒凉的，可它们的荒凉，比之雍多的荒凉，尚属较为寻常的一类；而且据说有些游牧部族偶尔会涉足其地。若命运眷顾，我或可偶遇其中一族。

===Original===
The scant fare revived me, and, for the first time in weeks of which I had lost all reckoning, I heard the whisper of a faint hope. The corpse-colored insects had long since ceased to follow me; and so far despite the eeriness of the sepulchral silence and the mounded dust of timeless ruin, I had met nothing half so horrible as those insects. I began to think that the terrors of Yondo were somewhat exaggerated. It was then that I heard a diabolic chuckle on the hillside above me. The sound began with a sharp abruptness that startled me beyond all reason, and continued endlessly, never varying its single note, like the mirth of an idiotic demon. I turned, and saw the mouth of a dark cave fanged with green stalactites, which I had not perceived before. The sound appeared to come from within this cave.

===Chinese===
那点微薄的口粮使我稍稍苏复；在我已全然忘却其数目的数周以来，我头一次听见了一缕微弱希望的絮语。那尸色的昆虫早已不再尾随我；而至此，尽管四周是墓穴般寂静的阴森，以及无时间之朽败那坟冢般的尘埃，我所遇之物，竟无一及得上那些昆虫一半可怖。我开始以为，雍多的诸般恐怖未免有些言过其实。正在那时，我听见头顶的山坡上传来一声恶魔般的低笑。那声音以一种令我莫名惊骇的尖锐突兀而起，继而无穷无尽地延续，单调不变，犹如一个白痴恶魔的欢愉。我转身望去，看见一个暗洞的洞口，满口獠牙般地生满了绿色的钟乳石——这是我先前未曾察觉的。那声音，似乎便是从这洞穴之中传出。

===Original===
With a fearful intentness I stared at the black opening. The chuckle grew louder, but for awhile I could see nothing. At last I caught a whitish glimmer in the darkness; then, with all the rapidity of nightmare, a monstrous Thing emerged. It had a pale, hairless, egg-shaped body, large as that of a gravid she-goat; and this body was mounted on nine long wavering legs with many flanges, like the legs of some enormous spider. The creature ran past me to the water's edge; and I saw that there were no eyes in its oddly sloping face; but two knife-like ears rose high above its head, and a thin, wrinkled snout hung down across its mouth, whose flabby lips, parted in that eternal chuckle, revealed rows of bats' teeth. It drank acidly of the bitter lake then, with thirst satisfied, it turned and seemed to sense my presence, for the wrinkled snout rose and pointed toward me, sniffing audibly. Whether the creature would have fled, or whether it meant to attack me, I do not know; for I could bear the sight no longer but ran with trembling limbs amid the massive boulders and great bars of salt along the lakeshore.

===Chinese===
我怀着惊惧的专注，凝视着那黑色的洞口。低笑声愈发响亮，但好一阵我什么也看不见。终于，我在黑暗中捕捉到一缕惨白的微光；随即，以噩梦般的迅疾，一个畸怪之物涌了出来。它有一个苍白、无毛、蛋形的身躯，大如一头怀胎的母山羊；这身躯装在九条长而摇摆、生着许多翼片的腿上，恰如某种巨型蜘蛛的腿。那生物从我身旁跑过，直奔水边；我看见它那奇异地倾斜的脸上并没有眼睛；可两只刀刃般的耳朵高耸于其头顶之上，一条瘦皱的长吻垂过它的嘴，那松弛的唇永远咧在那不绝的低笑里，露出层层叠叠的蝙蝠般的牙齿。它酸涩地饮着那苦湖的水，随后，渴既得解，它转过身来，似是察觉了我的存在，因为那条皱缩的长吻抬了起来，直直指向我，发出清晰可闻的嗅探之声。这生物本欲逃遁，抑或意欲攻我，我不得而知；因为我再也无法忍受这景象，遂以颤抖的四肢，在湖滨那巨硕的卵石与一道道巨大的盐柱之间奔逃。

===Original===
Utterly breathless I stopped at last, and saw that I was not pursued, I sat down, still trembling, in the shadow of a boulder. But I was to find little respite, for now began the second of those bizarre adventures which forced me to believe all the mad legends I had heard.

===Chinese===
终于，我上气不接下气地停了下来，发觉自己并未被追及，便在一块巨石的阴影里坐下，兀自颤抖。可我注定难得喘息，因为此刻，那迫使我信服我所听闻的一切疯狂传说的第二桩诡诞奇遇，已然开端。

===Original===
More startling even than that diabolic chuckle was the scream that rose at my very elbow from the salt-compounded sand—the scream of a woman possessed by some atrocious agony, or helpless in the grip of devils. Turning, I beheld a veritable Venus, naked in a white perfection that could fear no scrutiny, but immersed to her navel in the sand. Her terror-widened eyes implored me and her lotus hands reached out—with beseeching gesture. I sprang to her side—and touched a marble statue, whose carven lids were drooped in some enigmatic dream of dead cycles, and whose hands were buried with the lost loveliness of hips and thighs. Again I fled, shaken with a new fear; and again I heard the scream of a woman's agony. But this time I did not turn to see the imploring eyes and hands.

===Chinese===
比那恶魔般的低笑更令人惊心的，是一声从我手肘边、那掺盐的沙中升起的尖叫——那是一个女人被某种穷凶极恶的苦痛所附、或无助地落入群魔之掌时的尖叫。我转过身，看见一位真正的维纳斯，赤裸着一身不怕任何审视的洁白完美，却自腰脐以下没入沙中。她那因恐惧而圆睁的双眸向我哀求，她那莲瓣般的双手伸出——作着恳求的姿态。我扑到她身侧——却触到了一座大理石雕像，它那雕出的双睑低垂着，沉入某种属于已死纪元的玄奥迷梦，它的双手，连同那已逝的臀与股的秀美，一齐被埋没了。我再次奔逃，因一种新的恐惧而战栗；我再次听见一个女人苦痛的尖叫。可这一次，我没有转身去看那哀求的双眼与双手。

===Original===
Up the long slope to the north of that accursed lake stumbling over boulders of basanite and ledges that were sharp with verdigris-covered metals; floundering in pits of salt, on terraces wrought by the receding tide in ancient aeons. I fled as a man flies from dream to baleful dream of some cacodemoniacal night. At whiles there was a cold whisper in my ear, which did not come from the wind of my flight; and looking back as I reached one of the upper terraces, I perceived a singular shadow that ran pace by pace with my own. This shadow was not the shadow of man nor ape nor any known beast; the head was too grotesquely elongated, the squat body too gibbous; and I was unable to determine whether the shadow possessed five legs, or whether what appeared to be the fifth was merely a tail.

===Chinese===
我攀上那受诅咒之湖以北的长坡，在玄武岩（basanite）的巨石上踉跄，在那些因覆满铜绿的金属而锋利如刃的岩架上跌撞；我陷落于盐坑之中，陷落于远古纪元里退去的潮汐所凿出的层阶之上。我如一个在某个邪魔之夜从一重噩梦逃向另一重噩梦的人那般奔逃。不时，我耳畔有一缕冰冷的低语，那并非来自我奔逃时所带起的风；当我抵达其中一道上层阶地回首张望时，我察觉到一个奇异的影子，与我亦步亦趋。这影子，既非人也非猿、更非任何已知野兽的影子；它的头颅诡怪地过分拉长，矮胖的身躯又过于隆凸；我甚至无从断定，这影子究竟生着五条腿，还是那看似第五条的，不过是一条尾巴。

===Original===
Terror lent me new strength, and I had reached the hilltop when I dared to look back again. But still the fantastic shadow kept pace by pace with mine; and now I caught a curious and utterly sickening odor, foul as the odor of bats who have hung in a charnel-house amid the mold of corruption. I ran for leagues, while the red sun slanted above the asteroidal mountains to the west; and the weird shadow lengthened with mine but kept always at the same distance behind me.

===Chinese===
恐惧赐予我新的力量，待我敢再次回首时，我已攀上了山顶。可那诡异的影子，依旧与我亦步亦趋；而此刻，我嗅到了一股古怪而令人作呕的气味，恶臭如悬垂于藏骨堂（charnel-house）之中、置身腐朽霉斑之间的蝙蝠的气味。我奔行了不知多少里格，那轮猩红的太阳斜悬于西方那些陨星般的群山之上；那诡异的影子随我一同拉长，却始终保持着与我同样的距离，缀在我身后。

===Original===
An hour before sunset I came to a circle of small pillars that rose miraculously unbroken amid ruins that were like a vast pile of potsherds. As I passed among these pillars I heard a whimper, like the whimper of some fierce animal, between rage and fear, and saw that the shadow had not followed me within the circle. I stopped and waited, conjecturing at once that I had found a sanctuary my unwelcome familiar would not dare to enter; and in this the action of the shadow confirmed me, the Thing hesitated, then ran about the circle of columns pausing often between them; and, whimpering all the while, at last went away and disappeared in the desert toward the setting sun.

===Chinese===
日落之前一个时辰，我来到一圈小石柱之前，它们竟奇迹般完好无损地矗立于一片如山的陶片堆般的废墟之中。当我穿行于这些石柱之间时，我听见一声呜咽，宛如某只猛兽介于暴怒与恐惧之间的呜咽，并看见那影子没有随我进入这圈石柱之内。我停下脚步等候，当即揣度自己寻得了一处我那不请自来的使魔所不敢擅入的圣所；而影子的举动也证实了我的揣度：那物事踟蹰了一下，随即绕着那圈石柱奔走，频频在柱间驻足；继而一路呜咽不止，终于离去，消失在朝着落日方向的荒漠之中。

===Original===
For a full half hour I did not dare to move; then, the imminence of night, with all its probabilities of fresh terror, urged me to push on as far as I could to the north. For I was now in the very heart of Yondo where demons or phantoms might dwell who would not respect the sanctuary of the unbroken columns. Now, as I toiled on, the sunlight altered strangely; for the red orb nearing the mounded horizon, sank and smouldered in a belt of miasmal haze, where floating dust from all the shattered fanes and necropoli of Yondo was mixed with evil vapors coiling skyward from black enormous gulfs lying beyond the utmost rim of the world. In that light, the entire waste, the rounded mountains, the serpentine hills, the lost cities, were drenched with phantasmal and darkening scarlet.

===Chinese===
足有半个时辰，我不敢稍动；随后，黑夜之将至，连同其一切滋生新恐怖的可能，催促我尽量向北再赶一程。因为我此刻已身处雍多的腹心，这里或栖居着些不敬这完整石柱之圣所的恶魔或幽魂。当我艰难前行之际，那日光诡异地变了颜色；因为那轮猩红的日头临近那坟冢般的地平线，沉入并闷燃于一道瘴气薄雾之中，在那里，雍多所有破碎神庙与大墓场所扬起的浮尘，与自那远远超越大地极缘之外的、幽黑浩瀚的深渊里盘旋升空的邪秽蒸气交织一处。在那光里，整片荒原、那些浑圆的群山、那些蜿蜒的丘陵、那些湮没的城池，都浸透了幽幻而渐沉的猩红。

===Original===
Then, out of the north, where shadows mustered, there came a curious figure—a tall man fully caparisoned in chain-mail—or, rather, what I assumed to be a man. As the figure approached me, clanking dismally at each step on the sharded ground, I saw that its armor was of brass mottled with verdigris; and a casque of the same metal furnished with coiling horns and a serrate comb, rose high above its head. I say its head, for the sunset was darkening, and I could not see clearly at any distance; but when the apparition came abreast, I perceived that there was no face beneath the brows of the bizarre helmet whose empty edges were outlined for a moment against the smouldering light. Then the figure passed on, still clanking dismally and vanished.

===Chinese===
随即，自群影聚集的北方，走来一个古怪的身影——一个高大的男子，全身披挂着锁子甲——或者，更确切地说，一个我以为是个男子的东西。当那身影向我逼近，每一步都踩在碎裂的地上、发出凄凉的铿锵声时，我看见他的甲胄是覆满铜绿斑迹的黄铜所制；一顶同质的头盔，配着盘旋的双角与锯齿状的冠饰，高耸于其头顶之上。我之所以说"他的"头，是因为日落正暗，远处我已看不分明了；可当那幽灵般的身影与我擦肩而过时，我察觉在那怪异头盔的眉檐之下，并无一张面孔，那头盔空洞的边缘，在闷燃的光里被勾勒出片刻的轮廓。随后那身影径直走过，依旧发出凄凉的铿锵，渐次消失。

===Original===
But on its heels ere the sunset faded, there came a second apparition, striding with incredible strides and halting when it loomed almost upon me in the red twilight—the monstrous mummy of some ancient king still crowned with untarnished gold but turning to my gaze a visage that more than time or the worm had wasted. Broken swathings flapped about the skeleton legs, and above the crown that was set with sapphires and orange rubies, a black something swayed and nodded horribly; but, for an instant, I did not dream what it was. Then, in its middle, two oblique and scarlet eyes opened and glowed like hellish coals, and two ophidian fangs glittered in an apelike mouth. A squat, furless, shapeless head on a neck of disproportionate extent leaned unspeakably down and whispered in the mummy's ear. Then, with one stride, the titanic lich took half the distance between us, and from out the folds of the tattered sere-cloth a gaunt arm arose, and fleshless, taloned fingers laden with glowering gems, reached out and fumbled for my throat…

===Chinese===
可在它身后、日落尚未褪尽之际，又来了第二个幽灵，迈着不可思议的大步，直逼至我在那猩红的暮色中几乎被它笼罩才停下——那是一具某位古王的畸怪木乃伊，头上的金冠依然灿烂未黯，可转向我的那张脸，却已被比时间或蛆虫更甚之物所消蚀。破碎的裹布在它那骷髅般的双腿旁翻飞，在镶满蓝宝石与橘红宝石的王冠之上，一团黑色的什么东西可怖地摇摆、点首；可有一刹那，我竟不敢去想那是什么。随即，在那黑色之物的中部，两只斜长的猩红眼睛睁开，如地狱的余烬般灼灼发光，两枚蛇形的毒牙在一张猿般的嘴里闪烁。一颗矮胖、无毛、不成形的头颅，装在一根不成比例的长颈之上，难以言喻地俯下身来，对着木乃伊的耳朵低语。随即，只一大步，那提坦般的尸鬼便跨过了我们之间一半的距离，从破烂殓布（sere-cloth）的褶皱里伸出一条枯瘦的手臂，那无肉而利爪如钩、缀满阴沉宝石的手指，伸了出来，摸索着寻向我的咽喉……

===Original===
Back, back through aeons of madness and dread, in a prone, precipitate flight I ran from those fumbling fingers that hung always on the dusk behind me, back, back forever, unthinking, unhesitating, to all the abominations I had left; back in the thickening twilight toward the nameless and sharded ruins, the haunted lake, the forest of evil cacti, and the cruel and cynical inquisitors of Ong who waited my return.

===Chinese===
退，退，穿过疯狂与恐惧的万古，以一种匍匐而仓皇的奔逃，我从那些始终悬垂在我身后暮色里摸索探寻的手指前逃离；退，退，永无休止地，不假思索，不顾一切地，退向我抛在身后的一切可憎之物；退向那渐浓暮色之中那些无名而碎裂的废墟，那座作祟的怪湖，那片邪恶的仙人掌森林，以及昂的那些冷酷而讥诮的审判官——他们，正等着我回去。

## The End of the Story / 故事的结局

===Original===
The following narrative was found among the papers of Christophe Morand, a young law-student of Tours, after his unaccountable disappearance during a visit at his father’s home near Moulins, in November, 1798:

===Chinese===
以下这篇记述，是在克里斯托夫·莫朗（Christophe Morand）的遗稿之中发现的。莫朗是图尔（Tours）一名年轻的法科学生；1798年11月，他在穆兰（Moulins）附近父亲家中省亲期间，不明不白地失踪：

===Original===
A sinister brownish-purple autumn twilight, made premature by the imminence of a sudden thunderstorm, had filled the forest of Averoigne. The trees along my road were already blurred to ebon masses, and the road itself, pale and spectral before me in the thickening gloom, seemed to waver and quiver slightly, as with the tremor of some mysterious earthquake. I spurred my horse, who was woefully tired with a journey begun at dawn, and had fallen hours ago to a protesting and reluctant trot, and we galloped adown the darkening road between enormous oaks that seemed to lean toward us with boughs like clutching fingers as we passed.

With dreadful rapidity, the night was upon us, the blackness became a tangible clinging veil; a nightmare confusion and desperation drove me to spur my mount again with a more cruel rigor; and now, as we went, the first far-off mutter of the storm mingled with the clatter of my horse’s hoofs, and the first lightning flashes illumed our way, which, to my amazement (since I believed myself on the main highway through Averoigne), had inexplicably narrowed to a well-trodden footpath. Feeling sure that I had gone astray, but not caring to retrace my steps in the teeth of darkness and the towering clouds of the tempest, I hurried on, hoping, as seemed reasonable, that a path so plainly worn would lead eventually to some house or château where I could find refuge for the night. My hope was wellfounded, for within a few minutes I descried a glimmering light through the forest-boughs, and came suddenly to an open glade, where, on a gentle eminence, a large building loomed, with several litten windows in the lower story, and a top that was well-nigh indistinguishable against the bulks of driven cloud.

===Chinese===
一场骤然将至的雷暴将暮色提前催落；那不祥的、褐紫色的秋日黄昏，已然弥漫了整座阿维罗涅（Averoigne）的森林。我沿途所经的树木，早已模糊作一团团漆黑的阴影；而道路本身，在愈来愈浓的幽暗之中于我前方显得苍白而鬼气森森，似在微微地摇曳、战栗，宛如受了某场神秘地震的惊扰。我策动坐骑——那牲口因自破晓便启程的跋涉而疲顿已极，数个时辰之前便已委顿下来，满腹怨尤地踽踽小跑——我们沿着那条渐次沉入黑暗的道路飞驰，两旁参天的橡树在我们掠过之际，仿佛以形如攫人五指的枝桠向我们倾身俯压。

黑夜以可怖的迅疾降临到我们头上，那漆黑化作了一重触手可及、紧黏着人的帷幕；一场梦魇般的慌乱与焦灼驱使我以更无情的力道再度策马；此时我们行进之间，风暴最初的、远远的闷响已与我坐骑蹄声的嘚嘚交织在一起，最初的几道闪电照亮了我们前路——令我惊诧的是（因我自以为正走在贯穿阿维罗涅的大道之上），那路竟莫名其妙地收窄成一条被人踩踏得踏实的脚径。我明知自己已经走岔，却不愿在漆黑与那直压而下的风暴乌云之中循原路折返，便继续赶路，心想（这似乎也合乎情理）：一条踩踏得如此明晰的小径，终归会通向某处宅邸或城堡，我可在那里觅得一夜的庇身之所。我这一线希望并非落空——不过数分钟，我便透过林间的枝叶瞥见一点闪烁的灯火，随即骤然来到一片空旷的林间草地上；在一处和缓的高丘之上，一座庞大的建筑影影绰绰地矗立着，下层有几扇透着灯光的窗户，而它的顶端，在那一团团疾驰的乌云的衬托之下，几乎已分辨不清。

===Original===
“Doubtless a monastery,” I thought, as I drew rein, and descending from my exhausted mount, lifted the heavy brazen knocker in the form of a dog’s head and let it fall on the oaken door. The sound was unexpectedly loud and sonorous, with a reverberation almost sepulchral, and I shivered involuntarily, with a sense of startlement, of unwonted dismay. This, a moment later, was wholly dissipated when the door was thrown open and a tall, ruddy-featured monk stood before me in the cheerful glow of the cressets that illumed a capacious hallway.

“I bid you welcome to the abbey of Perigon,” he said, in a suave rumble, and even as he spoke, another robed and hooded figure appeared and took my horse in charge. As I murmured my thanks and acknowledgments, the storm broke and tremendous gusts of rain, accompanied by ever-nearing peals of thunder, drove with demoniac fury on the door that had closed behind me.

“It is fortunate that you found us when you did,” observed my host. “ ’Twere ill for man and beast to be abroad in such a hell-brew.”

===Chinese===
“想必是一座修道院，”我心想，一边勒住马，从那疲惫不堪的坐骑上翻身下来，提起那枚铸成狗头形状的沉重铜门环，让它落在橡木的门扉之上。那声响出乎意料地洪亮低沉，带着一种近乎墓穴般的回响，我不由自主地打了个寒噤，涌起一阵惊愕、一阵异乎寻常的惶遽。然而片刻之后，这一切便荡然无存——门扉洞开，一位身材高大、面色红润的修士，在几盏照亮了宽敞门厅的油灯那欢快的辉光之中，出现在我面前。

“欢迎阁下来到佩里贡修道院（abbey of Perigon），”他用一种温醇低沉的嗓音说道；话音未落，又一位身披长袍、兜帽罩首的身影出现，将我的马牵去照管。当我低声致谢道劳之际，风暴骤然发作，一阵急似一阵的雷鸣伴着倾盆急雨，以恶魔般的狂暴，扑打在我身后那扇已然合拢的门上。

“你能在那个时候寻到我们，实属万幸，”我的主人说道。“这等地狱里熬煮出的风狂雨暴之中，人也好，畜生也好，在外头奔走可实在不是什么好事。”

===Original===
Divining without question that I was hungry as well as tired, he led me to the refectory and set before me a bountiful meal of mutton, brown bread, lentils and a strong excellent red wine.

He sat opposite me at the refectory table while I ate, and, with my hunger a little mollified, I took occasion to scan him more attentively. He was both tall and stoutly built, and his features, where the brow was no less broad than the powerful jaw, betokened intellect as well as a love for good living. A certain delicacy and refinement, an air of scholarship, of good taste and good breeding, emanated from him, and I thought to myself: “This monk is probably a connoisseur of books as well as of wines.” Doubtless my expression betrayed the quickening of my curiosity, for he said, as if in answer:

===Chinese===
他毫不费疑地看出我又累又饿，便领我到食堂，在我面前摆下一席丰盛的饭菜：羊肉、黑面包、扁豆，还有一杯浓烈而极上乘的红葡萄酒。

我在食堂的长案前用餐时，他坐在我的对面；待我的饥火稍稍平息，我便趁机将他端详得更仔细些。他身量既高且壮，面庞上那与有力的下颌同样宽阔的额头，透出学识，亦显出对优裕生活之爱好。一种难以言传的精致与文雅，一种饱学的、品味不凡且教养良好的气度，自他身上散发出来，我心中暗想：“这位修士，恐怕既是美酒的鉴赏家，也是好书的赏鉴家。”想必我的神情泄露了那骤然涌起的好奇，因为他仿佛作答似地说道：

===Original===
“I am Hilaire, the abbot of Perigon. We are a Benedictine order, who live in amity with God and with all men, and we do not hold that the spirit is to be enriched by the mortification or impoverishment of the body. We have in our butteries an abundance of wholesome fare, in our cellars the best and oldest vintages of the district of Averoigne. And, if such things interest you, as mayhap they do, we have a library that is stocked with rare tomes, with precious manuscripts, with the finest works of heathendom and Christendom, even to certain unique writings that survived the holocaust of Alexandria.”

“I appreciate your hospitality,” I said, bowing. “I am Christophe Morand, a law-student, on my way home from Tours to my father’s estate near Moulins. I, too, am a lover of books, and nothing would delight me more than the privilege of inspecting a library so rich and curious as the one whereof you speak.”

Forthwith, while I finished my meal, we fell to discussing the classics, and to quoting and capping passages from Latin, Greek or Christian authors. My host, I soon discovered, was a scholar of uncommon attainments, with an erudition, a ready familiarity with both ancient and modern literature that made my own seem as that of the merest beginner by comparison. He, on his part, was so good as to commend my far from perfect Latin, and by the time I had emptied my bottle of red wine we were chatting familiarly like old friends.

All my fatigue had now flown, to be succeeded by a rare sense of well-being, of physical comfort combined with mental alertness and keenness. So, when the abbot suggested that we pay a visit to the library, I assented with alacrity.

===Chinese===
“我是希莱尔（Hilaire），佩里贡修道院的院长。我们属本笃会（Benedictine），与上帝、与一切世人皆和睦共处；我们并不以为，灵魂的丰盈须借肉身的苦行或匮乏来成就。我们的储膳室里备有丰足的康健之食，地窖之中藏有阿维罗涅全境最上乘、最陈年的佳酿。再者，若这类事物也能令阁下发生兴趣——也许确是如此——我们还有一座藏书楼，里面满是珍本典籍、贵重写卷，汇集了异教与基督教世界最精妙的著作，甚至还有几部劫后仅存的孤本，是从亚历山大城那场浩劫之中幸存下来的。”

“多谢您的盛情款待，”我躬身说道。“我是克里斯托夫·莫朗，一名法科学生，正从图尔赶回穆兰附近父亲的田庄。我亦是个爱书之人，若能有幸一观您所说的那样一座丰富而珍奇的藏书楼，再没有比这更叫我欢喜的事了。”

于是，在我吃完那顿饭的当儿，我们便讨论起古典，引征并互答拉丁、希腊或基督教作家的章句。我很快便发觉，我的主人是一位造诣非凡的学者，其博学、其对古今著述那种信手拈来的熟稔，相形之下使我自家的所学显得不过是个最浅陋的初学者。他那一头，倒也不吝称赞我那远称不上完美的拉丁文；待我将那一瓶红葡萄酒饮尽之时，我们已如老友一般熟络地闲谈了。

我这一路的劳乏此刻已飞散净尽，取而代之的是一种难得的安适之感——身体的舒泰与心智的清明敏锐交织在一起。于是，当院长提议我们去看一看藏书楼时，我便欣然应允。

---

===Original===
He led me down a long corridor, on each side of which were cells belonging to the brothers of the order, and unlocked, with a huge brazen key that depended from his girdle, the door of a great room with lofty ceiling and several deep-set windows. Truly, he had not exaggerated the resources of the library; for the long shelves were overcrowded with books, and many volumes were piled high on the tables or stacked in corners. There were rolls of papyrus, of parchment, of vellum; there were strange Byzantine or Coptic bibles; there were old Arabic and Persian manuscripts with floriated or jewel-studded covers; there were scores of incunabula from the first printing-presses; there were innumerable monkish copies of antique authors, bound in wood or ivory, with rich illuminations and lettering that was often in itself a work of art.

With a care that was both loving and meticulous, the abbot Hilaire brought out volume after volume for my inspection. Many of them I had never seen before; some were unknown to me even by fame or rumor. My excited interest, my unfeigned enthusiasm, evidently pleased him, for at length he pressed a hidden spring in one of the library tables and drew out a long drawer, in which, he told me, were certain treasures that he did not care to bring forth for the edification or delectation of many, and whose very existence was undreamed of by the monks.

===Chinese===
他领我穿过一条长长的走廊，走廊两侧便是修士们的静室；随后，他以一柄悬于腰带之上的巨大铜钥匙，打开了一间高大宽敞、开着几扇深凹窗户的大屋的房门。他确不曾夸大那座藏书楼的丰厚家底：长长的书架塞得满满当当，许多卷册高高叠在案头，或堆叠于角落。那里有纸莎草、羊皮纸、犊皮纸的卷轴；有奇异的拜占庭或科普特语《圣经》；有古老的阿拉伯与波斯文写卷，封面之上饰以花叶纹或嵌满珠宝；有数十册初代印刷机印行的摇篮本；更有无数修士手抄的古典作家著作，以木质或象牙装帧，彩绘华美，那字迹本身便往往已是一件艺术品。

希莱尔院长以一种既眷爱又审慎的细心，一卷接一卷地取出书来供我观览。其中许多我前所未见；有些甚至连名气与传闻也未曾听闻。我那兴奋的兴致、我那毫不作伪的热忱，显然使他甚为欢悦，因为到了后来，他按动藏书楼一张书案内的一个暗簧，抽出一只长长的抽屉——他告诉我，里面放着几件他不愿轻易示人、以供多数人开蒙或赏玩的珍品，而这些珍品的存在，连修道院里的修士们也做梦都不曾想到。

===Original===
“Here,” he continued, “are three odes by Catullus which you will not find in any published edition of his works. Here, also, is an original manuscript of Sappho—a complete copy of a poem otherwise extant only in brief fragments; here are two of the lost tales of Miletus, a letter of Perides to Aspasia, an unknown dialogue of Plato and an old Arabian work on astronomy, by some anonymous author, in which the theories of Copernicus are anticipated. And, lastly, here is the somewhat infamous Histoire d’Amour, by Bernard de Vaillantcoeur, which was destroyed immediately upon publication, and of which only one other copy is known to exist.”

===Chinese===
“这里，”他接着说，“是卡图卢斯（Catullus）的三首颂歌，你在任何已刊行的他的作品集里都找不到。这里还有萨福（Sappho）的一部原稿——一首诗的完整抄本，除此之外，这首诗仅以零星残篇存世；这里有两篇已佚的米利都（Miletus）故事，一封佩里德斯（Perides）致阿斯帕西娅（Aspasia）的信函，一篇无人知晓的柏拉图（Plato）对话录，还有一部出自某位无名作者之手的古老阿拉伯文天文学著作，其中竟已预示了哥白尼（Copernicus）的学说。最后，这里还有一部名声颇为不堪的《爱史》（Histoire d’Amour），作者贝尔纳·德·瓦扬克尔（Bernard de Vaillantcoeur），此书甫一刊行即被销毁，已知存世者除此之外仅另有一部。”

===Original===
As I gazed with mingled awe and curiosity on the unique, unheard-of treasures he displayed, I saw in one corner of the drawer what appeared to be a thin volume with plain untitled binding of dark leather. I ventured to pick it up, and found that it contained a few sheets of closely written manuscript in old French.

“And this?” I queried, turning to look at Hilaire, whose face, to my amazement, had suddenly assumed a melancholy and troubled expression.

“It were better not to ask, my son.” He crossed himself as he spoke, and his voice was no longer mellow, but harsh, agitated, full of a sorrowful perturbation. “There is a curse on the pages that you hold in your hand: an evil spell, a malign power is attached to them, and he who would venture to peruse them is henceforward in dire peril both of body and soul.” He took the little volume from me as he spoke, and returned it to the drawer, again crossing himself carefully as he did so.

===Chinese===
当我怀着敬畏与好奇交织的心情，凝望他所展示的那一件件举世无双、闻所未闻的珍品时，我瞥见抽屉的一角有一本看似薄薄的、以深色皮革素净装帧、未题书名的卷册。我斗胆将它拿起，发现里面是寥寥数页密密写满的古法文手稿。

“这一本呢？”我问道，转过头去看希莱尔——令我惊愕的是，他的脸上忽然浮起一种忧郁而惶乱的神情。

“最好莫问，我的孩子。”他一边说，一边在胸前画了个十字，他的嗓音不再醇厚，而是变得粗粝、激越，满含一种哀痛的扰攘。“你手中所执的那几页纸上，附着一道诅咒：一个邪咒、一股凶险之力附着其上，凡胆敢披阅之人，自此身心两方皆要陷入可怖的危难。”他一边说着，一边从我手中取过那本小书，放回抽屉，动作之间又郑重地画了一个十字。

===Original===
“But, father,” I dared to expostulate, “how can such things be? How can there be danger in a few written sheets of parchment?”

“Christophe, there are things beyond your understanding, things that it were not well for you to know. The might of Satan is manifestable in devious modes, in diverse manners; there are other temptations than those of the world and the flesh, there are evils no less subtle than irresistible, there are hidden heresies, and necromancies other than those which sorcerers practise.”

“With what, then, are these pages concerned, that such occult peril, such unholy power lurks within them?”

“I forbid you to ask.” His tone was one of great rigor, with a finality that dissuaded me from further questioning.

===Chinese===
“可是，神父，”我斗胆进言，“怎会有这样的事？几页写着字的羊皮纸，其中怎会有凶险？”

“克里斯托夫，世上有超出你理解的事物，有些事你不知道反而为好。撒旦的大能可以千般途径、万种形态显化；除了这尘世与肉身的诱惑之外，尚有别的诱惑；有些邪祟既幽微难测又势不可挡；有隐秘的异端，也有术士所施行之外的其他死灵之术。”

“那么，这几页纸究竟所关何事，其中竟潜伏着这等秘术之险、这等不洁之力？”

“我不许你追问。”他的语气十分严厉，带着一种不容置疑的决绝，打消了我继续追问的念头。

===Original===
“For you, my son,” he went on, “the danger would be doubly great, because you are young, ardent, full of desires and curiosities. Believe me, it is better to forget that you have ever seen this manuscript.” He closed the hidden drawer, and as he did so, the melancholy troubled look was replaced by his former benignity.

“Now,” he said, as he turned to one of the bookshelves, “I will show you the copy of Ovid that was owned by the poet Petrarch.” He was again the mellow scholar, the kindly, jovial host, and it was evident that the mysterious manuscript was not to be referred to again. But his odd perturbation, the dark and awful hints he had let fall, the vague terrific terms of his proscription, had all served to awaken my wildest curiosity, and, though I felt the obsession to be unreasonable, I was quite unable to think of anything else for the rest of the evening. All manner of speculations, fantastic, absurd, outrageous, ludicrous, terrible, defiled through my brain as I duly admired the incunabula which Hilaire took down so tenderly from the shelves for my delectation.

===Chinese===
“至于你，我的孩子，”他接着说，“那凶险将会加倍地大，因为你年轻、炽烈，满怀欲望与好奇。相信我，最好忘了你曾见过这部手稿。”他合上那只暗屉，合上之际，那忧郁惶乱的神情便被先前的和蔼所取代。

“来，”他说着，转身走向一处书架，“我给你看那部奥维德（Ovid），它曾是诗人彼特拉克（Petrarch）的旧藏。”他重又变回了那个醇厚的学者、那位和蔼快活的主人，显然那部神秘的手稿是不许再被提起的了。然而他那古怪的扰攘、他无意间漏出的那些黑暗可怖的暗示、他那道禁令里所用的一切模糊而骇人的字眼，都已足以唤醒我最为狂野的好奇；虽说我也觉出这般执念并不合理，可这一晚余下的时辰里，我再也无法去想别的任何事。种种臆测——荒诞的、悖理的、骇人听闻的、滑稽的、可怖的——如行军列队一般鱼贯穿过我的脑海，而我则一如既往地赞叹着希莱尔那般温存地从架上取下来供我赏玩的那些摇篮本。

===Original===
At last, toward midnight, he led me to my room—a room especially reserved for visitors, and with more of comfort, of actual luxury in its hangings, carpets and deeply quilted bed than was allowable in the cells of the monks or of the abbot himself. Even when Hilaire had withdrawn, and I had proved for my satisfaction the softness of the bed allotted me, my brain still whirled with questions concerning the forbidden manuscript. Though the storm had now ceased, it was long before I fell asleep; but slumber, when it finally came, was dreamless and profound.

===Chinese===
终于，临近午夜时分，他领我到我的卧房——这是一间专为来客保留的屋子，其帷幔、地毯与厚厚絮棉的床铺所含的舒适、乃至实在的奢华，比之修士乃至院长本人的静室所容许的，都更胜一筹。纵然希莱尔已经退出，纵然我已为了称心而试过那张分派给我的床的柔软，我的脑子仍因那部禁书所引发的种种疑问而旋转不休。风暴此时虽已止息，我却许久方才入睡；然而睡眠一旦真正降临，便是无梦而深沉的。

---

===Original===
When I awoke, a river of sunshine clear as molten gold was pouring through my window. The storm had wholly vanished, and no lightest tatter of cloud was visible anywhere in the pale-blue October heavens. I ran to the window and peered out on a world of autumnal forest and fields all a-sparkle with the diamonds of rain. All was beautiful, all was idyllic to a degree that could be fully appreciated only by one who had lived for a long time, as I had, within the walls of a city, with towered buildings in lieu of trees and cobbled pavements where grass should be. But, charming as it was, the foreground held my gaze only for a few moments; then, beyond the tops of the trees, I saw a hill, not more than a mile distant, on whose summit there stood the ruins of some old château, the crumbling, broken-down condition of whose walls and towers was plainly visible. It drew my gaze irresistibly, with an overpowering sense of romantic attraction, which somehow seemed so natural, so inevitable, that I did not pause to analyze or wonder; and once having seen it, I could not take my eyes away, but lingering at the window for how long I knew not, scrutinizing as closely as I could the details of each time-shaken turret and bastion. Some undefinable fascination was inherent in the very form, the extent, the disposition of the pile—some fascination not dissimilar to that exerted by a strain of music, by a magical combination of words in poetry, by the features of a beloved face. Gazing, I lost myself in reveries that I could not recall afterward, but which left behind them the same tantalizing sense of innominable delight which forgotten nocturnal dreams may sometimes leave.

===Chinese===
我醒来时，一道清澈如熔金般的阳光正从窗外倾泻而入。风暴已彻底消散，十月的淡蓝天穹之上，连最薄的一片碎云也无处可寻。我奔到窗前，向外窥望那一片秋日的林木与田野——它们尽在雨后钻石般的水珠里熠熠生辉。一切都那么美，一切都如田园诗境一般，美到一种境地，唯有长久以来如我这般、被囚于城墙之内、以高耸的楼宇取代树木、以鹅卵石路取代青草之人，方能充分领略。然而眼前近处的景致虽如此动人，却只攫住我的目光片刻；随即，在树梢的那一边，我望见一座山丘，不过一英里之遥，山顶之上矗立着一座古堡的废墟，其墙垣与塔楼倾颓破败之状，清晰可见。它以一种不可抵挡的、压倒一切的浪漫吸引力，无可挽回地将我的目光攫去；这吸引力不知怎的，显得如此自然、如此理所当然，以至于我竟不曾停下来分析或惊奇；而一旦看见它，我便再也无法移开双目，只在那窗前流连了不知多久，尽可能仔细地端详着每一座被岁月摇撼的角塔与壁垒的细部。那堆废墟的形式、范围、布局本身，便蕴含着某种无从界说的魅惑——一种与一段乐曲所施展的、与诗中字句的某种魔幻组合所施展的、与一张钟爱面庞的五官所施展的并无二致的魅惑。我凝望着，迷失在事后再也无法追忆的种种遐想之中，而这些遐想所留下的，恰如那些被遗忘的夜梦有时所留下的一样，是一种不可名状之欢愉的、撩人又难耐的余味。

===Original===
I was recalled to the actualities of life by a gentle knock at my door, and realized that I had forgotten to dress myself. It was the abbot, who came to inquire how I had passed the night, and to tell me that breakfast was ready whenever I should care to arise. For some reason, I felt a little embarrassed, even shamefaced, to have been caught daydreaming; and though this was doubtless unnecessary, I apologized for my dilatoriness. Hilaire, I thought, gave me a keen, inquiring look, which was quickly withdrawn, as, with the suave courtesy of a good host, he assured me that there was nothing whatever for which I need apologize.

When I had breakfast, I told Hilaire, with many expressions of gratitude for his hospitality, that it was time for me to resume my journey. But his regret at the announcement of my departure was so unfeigned, his invitation to tarry for at least another night was so genuinely hearty, so sincerely urgent, that I consented to remain. In truth, I required no great amount of solicitation, for, apart from the real liking I had taken to Hilaire, the mystery of the forbidden manuscript had entirely enslaved my imagination, and I was loth to leave without having learned more concerning it. Also, for a youth with scholastic leanings, the freedom of the abbot’s library was a rare privilege, a precious opportunity not to be passed over.

“I should like,” I said, “to pursue certain studies while I am here, with the aid of your incomparable collection.”

“My son, you are more than welcome to remain for any length of time, and you can have access to my books whenever it suits your need or inclination.” So saying, Hilaire detached the key of the library from his girdle and gave it to me. “There are duties,” he went on, “which will call me away from the monastery for a few hours today, and doubtless you will desire to study in my absence.”

===Chinese===
一阵轻轻的叩门声把我唤回了现实，我这才发觉自己竟忘了更衣。是院长，他来探问我昨夜睡得如何，并告诉我早餐已备好，我什么时候愿意起身都行。不知为何，被人撞见在白日做梦，我略觉有些窘迫，甚至有些赧然；虽说这无疑大可不必，我仍为自己的迟慢道了歉。我觉得希莱尔向我投来一道锐利的、探询的目光，但那目光旋即收回，他随即以一位好主人的温文礼数，向我保证我根本无须为任何事致歉。

用过早餐之后，我对希莱尔再三致谢其盛情款待，并说自己是时候继续赶路了。然而，听闻我将要离去，他的惋惜是那样毫不作伪，他邀请我至少再留一宿的殷勤是那样真诚恳切、那样发自肺腑，我便应允留下。说实在的，我并不需要多少劝留，因为除了我对希莱尔确实已生了好感之外，那部禁书的神秘已将我的想象完全奴役，我实在不愿在未曾多知一二之前便离去。况且，对一个向学的青年而言，能自由出入院长的那座藏书楼，乃是一种难得的恩遇，一个不可错过的珍贵良机。

“我希望能，”我说道，“趁我在此逗留期间，借您那无可比拟的藏书之助，做些研读。”

“我的孩子，你愿意留下多久都再欢迎不过，你随时可凭你的需要或兴致，取阅我的藏书。”说罢，希莱尔从腰带上解下藏书楼的钥匙，交给了我。“今天有几桩事务，”他接着说，“会让我离开修道院几个时辰，想必你也愿意趁我不在之时研读吧。”

===Original===
A little later, he excused himself and departed. With inward felicitations on the longed-for opportunity that had fallen so readily into my hands, I hastened to the library, with no thought save to read the proscribed manuscript. Giving scarcely a glance at the laden shelves, I sought the table with the secret drawer, and fumbled for the spring. After a little anxious delay, I pressed the proper spot and drew forth the drawer. An impulsion that had become a veritable obsession, a fever of curiosity that bordered upon actual madness, drove me, and if the safety of my soul had really depended upon it, I could not have denied the desire which forced me to take from the drawer the thin volume with plain unlettered binding.

===Chinese===
稍后，他告辞而去。我暗自庆幸这朝思暮想的机会竟如此轻易地落入我手中，便急匆匆赶往藏书楼，心头再无别的念头，只想着去读那部被禁的手稿。我几乎不曾向那塞得满满的书架瞥上一眼，径直寻到那张带暗屉的书案，摸索起那个暗簧。经过一阵焦灼的耽搁之后，我终于按中了那一处，把抽屉拉了出来。一股冲动——它已变成了一种名副其实的痴迷，一种近乎真正疯狂的、好奇的狂热——驱使着我，纵然我灵魂的安危真的系于此，我也无法抑制那股欲望，它迫着我从抽屉里取出了那本素净无字装帧的薄册。

---

===Original===
Seating myself in a chair near one of the windows, I began to peruse the pages, which were only six in number. The writing was peculiar, with letter-forms of a fantasticality I had never met before, and the French was not only old but well-nigh barbarous in its quaint singularity. Notwithstanding the difficulty I found in deciphering them, a mad, unaccountable thrill ran through me at the first words, and I read on with all the sensations of a man who had been bewitched or who had drunken a philtre of bewildering potency.

There was no title, no date, and the writing was a narrative which began almost as abruptly as it ended. It concerned one Gerard, Comte de Venteillon, who, on the eve of his marriage to the renowned and beautiful demoiselle, Eleanor des Lys, had met in the forest near his château a strange, half-human creature with hoofs and horns. Now Gerard, as the narrative explained, was a knightly youth of indisputably proven valor, as well as a true Christian; so, in the name of our Savior, Jesus Christ, he bade the creature stand and give an account of itself.

===Chinese===
我在靠窗的一张椅子上坐下，开始披阅那几页手稿——它们一共只有六页。那笔迹十分奇特，字母的形态诡谲得出奇，为我前所未见；那法文不仅古旧，更因它那乖僻的怪异而近乎蛮荒。尽管我辨认起来颇觉费解，然而读下最初几个字时，一股疯狂的、无从解释的战栗便流遍我全身，我继续读下去，浑身涌起一个中了邪术、或饮下了一剂令人神迷的迷魂汤之人所有的种种感觉。

那手稿既无标题，亦无日期，所写乃是一篇叙事，开头几乎与其结尾一样突兀。它讲的是一个名叫杰拉德（Gérard）、旺特伊永伯爵（Comte de Venteillon）的人，在他与那位远近闻名、美貌绝伦的少女埃莉诺·德·利（Eleanor des Lys）成婚的前夜，于自家城堡附近的森林之中遇见了一个长着蹄与角的、半人半兽的怪物。据这篇叙事所言，杰拉德乃是一位勇武经过无可置疑之验证的骑士少年，亦是一个真正的基督徒；于是，他以我等救主耶稣基督之名，命那怪物站住，交代它的来历。

===Original===
Laughing wildly in the twilight, the bizarre being capered before him, and cried:

“I am a satyr, and your Christ is less to me than the weeds that grow on your kitchen-middens.”

Appalled by such blasphemy, Gerard would have drawn his sword to slay the creature, but again it cried, saying:

“Stay, Gerard de Venteillon, and I will tell you a secret, knowing which, you will forget the worship of Christ, and forget your beautiful bride of tomorrow, and turn your back on the world and on the very sun itself with no reluctance and no regret.”

Now, albeit half unwillingly, Gerard lent the satyr an ear and it came closer and whispered to him. And that which it whispered is not known; but before it vanished amid the blackening shadows of the forest, the satyr spoke aloud once more, and said:

“The power of Christ has prevailed like a black frost on all the woods, the fields, the rivers, the mountains, where abode in their felicity the glad, immortal goddesses and nymphs of yore. But still, in the cryptic caverns of earth, in places far underground, like the hell your priests have fabled, there dwells the pagan loveliness, there cry the pagan ecstasies.” And with the last words, the creature laughed again its wild unhuman laugh, and disappeared among the darkening boles of the twilight trees.

===Chinese===
那怪物在暮色之中狂笑不止，在他面前蹦跳腾跃，高喊道：

“我是个萨提尔（satyr），你们的基督在我眼里，还不如你们垃圾堆上长出的杂草。”

杰拉德被这般亵渎之言骇住，本欲拔剑斩杀那怪物，可它又叫了起来，说道：

“且慢，杰拉德·德·旺特伊永，我要告诉你一个秘密——知道了它，你便会忘却对基督的礼拜，忘却你明日那美貌的新娘，转过身去背离这尘世、背离那日头本身，全无不舍，全无悔憾。”

于是，杰拉德虽是半不情愿，却也倾耳去听那萨提尔；那怪物凑得更近，向他耳语。它所耳语的内容，无人知晓；但在它消失于森林那渐渐变黑的阴影之中以前，那萨提尔又高声说了一句话：

“基督之力已如一场黑色的霜冻，降临在从前那些欢乐而不朽的女神与宁芙们安居其间的所有林莽、田野、河流与山峦之上。但在大地幽秘的洞穴里，在地下深远处那些有如你们的祭司所编造的地狱一般的地方，异教的佳丽依然栖居，异教的狂喜仍在呼号。”话音落下之际，那怪物又发出它那狂野的、非人的笑声，消失在暮色之中渐渐变暗的树干之间。

===Original===
From that moment, a change was upon Gerard de Venteillon. He returned to his château with downcast mien, speaking no cheery or kindly word to his retainers, as was his wont, but sitting or pacing always in silence, and scarcely heeding the food that was set before him. Nor did he go that evening to visit his betrothed, as he had promised; but, toward midnight, when a waning moon had arisen red as from a bath of blood, he went forth clandestinely by the postern door of the château, and followed an old, half-obliterated trail through the woods, found his way to the ruins of the Château des Faussesflammes, which stands on a hill opposite the Benedictine abbey of Perigon.

Now these ruins (said the manuscript) are very old, and have long been avoided by the people of the district; for a legendry of immemorial evil clings about them, and it is said that they are the dwelling-place of foul spirits, the rendezvous of sorcerers and succubi. But Gerard, as if oblivious or fearless of their ill renown, plunged like one who is devil-driven into the shadow of the crumbling walls, and went, with the careful groping of a man who follows some given direction, to the northern end of the courtyard. There, directly between and below the two centermost windows, which, it may be, looked forth from the chamber of forgotten chatelaines, he pressed with his right foot on a flagstone differing from those about it in being of a triangular form. And the flagstone moved and tilted beneath his foot, revealing a flight of granite steps that went down into the earth. Then, lighting a taper he had brought with him, Gerard descended the steps, and the flagstone swung into place behind him.

On the morrow, his betrothed, Eleanor des Lys, and all her bridal train, waited vainly for him at the cathedral of Vyones, the principal town of Averoigne, where the wedding had been set. And from that time his face was beheld by no man, and no vaguest rumor of Gerard de Venteillon or of the fate that befell him has ever passed among the living…

===Chinese===
自那一刻起，杰拉德·德·旺特伊永身上便生了一变。他回到自己的城堡，神情颓丧，对扈从们再不说一句欢悦或和蔼的话——这本非他的常态——只一味默然枯坐或踱步，对摆在面前的饮食几乎不理不睬。那天傍晚他也没有如他所许诺的那般去探望未婚妻；而是临近午夜，当一轮下弦月升起、红得仿佛刚从一浴血水中浮起之时，他悄悄从城堡的角门潜出，沿着林中一条古老而半被湮没的小径前行，寻到了伪焰堡（Château des Faussesflammes）的废墟——那废墟矗立在一座与本笃会佩里贡修道院遥遥相对的山丘之上。

那废墟（手稿说道）年代极为久远，早已为本乡之人所远远避开；因为一种太古相传的邪祟传说紧紧附着其上，据说那里是污秽精灵的栖身之所，是术士与梦魔交会的渊薮。然而杰拉德，仿佛对它们的恶名浑然不觉，又或全然无畏，像被魔鬼所驱一般，一头扎进那倾颓墙垣的阴影之中，以一种循着既定方向、小心翼翼摸索之人的姿态，走到庭院的北端。在那里，正对着、且位于两扇居中的窗户的下方——那两扇窗，或许曾是某位已被遗忘的堡主夫人的闺房向外眺望之处——他用右脚踏上一块与周围石板不同的、呈三角形的石板。那石板在他脚下移动、倾斜，露出了一道通入地下的花岗岩台阶。于是，杰拉德点燃随身带来的一支细烛，走下台阶，那块石板在他身后悄然归位。

翌日，他的未婚妻埃莉诺·德·利，连同她整队迎亲的扈从，在阿维罗涅首邑维奥纳（Vyones）的大教堂里白白等候于他——那婚礼本定在那里举行。自那以后，再无人见过他的面容，关于杰拉德·德·旺特伊永、关于降临于他身上的命运，活人之间再不曾有过哪怕最模糊的一丝传言……

===Original===
Such was the substance of the forbidden manuscript, and thus it ended. As I have said before, there was no date, nor was there anything to indicate by whom it had been written or how the knowledge of the happenings related had come into the writer’s possession. But, oddly enough, it did not occur to me to doubt their veridity for a moment; and the curiosity I had felt concerning the contents of the manuscript was now replaced by a burning desire, a thousandfold more powerful, more obsessive, to know the ending of the story and to learn what Gerard de Venteillon had found when he descended the hidden steps.

In reading the tale, it had of course occurred to me that the ruins of the Château des Faussesflammes, described therein, were the very same ruins I had seen that morning from my chamber window; and pondering this, I became more and more possessed by an insane fever, by a frenetic, unholy excitement. Returning the manuscript to the secret drawer, I left the library and wandered for awhile in an aimless fashion about the corridors of the monastery. Chancing to meet there the same monk who had taken my horse in charge the previous evening, I ventured to question him, as discreetly and casually as I could, regarding the ruins which were visible from the abbey windows.

===Chinese===
那部禁书的主旨便是如此，它也便这样戛然而止。正如我先前所说，手稿上没有日期，也没有任何迹象能表明它出自谁人之手，又或所记诸事的来龙去脉是如何为作者所知的。然而奇怪的是，我竟片刻也不曾想过要去怀疑它们的真实性；我对那部手稿的内容所曾怀抱的好奇，此刻已被一种灼热的欲望所取代——一种强过千倍、痴迷过千倍的欲望——去探知这故事的结局，去知晓杰拉德·德·旺特伊永在那隐秘台阶之下，究竟发现了什么。

在读这个故事时，我自然已留意到：其中所描绘的伪焰堡废墟，正是我那天清晨从卧房窗口望见的那座废墟；我反复思忖此事，愈发被一种癫狂的热病、一种躁乱而不洁的兴奋所攫住。我将手稿放回暗屉，离开藏书楼，漫无目的地在修道院的回廊之间游荡了一阵。恰巧在那里遇见了昨夜替我牵马的那位修士，我便尽可能委婉而随意地，向他打听起从修道院窗口望得见的那座废墟的事来。

===Original===
He crossed himself, and a frightened look came over his broad, placid face at my query.

“The ruins are those of the Château des Faussesflammes,” he replied. “For untold years, men say, they have been the haunt of unholy spirits, of witches and demons; and festivals not to be described or even named are held within their walls. No weapon known to man, no exorcism or holy water, has ever prevailed against these demons; many brave cavaliers and monks have disappeared amid the shadows of Faussesflammes, never to return; and once, it is told, an abbot of Perigon went thither to make war on the powers of evil; but what befell him at the hands of the succubi is not known or conjectured. Some say that the demons are abominable hags whose bodies terminate in serpentine coils; others that they are women of more than mortal beauty, whose kisses are a diabolic delight that consumes the flesh of men with the fierceness of hellfire.… As for me, I know not whether such tales are true; but I should not care to venture within the walls of Faussesflammes.”

===Chinese===
他在胸前画了个十字，听我这一问，那张宽阔而平和的脸上掠过一丝惊惶之色。

“那废墟便是伪焰堡，”他答道。“据说不知多少世代以来，它们一直是不洁精灵、女巫与群魔出没之地；其墙垣之内，还举行着不可描述、甚至连名目都不可道的狂欢。世人所知的一切兵器，一切驱魔之术或圣水，都从未能降伏这些恶魔；许多英勇的骑士与修士消失在伪焰堡的阴影之中，再未归来；据说曾有一次，佩里贡的一位院长亲往那里，欲与邪魔之力开战；然而他在那些梦魔手中究竟遭遇了什么，无人知晓，亦无人能揣测。有人说，那些恶魔是可憎的丑婆，身躯的末端是盘绕的蛇尾；又有人说，她们是美逾凡尘的女子，她们的吻是一种魔鬼般的极欢，以地狱之火的猛烈，将男子的血肉焚蚀殆尽。……至于我，我不知这些传说是否属实；但我是绝不愿踏入伪焰堡的墙垣之内的。”

===Original===
Before he had finished speaking, a resolve had sprung to life full-born in my mind: I felt that I must go to the Château des Faussesflammes and learn for myself, if possible, all that could be learned. The impulse was immediate, overwhelming, ineluctable; and even if I had so desired, I could no more have fought against it than if I had been the victim of some sorcerer’s invultuation. The proscription of the abbot Hilaire, the strange unfinished tale in the old manuscript, the evil legendry at which the monk had now hinted—all these, it would seem, should have served to frighten and deter me from such a resolve; but, on the contrary, by some bizarre inversion of thought, they seemed to conceal some delectable mystery, to denote a hidden world of ineffable things, of vague undreamable pleasures that set my brain on fire and made my pulses throb deliriously. I did not know, I could not conceive, of what these pleasures would consist; but in some mystical manner I was as sure of their ultimate reality as the abbot Hilaire was sure of heaven.

I determined to go that very afternoon, in the absence of Hilaire, who, I felt instinctively, might be suspicious of any such intention on my part and would surely be inimical toward its fulfilment.

My preparations were very simple: I put in my pockets a small taper from my room and the heel of a loaf of bread from the refectory; and making sure that a little dagger which I always carried was in its sheath, I left the monastery forthwith. Meeting two of the brothers in the courtyard, I told them I was going for a short walk in the neighboring woods. They gave me a jovial pax vobiscum and went upon their way in the spirit of the words.

===Chinese===
在他话音未落之时，一个决心便已在我心中瓜熟蒂落、应运而生：我觉得自己必须亲往伪焰堡，尽可能把一切能查知的事，一一亲身查个明白。这股冲动是那样迅疾、那样压倒一切、那样无可违逆；纵然我想要抵抗，也抵抗不了，正如一个中了某位术士巫蛊之术的受害者一般无能为力。希莱尔院长的禁令、那部古卷中那个怪异而未完的故事、那修士此刻所暗示的邪祟传说——这一切，照理本应将我从这般决心之中吓退、阻遏；然而恰恰相反，借由某种古怪的心念倒错，它们仿佛遮掩着某种甘美的玄秘，昭示着一个隐秘的世界，一个充满不可言说之物、充满模糊而不可揣度之欢愉的世界，将我的脑子点燃，令我的脉息狂乱地悸动。我并不知道、也无法设想这些欢愉究竟会是些什么；但以某种神秘的方式，我对它们终极的真实性确信不疑，一如希莱尔院长对天堂的确信。

我决意就在那天下午动身，趁希莱尔不在之时——我本能地觉得，他对我这等念头或会生出疑心，也必定会阻挠它的实现。

我的准备十分简单：我把卧房里的一支细烛、食堂里一条面包的一头塞进口袋；确认了那柄我随身常带的匕首就在鞘中，我便径自离开了修道院。在庭院里遇见两位修士兄弟，我告诉他们我要到附近的林子里散一散步。他们欢快地朝我说了一声 pax vobiscum（愿和平与你们同在），便怀着那句话的精神上路去了。

---

===Original===
Heading as directly as I could for Faussesflammes, whose turrets were often lost behind the high and interlacing boughs, I entered the forest. There were no paths, and often I was compelled to brief detours and divagations by the thickness of the underbrush. In my feverous hurry to reach the ruins, it seemed hours before I came to the top of the hill which Faussesflammes surmounted, but probably it was little more than thirty minutes. Climbing the last declivity of the boulder-strewn slope, I came suddenly within view of the château, standing close at hand in the center of the level table which formed the summit. Trees had taken root in its broken-down walls, and the ruinous gateway that gave on the courtyard was half-choked by bushes, brambles and nettle-plants. Forcing my way through, not without difficulty, and with clothing that had suffered from the bramblethorns, I went, like Gerard de Venteillon in the old manuscript, to the northern end of the court. Enormous evil-looking weeds were rooted between the flagstones, rearing their thick and fleshy leaves that had turned to dull sinister maroons and purples with the onset of autumn. But I soon found the triangular flagstone indicated in the tale, and without the slightest delay or hesitation I pressed upon it with my right foot.

===Chinese===
我尽可能地循直奔伪焰堡的方向走进森林——那废墟的角塔，时常隐没在高高、交错的枝叶背后。林中没有路径，我常因下层灌木的茂密而不得不略作迂回、绕行。在我急于抵达废墟的热切之中，仿佛过了好几个时辰才登上伪焰堡所踞的山顶，但实际多半不过三十分钟有余。攀上那段乱石散布的山坡的最后一段斜坡，我骤然望见了那座城堡，近在咫尺地矗立在那构成山顶的平整台地的正中央。树木已在它倾颓的墙垣之间扎下了根，那通向庭院的破败门洞，被灌木、荆棘与荨麻堵去了一半。我好不容易才挤了过去——并非没有费力，衣裳也遭了荆棘的划扯——便如古卷中的杰拉德·德·旺特伊永那样，走到庭院的北端。硕大的、面目可憎的杂草在石板之间生了根，支起它那肥厚多肉的叶片，因秋意渐深而已转为一种沉闷而不祥的褐紫与暗紫。但我很快便寻到了故事里所指的那块三角形石板，毫无半点延宕或犹疑，我用右脚踏了上去。

===Original===
A mad shiver, a thrill of adventurous triumph that was mingled with something of trepidation, leaped through me when the great flagstone tilted easily beneath my foot, disclosing dark steps of granite, even as in the story. Now, for a moment, the vaguely hinted horrors of the monkish legends became imminently real in my imagination, and I paused before the black opening that was to engulf me, wondering if some satanic spell had not drawn me thither to perils of unknown terror and inconceivable gravity.

Only for a few instants, however, did I hesitate. Then the sense of peril faded, the monkish horrors became a fantastic dream, and the charm of things unformulable, but ever closer at hand, always more readily attainable, tightened about me like the embrace of amorous arms. I lit my taper, I descended the stair; and even as behind Gerard de Venteillon, the triangular block of stone silently resumed its place in the paving of the court above me. Doubtless it was moved by some mechanism operable by a man’s weight on one of the steps; but I did not pause to consider its modus operandi, or to wonder if there were any way by which it could be worked from beneath to permit my return.

===Chinese===
当那块巨大的石板在我脚下轻易地倾斜，露出幽暗的花岗岩台阶——一如故事里那般——一股疯狂的战栗、一股掺杂着几分惊惶的冒险凯旋之快感，在我周身跃动而起。一时间，修士们那些传说中所隐隐暗示的可怖之事，在我想象之中变得迫在眉睫、真实可感起来，我在那将要吞噬我的漆黑洞口之前驻足，暗自狐疑：莫不是某种撒旦的咒术，将我引到此处，去赴一场未知的恐怖、一场不可设想的劫难？

然而，我只踌躇了片刻。随即，那危殆之感便消退了，修士们所说的种种可怖化作了一场荒诞不经的梦，而那种种无从言传、却已愈来愈近、愈来愈唾手可得之物的魅惑，如多情人臂膀的拥揽一般，将我越收越紧。我点燃细烛，走下台阶；正如在杰拉德·德·旺特伊永身后一样，那块三角形的石头在我头顶悄然归位，重新嵌入了庭院的铺石之中。它想必是由某种机关所带动，可由人踏在其中某一级台阶上的重量来触发；但我并不曾停下思忖它的运转之理，也不曾去想它是否有什么办法能从底下启动，好让我得以折返。

===Original===
There were perhaps a dozen steps, terminating in a low, narrow, musty vault that was void of anything more substantial than ancient, dust-encumbered cobwebs. At the end, a small doorway admitted me to a second vault that differed from the first only in being larger and dustier. I passed through several such vaults, and then found myself in a long passage or tunnel, half blocked in places by boulders or heaps of rubble that had fallen from the crumbling sides. It was very damp, and full of the noisome odor of stagnant waters and subterranean mold. My feet splashed more than once in little pools, and drops fell upon me from above, fetid and foul as if they had oozed from a charnel.

Beyond the wavering circle of light that my taper maintained, it seemed to me that the coils of dim and shadowy serpents slithered away in the darkness at my approach; but I could not be sure whether they really were serpents, or only the troubled and retreating shadows, seen by an eye that was still unaccustomed to the gloom of the vaults.

===Chinese===
大约有十来级台阶，尽头是一间低矮、狭窄、发霉的地下室，里面除了古旧蒙尘的蛛网之外，再无任何实在之物。在它的尽头，一道小门引我进入第二间地下室，它与第一间的分别，只在于更大、积尘更厚。我穿过好几间这样的地下室，随后便置身于一条长长的甬道、或者说地道之中，有些地方被从两侧崩塌的崖壁上落下的巨石或碎石堆堵去了一半。它极为潮湿，弥漫着一股死水与地下霉菌那令人作呕的臭气。我的脚不止一次踩进小小的水洼，溅起水花，头顶上也有水珠落在我身上，腥臭污浊，仿佛是从某处尸骨堂里渗出来的。

在我那支细烛所维持的摇曳光圈之外，我仿佛看见一团团幽暗、阴影般的蛇影，在我靠近之际窸窸窣窣地滑入了黑暗之中；但我无法断定它们究竟是不是蛇，抑或只是那些被惊扰而退去的阴影——落在一双尚不习惯地下室幽暗的眼睛之中。

===Original===
Rounding a sudden turn in the passage, I saw the last thing I had dreamt of seeing—the gleam of sunlight at what was apparently the tunnel’s end. I scarcely know what I had expected to find, but such an eventuation was somehow altogether unanticipated. I hurried on, in some confusion of thought, and stumbled through the opening, to find myself blinking in the full rays of the sun.
