Even before I had sufficiently recovered my wits and my eyesight to take note of the landscape before me, I was struck by a strange circumstance: Though it had been early afternoon when I entered the vaults, and though my passage through them could have been a matter of no more than a few minutes, the sun was now nearing the horizon. There was also a difference in its light, which was both brighter and mellower than the sun I had seen above Averoigne; and the sky itself was intensely blue, with no hint of autumnal pallor.

===Chinese===
绕过甬道一处突如其来的拐角，我看见了最最意想不到的东西——在地道显然是尽头之处，一缕阳光的闪烁。我几乎不知道自己原本指望寻见什么，但这样的结局，无论如何都是全然出乎意料的。我心头略有些纷乱，匆匆向前，跌跌撞撞地穿过那出口，发现自己正在那满溢而来的阳光里眨着眼睛。

甚至在我尚未充分恢复神志与目力、去打量眼前的景致之前，我便被一桩奇异的情形所震慑：我进入地下室时分明还是午后未晡，而我穿越其中所花的时间，至多不过几分钟而已，可此刻的太阳却已逼近地平线。那阳光也有所不同，比我方才在阿维罗涅所见的太阳更明亮、也更醇和；天穹本身则是一片浓烈的蓝，全无秋日那种苍白的痕迹。

===Original===
Now, with ever-increasing stupefaction, I stared about me, and could find nothing familiar or even credible in the scene upon which I had emerged. Contrary to all reasonable expectation, there was no semblance of the hill upon which Faussesflammes stood, or of the adjoining country; but around me was a placid land of rolling meadows, through which a golden-gleaming river meandered toward a sea of deepest azure that was visible beyond the tops of laurel-trees… But there are no laurel-trees in Averoigne, and the sea is hundreds of miles away: judge, then, my complete confusion and dumbfoundment.

It was a scene of such loveliness as I have never before beheld. The meadow-grass at my feet was softer and more lustrous than emerald velvet, and was full of violets and many-colored asphodels. The dark green of ilex-trees was mirrored in the golden river, and far away I saw the pale gleam of a marble acropolis on a low summit above the plain. All things bore the aspect of a mild and clement spring that was verging upon an opulent summer. I felt as if I had stepped into a land of classic myth, of Grecian legend; and moment by moment, all surprise, all wonder as to how I could have come there, was drowned in a sense of ever-growing ecstasy before the utter, ineffable beauty of the landscape.

===Chinese===
此时，怀着愈来愈甚的惊骇，我四下张望，在我所走出的这片景色之中，再也寻不见一丝熟悉的、哪怕是可信的东西。与一切合乎情理的预料相反，这里全无伪焰堡所踞的那座山丘的踪影，也全无周遭乡野的踪影；我周围是一片宁谧的、连绵草甸之乡，一条金光闪闪的河流蜿蜒穿过其中，流向一片最深邃的碧蓝之海——那海在月桂树的树梢之外依稀可见……可是阿维罗涅并无月桂树，而大海远在数百里格之外：那么，且想象一下我那彻底的迷乱与瞠目结舌吧。

那是一片我前所未见的、如此之美的景色。我脚下的草甸，比翡翠的天鹅绒更柔软、更润泽，开满了紫罗兰与五色斑斓的常春花（asphodels）。冬青栎的墨绿倒映在金色的河流之中，远处我望见一座大理石卫城在平原之上的一座低矮山巅上闪着苍淡的微光。万物皆呈现出一种温和、和煦的、正趋近于丰饶盛夏的春日气象。我觉得自己仿佛一步踏入了古典神话之境、希腊传说之乡；而一时复一时，所有的惊愕、所有对我是如何来到此处的惊奇，都淹没在一种不断高涨的狂喜之中——在那片景色那彻底的、不可言喻的美之前。

===Original===
Near by, in a laurel-grove, a white roof shone in the late rays of the sun. I was drawn toward it by the same allurement, only far more potent and urgent, which I had felt on seeing the forbidden manuscript and the ruins of Faussesflammes. Here, I knew with an esoteric certainty, was the culmination of my quest, the reward of all my mad and perhaps impious curiosity.

As I entered the grove, I heard laughter among the trees, blending harmoniously with the low murmur of their leaves in a soft, balmy wind. I thought I saw vague forms that melted among the boles at my approach; and once a shaggy, goat-like creature with human head and body ran across my path, as if in pursuit of a flying nymph.

===Chinese===
近处，一片月桂林中，一道白色的屋顶在迟暮的阳光里闪耀。我被它吸引过去，那吸引之力，正是我看见那部禁书、看见伪焰堡废墟时所感到的同一种魅惑，只不过更为强烈、更为急切。以一种秘传般的确定性，我知道：这里便是我这番追寻的极顶，便是我所有癫狂的、也许是不虔的好奇心的酬报。

我走入那片林子，听见树丛之间的笑声，与它们树叶在一阵轻柔、和煦的风中那低低的絮语，和谐地交融在一起。我仿佛看见一些模糊的身影，在我靠近之际隐没到了树干之间；还有一次，一个毛茸茸的、山羊般的生灵，却长着人的头颅与身躯，从我路上横穿而过，仿佛正在追逐一个奔逃的宁芙。

---

===Original===
In the heart of the grove, I found a marble place with a portico of Doric columns. As I neared it, I was greeted by two women in the costume of ancient slaves; and though my Greek is of the meagerest, I found no difficulty in comprehending their speech, which was of Attic purity.

“Our mistress, Nycea, awaits you,” they told me. I could no longer marvel at anything, but accepted my situation without question or surmise, like one who resigns himself to the progress of some delightful dream. Probably, I thought, it was a dream, and I was still lying in my bed at the monastery; but never before had I been favored by nocturnal visions of such clarity and surpassing loveliness. The interior of the palace was full of a luxury that verged upon the barbaric, and which evidently belonged to the period of Greek decadence, with its intermingling of Oriental influences. I was led through a hallway gleaming with onyx and polished porphyry, into an opulently furnished room, where, on a couch of gorgeous fabrics, there reclined a woman of goddess-like beauty.

===Chinese===
在林子的腹地，我寻见一座大理石的宫室，门廊立着一排多立克式的柱子。待我走近，便有两个身着古时女奴服饰的女子前来迎候；尽管我的希腊文极为贫薄，却发现要听懂她们的话语毫无窒碍——那是一种雅典风范的、纯正无瑕的希腊语。

“我家女主人妮赛娅（Nycea）正等候着您，”她们对我说。我已再不能为任何事感到惊异，只是将自身所处之境不加疑问、不加揣度地接受下来，一如一个听任某场甜美之梦自行演进、不再挣扎之人。我心想，这恐怕是一场梦，我仍躺在修道院的那张卧床之上；可我此前何曾蒙受过如此清明、如此超凡之美的夜梦眷顾。那宫室的内部，弥漫着一种近乎蛮荒的奢华，显然属于希腊式微的那个时代，其间糅杂着东方风物的影响。我被引着穿过一道闪烁着缟玛瑙与磨光斑岩之光华的门厅，进入一间陈设华美的屋子；屋中一张铺着锦绣织物的卧榻之上，斜倚着一位宛若神祇般美丽的女子。

===Original===
At sight of her, I trembled from head to foot with the violence of a strange emotion. I had heard of the sudden mad loves by which men are seized on beholding for the first time a certain face and form; but never before had I experienced a passion of such intensity, such all-consuming ardor, as the one I conceived immediately for this woman. Indeed, it seemed as if I had loved her for a long time, without knowing that it was she whom I loved, and without being able to identify the nature of my emotion or to orient the feeling in any manner.

===Chinese===
一见着她，我便因一种奇异情感的剧烈而自顶至踵地战栗起来。我曾听闻世间有那等突如而来的痴狂之恋——人于初见某张面容、某种身段之时便为之攫住；可我此前何曾经验过如此炽烈、如此焚心噬骨的激恋，一如我即刻对这女子所怀有的那般。诚然，我仿佛已爱了她许久，却不知我所爱者正是她，亦无从辨认我这情感的底蕴，无从以任何方式为之定向。

===Original===
She was not tall, but was formed with exquisite voluptuous purity of line and contour. Her eyes were of a dark sapphire blue, with molten depths into which the soul was fain to plunge as into the soft abysses of a summer ocean. The curve of her lips was enigmatic, a little mournful, and gravely tender as the lips of an antique Venus. Her hair, brownish rather than blond, fell over her neck and ears and forehead in delicious ripples confined by a plain fillet of silver. In her expression, there was a mixture of pride and voluptuousness, of regal imperiousness and feminine yielding. Her movements were all as effortless and graceful as those of a serpent.

===Chinese===
她身量不高，却生就一种线条与轮廓上精妙而肉感的纯净。她的双眸是深蓝宝石的色泽，那熔融的幽深之处，引得灵魂欣然欲坠，一如沉入夏日海洋那柔和的深渊。她唇的弧度是谜一般的，略带哀戚，又沉静而温柔，宛如一尊古时维纳斯的唇。她的头发，与其说是金色，毋宁说是褐色，以一道道醉人的涟漪垂落在她的颈、耳与额前，为一枚素净的银色发带所束。她的神情之中，糅杂着矜傲与耽溺、王者的威严与女子的柔顺。她的一举一动，皆如蛇一般毫不费力、优雅从容。

===Original===
“I knew you would come,” she murmured in the same soft-voweled Greek I had heard from the lips of her servants. “I have waited for you long; but when you sought refuge from the storm in the abbey of Perigon, and saw the manuscript in the secret drawer, I knew that the hour of your arrival was at hand. Ah! you did not dream that the spell which drew you so irresistibly, with such unaccountable potency, was the spell of my beauty, the magical allurement of my love!”

“Who are you?” I queried. I spoke readily in Greek, which would have surprised me greatly an hour before. But now, I was prepared to accept anything whatever, no matter how fantastic or preposterous, as part of the miraculous fortune, the unbelievable adventure which had befallen me.

“I am Nycea,” she replied to my question. “I love you, and the hospitality of my palace and of my arms is at your disposal. Need you know anything more?”

===Chinese===
“我就知道你会来，”她以我从她仆婢唇间所听到的那同一种元音柔和的希腊语低语道。“我已等候你多时了；可当你在佩里贡修道院中躲避那场风暴、在暗屉之中看见那部手稿之时，我便知道你到来的时辰已近。啊！你做梦也不曾想到，那般无可抗拒、那般不可解地有力地将你引来的咒术，正是我美貌的咒术，是我爱情的魔幻魅惑！”

“你是谁？”我问道。我说起希腊语来毫不费劲——这若在一小时之前，必会使我大为惊异。可此刻，我已准备接受一切，无论何等荒诞不经、何等悖谬离奇，皆视作我所遭遇的那桩奇迹般的际遇、那场不可置信的奇遇的一部分。

“我是妮赛娅，”她回答我的问话。“我爱你，我这宫室的、我这一双臂膀的款待，皆任凭你享用。你尚须知晓别的什么吗？”

===Original===
The slaves had disappeared. I flung myself beside the couch and kissed the hand she offered me, pouring out protestations that were no doubt incoherent, but were nevertheless full of an ardor that made her smile tenderly. Her hand was cool to my lips, but the touch of it fired my passion. I ventured to seat myself beside her on the couch, and she did not deny my familiarity. While a soft purple twilight began to fill the corners of the chamber, we conversed happily, saying over and over again all the sweet absurd litanies, all the felicitous nothings that come instinctively to the lips of lovers. She was incredibly soft in my arms, and it seemed almost as if the completeness of her yielding was unhindered by the presence of bones in her lovely body.

The servants entered noiselessly, lighting rich lamps of intricately carven gold, and setting before us a meal of spicy meats, of unknown savorous fruits and potent wines. But I could eat little, and while I drank, I thirsted for the sweeter wine of Nycea’s mouth.

===Chinese===
那些奴婢已然退去。我扑到卧榻之旁，亲吻她伸给我的那只手，倾吐出一连串无疑语无伦次、却满含令她温柔微笑之炽热的表白。她的手在我唇边是沁凉的，可那触碰却将我的欲念点燃。我斗胆在她身畔的榻上坐下，她并未拒绝我的狎昵。当一层柔和的紫色暮色开始充盈这间屋子的各个角落之时，我们欢悦地交谈着，一遍又一遍地诉说那些甜蜜而荒唐的连祷之辞、那些自然涌上情人唇间的恰切而无谓的絮语。她在我的臂弯里柔软得难以置信，她那般彻底的顺从，竟仿佛全然不受她那可爱身躯之中骨骼存在的妨碍。

仆人们悄无声息地进来，点亮了一盏盏雕工繁复的华美金灯，又在我们面前摆下一席香料的肉食、叫不出名字的美味鲜果与烈性的美酒。可我几乎食不下咽；我虽饮酒，所渴慕的却是妮赛娅唇间那更甘美的酒。


---

===Original===
I do not know when we fell asleep; but the evening had flown like an enchanted moment. Heavy with felicity, I drifted off on a silken tide of drowsiness, and the golden lamps and the face of Nycea blurred in a blissful mist and were seen no more.

===Chinese===
我不知道我们是何时入睡的；但那一晚已如一个着了魔的瞬间般飞逝而过。满载着至福，我顺着一条丝缎般的倦意之潮漂流而去，那些金灯与妮赛娅的面庞在一团至乐的雾霭里模糊了，再不可见。

===Original===
Suddenly, from the depths of a slumber beyond all dreams, I found myself compelled into full wakefulness. For an instant, I did not even realize where I was, still less what had aroused me. Then I heard a footfall in the open doorway of the room, and peering across the sleeping head of Nycea, saw in the lamplight the abbot Hilaire, who had paused on the threshold, A look of absolute horror was imprinted upon his face, and as he caught sight of me, he began to gibber in Latin, in tones where something of fear was blended with fanatical abhorrence and hatred. I saw that he carried in his hands a large bottle and an aspergillus. I felt sure that the bottle was full of holy water, and of course divined the use for which it was intended.

Looking at Nycea, I saw that she too was awake, and knew that she was aware of the abbot’s presence. She gave me a strange smile, in which I read an affectionate pity, mingled with the reassurance that a woman offers a frightened child.

“Do not fear for me,” she whispered.

===Chinese===
蓦然，从一场超越一切梦境的沉眠深处，我发现自己被一股力量逼入了完全的清醒。有那么一瞬，我甚至不知自己身在何处，更不知是什么惊醒了我。随即，我听见屋子那扇敞开的门口处有一阵脚步声，我从妮赛娅那沉睡的头颅那边望过去，借着灯光看见了希莱尔院长——他正停在门槛之上，一张脸上烙印着一种绝对的惊恐之色；待他一看见我，便开始用拉丁语急促不清地嘟哝起来，那声调之中糅杂着几分恐惧，以及一种狂热的憎嫌与仇怨。我看见他双手捧着一只大瓶与一支洒圣水器。我断定那瓶中所盛必是圣水，自然也揣度出了它将派何等用场。

我看向妮赛娅，见她亦已醒来，并知道她也察觉了院长的到来。她向我投来一个奇异的微笑，我在其中读出一种怜爱的悯恤，糅合着一个女子给予受惊孩童的那般抚慰。

“不必为我忧惧，”她低语道。

===Original===
“Foul vampire! accursed lamia! she-serpent of hell!” thundered the abbot suddenly, as he crossed the threshold of the room, raising the aspergillus aloft. At the same moment, Nycea glided from the couch, with an unbelievable swiftness of motion, and vanished through an outer door that gave upon the forest of laurels. Her voice hovered in my ear, seeming to come from an immense distance:

“Farewell for awhile, Christophe. But have no fear. You shall find me again if you are brave and patient.”

===Chinese===
“污秽的吸血鬼！可咒的拉弥亚！地狱里的雌蛇！”院长蓦地如雷般怒喝，一边迈过屋子的门槛，将那洒圣水器高高举起。就在同一刹那，妮赛娅以一种难以置信的迅疾，从卧榻上滑了下去，消失在一扇通向月桂林的门外。她的声音在我耳畔萦绕，仿佛来自极远极远之处：

“暂且别了，克里斯托夫。但不必忧惧。只要你够勇敢、够耐心，你必将再寻得我。”

===Original===
As the words ended, the holy water from the aspergillus fell on the floor of the chamber and on the couch where Nycea had lain beside me. There was a crash as of many thunders, and the golden lamps went out in a darkness that seemed full of falling dust, of raining fragments. I lost all consciousness, and when I recovered, I found myself lying on a heap of rubble in one of the vaults I had traversed earlier in the day. With a taper in his hand, and an expression of great solicitude, of infinite pity upon his face, Hilaire was stooping over me. Beside him lay the bottle and the dripping aspergillus.

===Chinese===
话音落下之际，那洒圣水器中的圣水便落在屋子的地板之上、落在妮赛娅方才卧于我身侧的那张卧榻之上。一声宛如众雷齐发般的轰响炸开，那些金灯在一重似乎满是飞坠尘埃、如雨碎片的黑暗之中尽数熄灭。我失去了所有知觉；待我苏醒过来，发觉自己正躺在一堆碎石之上，身处我白日里曾穿行过的那些地下室中的一间。希莱尔手持一支细烛，脸上带着一种极度的焦切、一种无量的悯恤之色，正俯身在我之上。他身侧放着那只瓶子与那支正滴着水的洒圣水器。

===Original===
“I thank God, my son, that I found you in good time,” he said. “When I returned to the abbey this evening and learned that you were gone, I surmised all that had happened. I knew you had read the accursed manuscript in my absence, and had fallen under its baleful spell, as have so many others, even to a certain reverend abbot, one of my predecessors. All of them, alas! beginning hundreds of years ago with Gerard de Venteillon, have fallen victims to the lamia who dwells in these vaults.”

“The lamia?” I questioned, hardly comprehending his words.

===Chinese===
“感谢上帝，我的孩子，幸而我寻你寻得还算及时，”他说道。“今夜我回到修道院，得知你已离去，我便将所发生的一切都揣度清楚了。我知道你趁我不在之时披阅了那部可咒的手稿，已堕入它那凶邪的咒术之下——如许多人一般，甚至包括某位可敬的院长，我的前任之一。他们所有人，唉！自数百年前的杰拉德·德·旺特伊永开始，皆已沦为栖居在这些地下室里的那头拉弥亚的牺牲。”

“拉弥亚？”我追问，几乎不能理解他的话。

===Original===
“Yes, my son, the beautiful Nycea who lay in your arms this night is a lamia, an ancient vampire, who maintains in these noisome vaults her palace of beatific illusions. How she came to take up her abode at Faussesflammes is not known, for her coming antedates the memory of men. She is old as paganism; the Greeks knew her; she was exorcised by Apollonius of Tyana; and if you could behold her as she really is, you would see, in lieu of her voluptuous body, the folds of a foul and monstrous serpent. All those whom she loves and admits to her hospitality, she devours in the end, after she has drained them of life and vigor with the diabolic delight of her kisses. The laurel-wooded plain you saw, the ilex-bordered river, the marble palace and all the luxury therein, were no more than a satanic delusion, a lovely bubble that rose from the dust and mold of immemorial death, of ancient corruption. They crumbled at the kiss of the holy water I brought with me when I followed you. But Nycea, alas! has escaped, and I fear she will still survive, to build again her palace of demoniacal enchantments, to commit again and again the unspeakable abomination of her sins.”

===Chinese===
“是的，我的孩子，今夜偎在你臂中的那位美丽的妮赛娅，乃是一头拉弥亚，一个古老的吸血鬼，在这些腥臭的地下室之中维持着她那至福幻象的宫室。她是何时前来伪焰堡安身的，已无人知晓，因为她的到来远在世人的记忆之前。她与异教一般古老；希腊人知道她；她曾被提亚纳的阿波罗尼乌斯（Apollonius of Tyana）所驱祓；你若能见到她真正的样子，便会看见，取代她那肉感身躯的，是一头污秽可怖之蛇的盘曲鳞身。凡她所爱、凡她纳于款待之人，她终将一一吞食——待她以那魔鬼般极欢之吻，将他们的生气与精力汲取净尽之后。你所见的那片月桂之林所覆的平原、那冬青栎夹岸的河流、那座大理石的宫室与其中一切的奢华，不过是撒旦的幻障，一个从太古之死、从古老腐朽的尘与霉之中升起的美丽泡影。它们在我追随你而来时所带来的圣水之一吻下，尽数崩塌。然而妮赛娅，唉！已然逃脱，我恐她仍将存世，去重建她那魔幻迷障的宫室，去再三再四地犯下她那不可名状的秽行。”

===Original===
Still in a sort of stupor at the ruin of my newfound happiness, at the singular revelations made by the abbot, I followed him obediently as he led the way through the vaults of Faussesflammes. He mounted the stairway by which I had descended, and as he neared the top and was forced to stoop a little, the great flagstone swung upward, letting in a stream of chill moonlight. We emerged; and I permitted him to take me back to the monastery.

===Chinese===
我仍因我这初得之幸福的倾覆、因院长那番奇异的揭示而处于一种恍惚之中，便恭顺地跟随着他，由他领路穿过伪焰堡的地下墓室。他登上我先前走下的那道台阶，待他将近顶端、不得不略略弯腰之时，那块巨大的石板便向上旋开，放进一道清冷的月光。我们走了出来；我任由他将我带回修道院。

===Original===
As my brain began to clear, and the confusion into which I had been thrown resolved itself, a feeling of resentment grew apace﻿—a keen anger at the interference of Hilaire. Unheedful whether or not he had rescued me from dire physical and spiritual perils, I lamented the beautiful dream of which he had deprived me. The kisses of Nycea burned softly in my memory, and I knew that whatever she was, woman or demon or serpent, there was no one in all the world who could ever arouse in me the same love and the same delight. I took care, however, to conceal my feelings from Hilaire, realizing that a betrayal of such emotions would merely lead him to look upon me as a soul that was lost beyond redemption.

===Chinese===
随着我的脑子渐次清明，那将我抛入其中的迷乱也自行化解，一种怨怼之感便随之滋长起来——一股针对希莱尔横加干预的、尖锐的愠怒。我不去理会他究竟是否将我从身心两方的险境之中搭救出来，我只为那被他褫夺的美丽之梦而痛惜。妮赛娅的吻在我记忆之中轻柔地灼烧着，我知道，无论她是什么——女人、恶魔抑或蛇——在这世间，再无人能在我心中唤起同样的爱恋与同样的欢悦。然而，我小心地对自己的情感加以掩饰，不令希莱尔察觉，因为我明白，这般情感的泄露，只会令他将我看作一个已无可救赎的沉沦之魂。

===Original===
On the morrow, pleading the urgency of my return home, I departed from Perigon. Now, in the library of my father’s house near Moulins, I write this account of my adventures. The memory of Nycea is magically clear, ineffably dear as if she were still beside me, and still I see the rich draperies of a midnight chamber illumined by lamps of curiously carven gold, and still I hear the words of her farewell:

“Have no fear. You shall find me again if you are brave and patient.”

===Chinese===
次日，我以归家之事迫切为由，辞别了佩里贡。此刻，在穆兰附近我父亲宅邸的藏书室之中，我写下这番奇遇的记述。妮赛娅的记忆以一种魔幻般的清明留在我心里，以一种不可言说的亲切留在我心里，仿佛她仍在我身侧；我仍看见一间子夜之屋的华美帷幔，被一盏盏雕工奇诡的金灯所照亮；我仍听见她道别的话语：

“不必忧惧。只要你够勇敢、够耐心，你必将再寻得我。”

===Original===
Soon I shall return, to visit again the ruins of the Château des Faussesflammes, and redescend into the vaults below the triangular flagstone. But, in spite of the nearness of Perigon to Faussesflammes, in spite of my esteem for the abbot, my gratitude for his hospitality and my admiration for his incomparable library, I shall not care to revisit my friend Hilaire.

===Chinese===
不久我便将重返，再去造访伪焰堡的废墟，再下到那块三角形石板之下的地下室里。可是，尽管佩里贡与伪焰堡近在咫尺，尽管我对院长心怀敬重、对他的盛情款待心怀感念、对他那无可比拟的藏书楼心怀钦慕，我却不愿再去造访我的朋友希莱尔了。

## The Ghost of Mohammed Din / 穆罕默德·丁之鬼

===Original===
"I'll wager a hundred rupees that you won't stay there overnight," said Nicholson.

It was late in the afternoon, and we were seated on the veranda of my friend's bungalow in the Begum suburb at Hyderabad. Our conversation had turned to ghosts, on which subject I was, at the time, rather skeptical, and Nicholson, after relating a number of bloodcurdling stories, had finished by remarking that a nearby house, which was said to be haunted, would give me an excellent chance to put the matter to the test.

===Chinese===
“我愿以一百卢比打赌，你断不敢在那屋里挨过一宿，”尼科尔森（Nicholson）说道。

那还是薄暮时分，我俩正坐在海得拉巴（Hyderabad）贝古姆区我朋友那幢平房的游廊上。先前话头转到了鬼魂一节，而这题目，我彼时是颇不以为然的。尼科尔森连着讲了数则令人毛骨悚然的传闻作结，末了又道：邻近有一座宅子，据说常闹鬼，倒正好给我一个当面验证的绝好机会。

===Original===
"Done!" I answered, laughing.

"It's no joking matter," said my friend, seriously. "However, if you really wish to encounter the ghost, I can easily secure you the necessary permission. The house, a six-roomed bungalow, owned by one Yussuf Ali Borah, is tenanted only by the spirit who appears to regard it as his exclusive property.

"Two years ago it was occupied by a Muslim merchant named Mohammed Din, and his family and servants. One morning they found the merchant dead—stabbed through the heart, and no trace of his murderer, whose identity still remains unrevealed.

"Mohammed Din's people left, and the place was let to a Parsee up from Bombay on business. He vacated the premises abruptly about midnight, and told a wild tale the next morning of having encountered a number of disembodied spirits, describing the chief one as Mohammed Din.

"Several other people took the place in turn, but their occupancy was generally of short duration. All told tales similar to the Parsee's. Gradually it acquired a bad reputation, and the finding of tenants became impossible."

===Chinese===
“一言为定！”我笑着应道。

“这可不是闹着玩的，”我朋友正色道，“不过，你若果真想会一会那鬼，要弄到入宅的许可，于我易如反掌。那宅子是一座六间房的平房，业主乃是一个名叫优素福·阿里·博拉（Yussuf Ali Borah）的人；如今租住的，却只有那幽魂一个，它俨然把这宅子视作自己独占的私产。

“两年之前，宅中住着一个名叫穆罕默德·丁（Mohammed Din）的穆斯林商人，连同他的家眷与仆役。某日清晨，他们发现这商人已然气绝——一刀贯穿心口，凶手却杳无踪迹，至今身份不明。

“穆罕默德·丁的家人迁走之后，此宅又租给了一个从孟买（Bombay）北上来此地办事的帕西人（Parsee）。此人约莫半夜便仓皇撤离，次日清晨讲出一通离奇之谈，说自己撞见了一群无形之鬼，还说为首的那个，正是穆罕默德·丁。

“其后又有数人相继租下此宅，然皆住不长久，所讲的故事，皆与那帕西人如出一辙。渐渐地，这宅子恶名远播，再想寻个租客，便万万不能了。”

===Original===
"Have you ever seen the ghost yourself?" I asked.

"Yes; I spent a night, or rather part of one, there, for I went out of the window about one o'clock. My nerves were not strong enough to stand it any longer. I wouldn't enter the place again for almost any sum of money."

Nicholson's story only confirmed my intention of occupying the haunted house. Armed with a firm disbelief in the supernatural, and a still firmer intention to prove it all rot, I felt myself equal to all the ghosts, native and otherwise, in India. Of my ability to solve the mystery, if there were any, I was quite assured.

===Chinese===
“那鬼，你可曾亲眼见过？”我问道。

“见过。我在那里头熬过一夜——说是一夜，其实只熬了一半，因为大约一点钟光景，我便从窗户里翻了出来。我的神经实在撑不住了。如今几乎任凭出多大一笔钱，我也再不肯踏进那地方一步了。”

尼科尔森这番话，反倒愈发坚定了我要去那凶宅住宿的念头。我胸中既怀着对鬼神之说坚不可移的不信，又怀着更要紧的一桩决心——非要戳穿这全是胡说不可；仗着这两样，我觉得纵是全印度那些鬼，无论本地的还是外来的，一齐扑上前来，我也应付裕如。至于戳破其中的玄虚——倘若当真有什么玄虚的话——我更是自问绰绰有余。

===Original===
"My friend," said Nicholson to Yussuf Ali Borah an hour later, "wishes to spend a night in your haunted bungalow."

The person addressed, a fat little Muslim gentleman, looked at me curiously.

"The house is at your service, Sahib," he said, "I presume that Nicholson Sahib has told you the experiences of the previous tenants?"

I replied that he had. "If the whole thing is not a trumped-up story, there is doubtless some trickery afoot," said I, "and I warn you that the trickster will not come off unharmed. I have a loaded revolver, and shall not hesitate to use it if I meet any disembodied spirits."

===Chinese===
一小时之后，尼科尔森对优素福·阿里·博拉说道：“我这位朋友，想在您那闹鬼的平房里宿上一夜。”

被称作的那人，是个矮胖的穆斯林绅士，正带着好奇之色打量着我。

“这宅子，萨希布（Sahib）尽管随意使用，”他说道，“我想，尼科尔森萨希布总该把先前那些租客的遭遇，都告诉过您了吧？”

我答说自然告诉过。“这事若非凭空捏造的胡言，那便必定有人在暗中装神弄鬼，”我说道，“我可把话说明在前头：那装鬼之人，休想全身而退。我带了上膛的左轮手枪，倘若当真撞见什么无形之鬼，决不会迟疑开枪。”

===Original===
Yussuf's only answer was to shrug his shoulders.

He gave us the keys, and we set out for the bungalow, which was only a few minutes' walk distant. Night had fallen when we reached it. Nicholson unlocked the door and we entered, and lighting a lamp I had brought with me, set out on a tour of inspection. The furniture consisted chiefly of two charpoys, three tabourets, an old divan quite innocent of cushions, a broken punkah, a three-legged chair and a dilapidated rug. Everything was covered with dust; the shutters rattled disconsolately, and all the doors creaked. The other rooms were meagrely furnished. I could hear rats running about in the dark. There was a compound adjoining, filled with rank reed and a solitary pipal tree. Nicholson said that the ghost generally appeared in one of the rooms opening upon it, and this I selected as the one in which to spend the night. It was a fitting place for ghosts to haunt. The ceiling sagged listlessly, and the one charpoy which it contained had a wobbly look.

===Chinese===
优素福唯一的回应，只是耸了耸肩。

他把钥匙交给我们，我们便动身往那平房走去，相去不过几分钟的脚程。到得门前，夜色已经四合。尼科尔森开了门锁，我们走了进去；我点亮随身带来的一盏油灯，便着手四处巡视一番。屋中的陈设，无非两张查尔帕伊（charpoy，绳床）、三只绣墩（tabouret）、一张旧长榻（divan）——榻上半个软垫也无——一具破败的布风扇（punkah），一把三条腿的椅子，外加一块破烂的地毯。件件器物都蒙着厚厚的灰尘；百叶窗在风中凄凄切切地哐当作响，所有的门都吱呀出声。其余各间的陈设亦同样寒素。黑暗之中，我能听见老鼠奔窜的声响。紧连着一侧的，是一座荒草丛生、长满茂密芦苇的庭院，其间孤零零立着一棵菩提树（pipal）。尼科尔森说，那幽魂通常显现在朝这庭院开门的那几间屋子里，我便拣了其中一间作为今夜的栖身之所。这确是鬼魂盘桓的相宜之地：顶棚有气无力地下垂着，屋里唯一的那张绳床，瞧着也摇摇欲坠。

===Original===
"Sleep well," said Nicholson. "You will find the atmosphere of this spirit-ridden place most conducive to slumber."

"Rats!" said I.

"Yes, there are plenty of rats here," he answered as he went out.

===Chinese===
“祝你好睡，”尼科尔森说道，“这鬼气森森的地方，那股氛围最是助眠不过。”

“不过是几只老鼠罢了！”我说道。

“不错，这里头老鼠确实不少，”他一边出门，一边答道。

===Original===
Placing the lamp on a tabouret, I lay down, with some misgivings as to its stability, on the charpoy. Happily, these proved unfounded, and laying my revolver close at hand, I took out a newspaper and began to read.

Several hours passed and nothing unusual happened. The ghost failed to materialize, and about eleven, with my fine skepticism greatly strengthened, and feeling a trifle ashamed concerning the hundred rupees which my friend would have to hand over the next morning, I lay down and tried to go to sleep. I had no doubt that my threat about the revolver to Yussuf Ali Borah had checked any plans for scaring me that might have been entertained.

Scarcely were my eyes closed when all the doors and windows, which had been creaking and rattling all evening, took on renewed activity. A light breeze had sprung up, and one shutter, which hung only by a single hinge, began to drum a tune on the wall. The rats scuttled about with redoubled energy, and a particularly industrious fellow gnawed something in the further corner for about an hour. It was manifestly impossible to sleep. I seemed to hear whisperings in the air, and once thought that I detected faint footsteps going and coming through the empty rooms. A vague feeling of eeriness crept upon me, and it required a very strong mental effort to convince myself that these sounds were entirely due to imagination.

===Chinese===
我把油灯搁在一只绣墩上——心里对这墩子是否承得住，未免有些惴惴——便在绳床上躺了下来。所幸这些顾虑并未应验。我把左轮手枪放在伸手可及之处，取出一张报纸，展读起来。

数个钟头过去，并无半点异样。那幽魂始终不曾显形。到了十一点光景，我那漂亮的怀疑之心愈发坚定，想起明早朋友便得乖乖交出一百卢比，反倒有几分惭愧，于是躺下来，试着入睡。我心下笃定：我对优素福·阿里·博拉所放的那番关于手枪的狠话，必定已吓得任何装鬼吓我的盘算都不敢施展了。

谁知我才一合眼，整晚吱呀哐当作响不止的门窗，竟又重新闹腾起来。一阵微风骤然而起，有一扇只余一只铰链挂着的百叶窗，竟拿墙作鼓，咚咚地敲起调子来。老鼠们加倍起劲地四下奔窜，其中一只格外勤快的，在远处墙角啃嚼着什么东西，足足啃了一个钟头。这般光景，要睡自然是万万不能的。我恍若听见半空里有人窃窃私语，又一度以为辨出了若有若无的脚步声，在那些空屋之间来来去去。一种说不清道不明的诡谲之感悄悄爬上心头，我不得不竭尽心力地劝服自己：这一切声响，全然是出于臆想。

===Original===
Finally the breeze died down, the loose shutter ceased to bang, the rat stopped gnawing, and comparative quiet being restored, I fell asleep. Two hours later I woke, and taking out my watch, saw, though the lamp had begun to burn dimly, that the hands pointed to two o'clock. I was about to turn over, when again I heard the mysterious footsteps, this time quite audibly. They seemed to approach my room, but when I judged them to be in the next apartment, ceased abruptly. I waited five minutes in a dead silence, with my nerves on edge and my scalp tingling.

===Chinese===
终于，风息了，那扇松脱的百叶窗不再拍打，老鼠也住了嘴；较为安宁的寂静既已恢复，我便沉沉睡去。两个钟头之后，我猛然惊醒，掏出怀表一瞧——灯芯已开始发暗——时针正指着两点。我正待翻身，孰料那神秘的脚步声又起，这一回，听得清清楚楚。那声响似是朝着我这间屋子逼近，可当我估量它已走入隔壁那间时，却又戛然而止。我在死一般的寂静中等了五分钟，神经绷得笔挺，头皮一阵阵发麻。

===Original===
Then I became aware that there was something between me and the opposite wall. At first it was a dim shadow, but as I watched, it darkened into a body. A sort of phosphorescent light emanated from it, surrounding it with pale radiance.

The lamp flared up and went out, but the figure was still visible. It was that of a tall native dressed in flowing white robes and a blue turban. He wore a bushy beard and had eyes like burning coals of fire. His gaze was directed intently upon me, and I felt cold shivers running up and down my spine. I wanted to shriek, but my tongue seemed glued to the roof of my mouth. The figure stepped forward and I noticed that the robe was red at the breast as though with blood.

This, then, was the ghost of Mohammed Din. Nicholson's story was true, and for a moment my conviction that the supernatural was all nonsense went completely to pieces. Only momentarily, however, for I remembered that I had a revolver, and the thought gave me courage. Perhaps it was a trick after all, and anger arose in me, and a resolve not to let the trickster escape unscathed.

===Chinese===
就在此时，我忽然察觉，在我与对面那堵墙之间，多出了什么东西。起初不过是一团模糊的暗影，可在我注视之下，它竟渐渐凝深，化为一个身形。那身形周身散发出一种磷火似的光，将它笼罩在一圈惨白的辉芒里。

油灯猛地一窜，旋即熄灭，然而那身影仍旧清晰可见。那是一个身量颇高的本地人，披着一袭飘垂的白袍，头缠一顶蓝色缠头。他生着浓密的胡须，一双眼睛犹如燃烧的火炭。他的目光直勾勾地盯住我，我只觉一阵阵寒噤顺着脊背上下游走。我想要惊呼，舌头却仿佛粘在了上颚。那身形向前迈了一步，我这才看清，他胸前的袍子上洇着一团殷红，仿佛是血。

那么，这便是穆罕默德·丁的鬼魂了。尼科尔森所讲的原是真的，刹那之间，我那“鬼神尽属无稽”的坚信，登时土崩瓦解。然而也只是一刹那——我记起自己尚有左轮手枪在手，这一念便壮了我的胆。说不定这终究是一场障眼法呢；于是我怒从心起，决计不让那装神弄鬼之人全身而退。

===Original===
I raised the weapon with a quick movement and fired. The figure being not over five paces distant, it was impossible to miss, but when the smoke had cleared it had not changed its position.

It began to advance, making no sound, and in a few moments was beside the charpoy. With one remaining vestige of courage I raised my revolver and pulled the trigger three times in succession, but without visible effect. I hurled the weapon at the figure's head, and heard it crash against the opposite wall an instant later. The apparition, though visible, was without tangibility.

===Chinese===
我迅疾地举枪，抬手便是一发。那身形距我不过五步之遥，断无落空之理，可待硝烟散去，它却分毫未移其位。

它开始向前逼来，全无声息，片刻之间便已到了绳床之畔。我凭着残存的一丝胆气，再次举起左轮手枪，接连扣动扳机三下，却毫无可见之效。我将手枪朝那身形的头上狠狠掷去，须臾便听见它哐当一声砸在对面墙上。这幻影虽则肉眼可见，却并无实体可触。

===Original===
Now it began to disappear. Very slowly at first it faded, then more rapidly until I could make out only the bare outlines. Another instant and all was gone but the outline of one hand, which hung motionless in the air. I got up and made a step towards it, then stopped abruptly, for the outlines again began to fill in, the hand to darken and solidify. Now I noticed something I had not before seen—a heavy gold ring set with some green gem, probably an emerald, appeared to be on the middle finger.

The hand began to move slowly past me towards the door opening into the next apartment. Lighting the lamp, I followed, all fear being thrown aside and desiring to find the explanation of the phenomenon. I could hear faint footfalls beneath the hand, as though the owner, though invisible, were still present. I followed it through the adjoining apartment and into the next, where it again stopped and hung motionless. One finger was pointed toward the further corner, where stood a tabouret, or stand.

===Chinese===
此时，它开始消隐了。起初褪去得极其缓慢，继而愈来愈快，直褪到只剩下一个空落落的轮廓。再过一瞬，一切俱已化为乌有，唯余一只手的轮廓，悬在半空，纹丝不动。我起身朝它迈了一步，又猛地收住脚——因为那轮廓竟又开始重新填实，那只手也重新变深、凝实。这时我才留意到一桩先前未曾看清的事：那中指之上，似戴着一枚沉甸甸的金戒指，镶着一颗碧绿的宝石，多半是祖母绿。

那只手开始从我身畔缓缓移过，朝向通往隔壁的那扇门而去。我重新点起油灯，跟了上去，一切恐惧俱已抛诸脑后，一心只想寻出这怪象的究竟。我隐约听见那只手之下有微弱的足音，仿佛其主人纵已隐形，却仍在近旁。我跟着它穿过相邻的那间屋子，进入再下一间，它重又停下，悬在半空，纹丝不动。它伸出一根指头，指向远处墙角——那里放着一只绣墩，或者说一只矮几。

===Original===
Impelled, I think, by some force other than my own volition, I went over and lifting the tabouret, found a small wooden box, covered with dust, beneath.

Turning about I saw that the hand had disappeared.

Taking the box with me, I returned to my room. The thing was made of very hard wood and in size was perhaps ten inches in length by eight in width and four in height. It was light, and the contents rustled when I shook it. I guessed them to be letters or papers, but having nothing to pry the box open with, I concluded to wait until morning before trying to.

===Chinese===
我如今想来，彼时乃是受了一股并非出于己意的力量所驱使：我走过去，掀起那只绣墩，底下竟有一只蒙满灰尘的小木盒。

我回过身，却见那只手已然消失不见。

我带着那只盒子回到自己屋里。那物事乃极硬的木头所制，长可十英寸，宽约八英寸，高约四英寸。分量甚轻，摇晃起来，里头的物件窸窣作响。我猜是些信件或纸张，但一时又寻不着什么可以撬开它的家什，便决计且等到天明再作理会。

===Original===
Strange as it may seem I soon fell asleep. You would naturally think that a man would not feel inclined to slumber immediately after encountering a disembodied spirit. I can give no explanation of it.

The sun was streaming through the window when I awoke, and so cheerful and matter-of-fact was the broad daylight that I wondered if the events of the night were not all a dream. The presence of the box, however, convinced me that they were not.

===Chinese===
说来也怪，不多时我竟沉沉睡去了。常人自然以为，一个人刚刚撞过无形之鬼，断无立刻便能安眠之理。个中缘由，我自己也说不分明。

我醒来时，日头正从窗棂间倾泻而入。那一片堂堂的白昼之光，明快而又切实得可以，我竟不由得怀疑昨夜种种，莫非全是一场大梦。然而那只盒子尚在，叫我笃信那并非梦境。

===Original===
Nicholson came in and appeared much surprised and a trifle discomfited to find me still in possession.

"Well," he inquired, "what happened? What did you see?"

I told him what had occurred and produced the box as proof.

===Chinese===
尼科尔森走了进来，见我居然仍安然据守于此，神情既大为惊讶，又带着几分难堪。

“那么，”他问道，“昨夜如何？你可见着什么了？”

我把经过一一相告，又取出那只盒子为证。

===Original===
An hour afterwards, Nicholson, with a short native sword and considerable profanity, was trying to pry the thing open. He finally succeeded. Within were a number of closely-written sheets of paper and some letters, most of which were addressed to Mohammed Din.

The papers were mostly in the form of memoranda and business accounts such as would be made by a merchant. They were written in execrable Urdu, hopelessly jumbled together, and though all were dated, it was no small task to sort them out. The letters were mostly regarding business affairs, but several, which were written in a very fair hand, were from a cousin of Mohammed Din's, one Ali Bagh, an Agra horse-trader. These, too, with one exception, were commonplace enough. Nicholson knitted his brows as he read it, and then handed it to me. The greater part, being of little interest, has escaped my memory, but I recollect that the last paragraph ran thus:

===Chinese===
一个钟头之后，尼科尔森操起一把本地短剑，伴着一连串的咒骂，正费劲地撬那盒子。他终于把它撬开了。盒内是数张写满蝇头小字的纸页，以及若干信件，大多都是寄给穆罕默德·丁的。

那些纸页多半是些备忘录与商号账目之类，正是一个商人所会记下的。皆用极恶劣的乌尔都文（Urdu）写就，凌乱地纠缠作一团，虽则都署了日子，要一一理清，却着实费了一番工夫。信件多涉商务，但其中有几封，出自一手颇见功力的字迹，乃穆罕默德·丁的一位表亲——一个名叫阿里·巴格（Ali Bagh）的阿格拉马贩——所寄。这几封信除去其中一封之外，也都平淡无奇。尼科尔森读那一封时，眉头越锁越紧，读罢便递给了我。那信大半并无多少意趣，我已记不分明了，只记得末后一段，其辞如下：

===Original===
"I do not understand how you came by the knowledge, nor why you wish to use it to ruin me. It is all true. If you have any love for me, forbear."

"What does that mean?" asked Nicholson. "What secret did Mohammed Din possess that he could have used to ruin his cousin?"

===Chinese===
“我不解你从何处探知此事，亦不解你何以欲借此将我毁于一旦。所言皆属事实。你若对我尚存半分情谊，便请高抬贵手。”

“此言何意？”尼科尔森问道，“穆罕默德·丁究竟握着什么秘密，竟能以此倾覆他的表亲？”

===Original===
We went through the memoranda carefully, and near the bottom found the following, dated April 21, 1881, according to our notation:

"Today I found the letters which I have long been seeking. They are ample proof of what I have long known, but have hitherto been unable to substantiate, that Ali Bagh is a counterfeiter, the chief of a large band. I have but to turn them over to the police, and he will be dragged away to jail, there to serve a term of many years. It will be good revenge—part compensation, at least, for the injuries he has done me.

===Chinese===
我们又仔细翻检那些备忘录，将近底部之处，寻得下面这一则，按我们的纪年之法，署的是 1881 年 4 月 21 日：

“今日我寻得了那些久觅不得的信件。它们足以坐实我一向所知、却始终无从证实之事：阿里·巴格是个伪造钱币之徒，乃一大团伙的首脑。我只消将它们交付官府，他便会给拿问入狱，去服上许多年的徒刑。这便是绝妙的报复——对他加诸于我的种种侵害，至少也算得几分补偿。

===Original===
"That explains Ali Bagh's letter," said Nicholson. "Mohammed Din was boastful enough to write to him, telling him that he knew of his guilt and intended to prove it."

Next were several sheets in a different hand and signed "Mallek Khan." Mallek Khan, it seemed, was a friend of Ali Bagh's, and the sheets were in the form of a letter. But being without fold, it was quite evident they had not been posted.

The communication related to certain counterfeiting schemes, and the names of a number of men implicated appeared. This, plainly, was the proof alluded to by Mohammed Din, and which he had threatened his cousin to turn over to the police.

===Chinese===
“这就解开了阿里·巴格那封信，”尼科尔森说道，“穆罕默德·丁竟自负到写信给他，告诉他自已知悉其罪状，且要将其揭发出来。”

接下来是数张字迹迥异的纸页，署名“马利克·汗（Mallek Khan）”。看来这马利克·汗乃是阿里·巴格的一个朋友，那几张纸形同一封信函；然其上并无折痕，显见未曾付邮。

信中所叙，关乎若干伪造钱币的图谋，并开列了一批被牵涉在内的人名。这分明便是穆罕默德·丁所暗指、且曾扬言要交与官府的那桩铁证。

===Original===
There was nothing else of interest save the following in Mohammed Din's hand, dated April 17th, 1881:

"Tomorrow I shall give the papers to the authorities. I have delayed too long, and was very foolish to write to Ali Bagh.

"I passed a man in the street today who bore a resemblance to my cousin.… I could not be sure.… But if he is here, then may Allah help me, for he will hesitate at nothing.…"

What followed was illegible.

===Chinese===
此外再无可观之物，唯余下面这一则，出自穆罕默德·丁的手笔，署为 1881 年 4 月 17 日：

“明日我便将那些文书交予官家。我耽搁得太久了，当初写信给阿里·巴格，实属愚蠢。

“今日我在街上与一个男人擦肩而过，那人酷似我的表亲。……我不能确信。……然则，他若当真来到此处，那么愿安拉（Allah）佑我，因为他是无所顾忌、什么也做得出来的。……”

其后所书，已模糊难辨。

===Original===
"On the night of April 21st," said Nicholson, "Mohammed Din was killed by a person or persons unknown." He paused and then went on: "This Ali Bagh is a man with whom I have had some dealings in horses, and an especially vicious crook it was that he got three hundred rupees out of me for. He has a bad reputation as a horse-dealer, and the Agra police have long been patiently seeking evidence of his implication in several bold counterfeiting schemes. Mallek Khan, one of his accomplices, was arrested, tried and sentenced to fifteen years' imprisonment, but refused to turn State's evidence on Ali Bagh. The police are convinced that Ali Bagh was as much, if not more implicated, than Mallek Khan, but they can do nothing for lack of proof. The turning over of these papers, however, as poor Mohammed Din would have done had he lived, will lead to his arrest and conviction.

"It was Ali Bagh who killed Mohammed Din, I am morally convinced, his motive, of course, being to prevent the disclosure of his guilt. Your extraordinary experience last night and the murdered man's papers point to it. Yet we can prove nothing, and your tale would be laughed at in court."

===Chinese===
“四月二十一日夜里，”尼科尔森说道，“穆罕默德·丁为人所杀，凶手不详。”他顿了一顿，又续道：“这个阿里·巴格，我曾与他做过几笔马的生意，有一回他卖我一匹，骗得格外狠，足足敲去我三百卢比。他做马贩的名声极劣，阿格拉的官府多年来一直在耐心搜集他涉嫌数起胆大包天的伪造钱币案的证据。他的同党之一马利克·汗，已经拿获受审，判了十五年监禁，却不肯转为污点证人，出庭指证阿里·巴格。官府深信阿里·巴格牵涉之深，即不甚于马利克·汗，亦不遑多让，只是苦无实证，无可奈何。然而这些文书一旦交出去——正如可怜的穆罕默德·丁倘若在世必将做的那样——便足以将他拿获定罪。

“杀死穆罕默德·丁的，正是阿里·巴格，我于此道义上确信无疑；其动机，自是为了堵住其罪行败露之口。你昨夜那番奇遇，连同这被害人的文书，皆指向此节。只是我们无从证实，而你那番话，拿到堂上，不过惹人哄笑罢了。”

===Original===
Some blank sheets remained in the bottom of the box, and my friend tilted them out as he spoke. They fluttered to the veranda and something rolled out from amongst them and lay glittering in the sunshine. It was a heavy gold ring set with an emerald—the very same that I had seen upon the apparition's finger several hours before.

A week or so later, as the result of the papers that Nicholson sent to the Agra police, accompanied by an explanatory note, one Ali Bagh, horse-trader, found himself on trial, charged with counterfeiting. It was a very short trial, his character and reputation going badly against him, and it being proven that he was the leader of the gang of which Mallek Khan was thought to be a member, he was sentenced to a somewhat longer term in jail than his accomplice.

===Chinese===
盒底尚余几张空白纸页，我朋友说话间便将它们倾倒出来。纸页纷纷飘落到游廊之上，其中有一件物事骨碌碌滚出，在阳光之下闪烁生辉。那是一枚沉甸甸的、镶着祖母绿的金戒指——正是数个钟头之前，我见那幻影指上所戴的同一只。

约莫一周之后，因尼科尔森将那些文书并附一纸说明，送交阿格拉官府，那名叫阿里·巴格的马贩，便因伪造钱币之罪，受审于堂上了。审讯极其简短，其人品与声名皆大大不利于他，又经查明他正是马利克·汗所隶属那个团伙的首脑，遂被判了一个比其同党更长的刑期，收入狱中。

## The Last Incantation / 最后的咒语

===Original===
Malygris the magician sat in the topmost room of his tower that was builded on a conical hill above the heart of Susran, capital of Poseidonis. Wrought of a dark stone mined from deep in the earth, perdurable and hard as the fabled adamant, this tower loomed above all others, and flung its shadow far on the roofs and domes of the city, even as the sinister power of Malygris had thrown its darkness on the minds of men.

===Chinese===
巫师马利格里斯（Malygris）端坐于其塔楼最顶层的内室，那塔楼筑于波塞冬尼斯（Poseidonis）首都苏斯兰（Susran）中央一座圆锥形山丘之上。塔身以自地腹深处采来的幽暗之石筑成，亘久不坏，其硬堪比传说里的金刚石；它凌驾于群塔之上，将自身的阴影远远投落于城中的屋顶与穹顶之上——恰如马利格里斯那不祥的权能，已将其幽暗投落于万民的心智之上。

===Original===
Now Malygris was old, and all the baleful might of his enchantments, all the dreadful or curious demons under his control, all the fear that he had wrought in the hearts of kings and prelates, were no longer enough to assuage the black ennui of his days. In his chair that was fashioned from the ivory of mastodons, inset with terrible cryptic runes of red tourmalines and azure crystals, he stared moodily through the one lozenge-shaped window of fulvous glass. His white eyebrows were contracted to a single line on the umber parchment of his face, and beneath them his eyes were cold and green as the ice of ancient floes; his beard, half white, half of a black with glaucous gleams, fell nearly to his knees and hid many of the writhing serpentine characters inscribed in woven silver athwart the bosom of his violet robe. About him were scattered all the appurtenances of his art; the skulls of men and monsters; phials filled with black or amber liquids, whose sacrilegious use was known to none but himself; little drums of vulture-skin, and crotali made from the bones and teeth of the cockodrill, used as an accompaniment to certain incantations. The mosaic floor was partly covered with the skins of enormous black and silver apes: and above the door there hung the head of a unicorn in which dwelt the familiar demon of Malygris, in the form of a coral viper with pale green belly and ashen mottlings. Books were piled everywhere: ancient volumes bound in serpent-skin, with verdigris-eaten clasps, that held the frightful lore of Atlantis, the pentacles that have power upon the demons of the earth and the moon, the spells that transmute or disintegrate the elements; and runes from a lost language of Hyperborea, which, when uttered aloud, were more deadly than poison or more potent than any philtre.

===Chinese===
如今马利格里斯已届垂暮，他那些法术的一切凶邪伟力、统御于他座下的一切可怖或奇诡的恶魔、他在列王与主教心中所激起的一切畏惧，都已不足纾解他岁月里那漆黑的倦怠。他坐在那张以乳齿象之牙所制的座椅中，椅上嵌着以红电气石与蔚蓝水晶所錾的、可怖而隐秘的符文，郁郁地透过那唯一一扇菱形的褐黄色玻璃窗凝望出去。他雪白的双眉紧蹙，在那张焦褐色羊皮纸般的脸庞上连成一线；眉下双眸，冰冷而碧绿，宛如太古的浮冰；他的胡须，半白，半作带海绿幽光的黑色，几乎垂及膝头，遮住了胸前那件紫袍上、以编银绣就的诸多蜿蜒蛇形字符。在他四周，散置着他这门技艺的一切器用：人与怪物的颅骨；满盛黑色或琥珀色液体的药瓶，那亵渎的用途除他之外无人知晓；秃鹫皮蒙的小鼓，以及以鳄鱼的骨与牙所制的响板，用以为某些咒语伴奏。那镶嵌拼花的地板上，局部铺着硕大无朋的黑、银二色猿猴之皮；门楣之上，悬挂着一颗独角兽的头颅，马利格里斯的使魔便栖居其间，作一条珊瑚蝰蛇之形，腹作淡绿，身布灰白斑纹。书籍堆积得到处皆是：那些以蛇皮装订、铜锈斑驳扣锁的古卷，载录着亚特兰蒂斯（Atlantis）可怖的秘学，载录着能统御大地与月亮诸恶魔的五芒星，载录着能变转或销蚀诸元素的咒言；还有取自许珀玻瑞亚（Hyperborea）一种失传语言的符文，其音若出诸口，或比鸩毒更为致命，或比任何迷情之剂更为灵验。

===Original===
But, though these things and the power they held or symbolized were the terror of the peoples and the envy, of all rival magicians, the thoughts of Malygris were dark with immitigable melancholy, and weariness filled his heart as ashes fill the hearth where a great fire has died. Immovable he sat, implacable he mused, while the sun of afternoon, declining on the city and on the sea that was beyond the city, smote with autumnal rays through the window of greenish-yellow glass, and touched his shrunken hands with its phantom gold and fired the balas-rubies of his rings till they burned like demonian eyes. But in his musings there was neither light nor fire; and turning from the grayness of the present, from the darkness that seemed to close in so imminently upon the future, he groped among the shadows of memory, even as a blind man who has lost the sun and seeks it everywhere in vain. And all the vistas of time that had been so full of gold and splendor, the days of triumph that were colored like a soaring flame, the crimson and purple of the rich imperial years of his prime, all these were chill and dim and strangely faded now, and the remembrance thereof was no more than the stirring of dead embers. Then Malygris groped backward to the years of his youth, to the misty, remote, incredible years, where, like an alien star, one memory still burned with unfailing luster﻿—the memory of the girl Nylissa whom he had loved in days ere the lust of unpermitted knowledge and necromantic dominion had ever entered his soul. He had well-nigh forgotten her for decades, in the myriad preoccupations of a life so bizarrely diversified, so replete with occult happenings and powers, with supernatural victories and perils; but now, at the mere thought of this slender and innocent child, who had loved him so dearly when he too was young and slim and guileless, and who had died of a sudden mysterious fever on the very eve of their marriage-day, the mummylike umber of his cheeks took on a phantom flush, and deep down in the icy orbs was a sparkle like the gleam of mortuary tapers. In his dreams arose the irretrievable suns of youth, and he saw the myrtle-shaded valley of Meros, and the stream Zemander, by whose ever-verdant marge he had walked at eventide with Nylissa, seeing the birth of summer stars in the heavens, the stream, and the eyes of his beloved.

===Chinese===
然而，尽管这些事物及其所持有或象征的权能，乃是万民之畏、众术士之妒，马利格里斯的心绪却因无可纾解的郁黯而阴翳，倦怠充塞着他的心，一如灰烬充塞着一场大火熄灭后的炉膛。他纹丝不动地坐着，冥顽不化地沉吟，午后之日，斜照于城池、斜照于城外的大海，以秋日的光线透过那扇绿黄色的玻璃窗射入，以它幽灵般的黄金抚上他枯缩的双手，将他指环上的巴拉斯红宝石映得灼灼，直如恶魔的双瞳。可他的沉思之中，既无光，亦无火；他转过脸去，背对眼前的灰败，背对那似乎正迫近着、行将合拢于未来之上的黑暗，在记忆的诸般暗影中摸索——恰如一个失了太阳的盲人，处处寻觅而终不可得。而那些曾经满是黄金与辉光的时光之景，那些如腾焰般染色的凯旋之日，他盛年那富丽堂皇、朱紫交织的帝王般的年华，这一切如今都寒凉、暗淡、奇异地褪了色，对它们的追忆，不过是死灰中的一点微动罢了。于是马利格里斯向后摸索，摸向他青春的岁月，摸向那些朦胧、邈远、难以置信的年月——在那里，宛如一颗异域的星辰，有一段记忆仍以不熄的光华灼灼燃烧着：那是少女妮莉莎（Nylissa）的记忆。他爱过她，其时，那对禁断之知的贪欲、那死灵法术的统治之欲，尚未曾侵入他的灵魂。数十年来他几乎已把她忘却，在那样一段荒诞地纷繁多变、充溢着秘术的异变与权能、超自然的凯旋与凶险的人生里，有无数的营营役役；可如今，单是想到这个纤秀而天真的孩子——当他亦年轻、清瘦而不设防时，曾那样深深地爱过他，却又在他们婚仪的前夜，死于一场突如其来的诡异热病——他那木乃伊般焦褐的双颊便浮起一缕幽灵似的红晕，在那冰冷的双眸深处，跃起一星如停尸之烛的光芒。在他梦一般的恍惚中，青春那不可复得之阳冉冉升起；他看见了香桃木荫蔽的梅罗斯（Meros）山谷，看见了泽曼德（Zemander）溪流——他曾于薄暮时分与妮莉莎并肩行于它那万古青翠的水湄，看夏日初升的星辰，一颗颗诞生于苍穹之上、溪流之中、他爱人的双眸之内。

===Original===
Now, addressing the demonian viper that dwelt in the head of the unicorn, Malygris spoke, with the low monotonous intonation of one who thinks aloud:

“Viper, in the years before you came to dwell with me and to make your abode in the head of the unicorn, I knew a girl who was lovely and frail as the orchids of the jungle, and who died as the orchids die﻿ … Viper, am I not Malygris, in whom is centered the mastery of all occult lore, all forbidden dominations, with dominion over the spirits of earth and sea and air, over the solar and lunar demons, over the living and the dead? If so I desire, can I not call the girl Nylissa, in the very semblance of all her youth and beauty, and bring her forth from the never-changing shadows of the cryptic tomb, to stand before me in this chamber, in the evening rays of this autumnal sun?”

===Chinese===
于是，马利格里斯向那栖居在独角兽头颅中的恶魔般的蝰蛇开口，以一个出声自语者那低沉单调的声调说道：

“蝰蛇啊，在你来与我同住、将你的居所安在独角兽的头颅之前那些年里，我曾识得一个少女，她如丛林中的兰花一般娇美纤弱，又如同兰花一般地死去……蝰蛇啊，我岂不是马利格里斯吗？一切秘术奥义的宗师、一切禁断统御之主，皆集于我之一身，统辖大地、海洋、天空的众灵，统辖日之恶魔与月之恶魔，统辖生者与死者。我若欲如此，难道不能将少女妮莉莎召来，依她全部的青春与美貌，丝毫不差地现于我前，将她从那幽秘墓穴中永不更变的暗影里唤出，叫她立于这间内室之中，立于这秋日夕阳的余光之中吗？”

===Original===
“Yes, master,” replied the viper, in a low but singularly penetrating hiss, “you are Malygris, and all sorcerous or necromantic power is yours, all incantations and spells and pentacles are known to you, It is possible, if you so desire, to summon the girl Nylissa from her abode among the dead, and to behold her again as she was ere her loveliness had known the ravening kiss of the worm.”

“Viper, is it well, is it meet, that I should summon her thus?﻿ … Will there be nothing to lose, and nothing to regret?”

The viper seemed to hesitate. Then, in a more slow and measured hiss: “It is meet for Malygris to do as he would. Who, save Malygris, can decide if a thing be well or ill?”

“In other words, you will not advise me?” the query was as much a statement as a question, and the viper vouchsafed no further utterance.

===Chinese===
“是，主人。”蝰蛇答道，发出一声低沉却格外钻心的嘶鸣，“您是马利格里斯，一切巫术与死灵法术的权能皆归您所有，一切咒语、法术与五芒星您无所不知。若您欲如此，确有可能将少女妮莉莎从她死者之中的居所召来，再度瞻见她的容颜——一如她的美貌尚未遭受那贪婪蠕虫之吻时的模样。”

“蝰蛇啊，我这样召她前来，妥当吗？合宜吗？……难道不会有所失、有所憾吗？”

那蝰蛇似是迟疑了一下。随后，以更为缓慢而审慎的嘶声道：“马利格里斯欲行何事，皆是合宜。除马利格里斯之外，谁人能裁断一事之为善为恶？”

“换句话说，你不肯给我忠告了？”这声询问与其说是疑问，不如说是断言，而那蝰蛇也不肯再赐以一言半语。

---

===Original===
Malygris brooded for awhile, with his chin on his knotted hands. Then he arose, with a long-unwonted celerity and sureness of movement that belied his wrinkles, and gathered together, from different coigns of the chamber, from ebony shelves, from caskets with locks of gold or brass or electrum, the sundry appurtenances that were needful for his magic. He drew on the floor the requisite circles, and standing within the centermost he lit the thuribles that contained the prescribed incense, and read aloud from a long narrow scroll of gray vellum the purple and vermilion runes of the ritual that summons the departed. The fumes of the censers, blue and white and violet, arose in thick clouds and speedily filled the room with ever-writhing interchanging columns, among which the sunlight disappeared and was succeeded by a wan unearthly glow, pale as the light of moons that ascend from Lethe. With preternatural slowness, with unhuman solemnity, the voice of the necromancer went on in a priest-like chant till the scroll was ended and the last echoes lessened and died out in hollow sepulchral vibrations. Then the colored vapors cleared away, as if the folds of a curtain had been drawn back. But the pale unearthly glow still filled the chamber, and between Malygris and the door where hung the unicorn’s head there stood the apparition of Nylissa, even as she had stood in the perished years, bending a little like a windblown flower, and smiling with the unmindful poignancy of youth. Fragile, pallid, and simply gowned, with anemone blossoms in her black hair, with eyes that held the newborn azure of vernal heavens, she was all that Malygris had remembered, and his sluggish heart was quickened with an old delightful fever as he looked upon her.

===Chinese===
马利格里斯沉吟了一阵，下巴搁在他那节瘤虬结的双手上。随后他站起身来，动作之迅捷笃定久已罕见，与他的皱纹全不相称；他从内室各处、从乌木架子上、从配着黄金、黄铜或琥珀金之锁的匣子里，把施法所需的种种器用一一聚拢过来。他在地板上画出必不可少的诸般圆环，立于最中央的那一圈之中，点燃了盛有所规定之香的香炉，自一卷狭长的灰色犊皮纸上，朗声诵读那召唤亡者的仪轨上紫红与朱砂的符文。香炉的烟霭——蓝的、白的、紫的——升起为浓云，须臾便以永在扭结、互相交替的烟柱充满了一室；日光在其中隐没，继之而起的是一缕幽白的、非人世所有的微光，惨淡如自忘川（Lethe）升起的诸月之光。以超乎自然的迟缓，以非人的庄重，那死灵法师的嗓音续续不断，如祭司的咏唱一般，直读到卷尽，最后的余响方渐次低微，在空洞如墓的回响中归于寂灭。随后那彩色的烟霭散去，仿佛帘幕的褶皱被人撩开。但那幽白的非世之微光仍盈满内室；在马利格里斯与悬挂着独角兽头颅的房门之间，立着妮莉莎的幽影——一如她在那些已逝的岁月中立过的模样，微微欠身，如一朵被风吹拂的花，带着青春那浑然不觉的动人之姿微笑着。她纤弱、苍白、衣裳素朴，黑发间簪着银莲花，双眸含着春日苍穹初生的蔚蓝——她正是马利格里斯所记得的一切，当他凝望她时，他那迟滞的心，因一种久违而甘美的热症，重新搏动起来。

===Original===
“Are you Nylissa?” he asked﻿—“the Nylissa whom I loved in the myrtle-shaded valley of Meros, in the golden-hearted days that have gone with all dead eons to the timeless gulf?”

“Yes, I am Nylissa,” Her voice was the simple and rippling silver of the voice that had echoed so long in his memory﻿ … But somehow, as he gazed and listened, there grew a tiny doubt﻿—a doubt no less absurd than intolerable, but nevertheless insistent: was this altogether the same Nylissa he had known? Was there not some elusive change, too subtle to he named or defined, had time and the grave not taken something away﻿—an innominable something that his magic had not wholly restored? Were the eyes as tender, was the black hair as lustrous, the form as slim and supple, as those of the girl he recalled? He could not be sure, and the growing doubt was succeeded by a leaden dismay, by a grim despondency that choked his heart as with ashes. His scrutiny became searching and exigent and cruel, and momently the phantom was less and less the perfect semblance of Nylissa, momently the lips and brow were less lovely, less subtle in their curves; the slender figure became thin, the tresses took on a common black and the neck an ordinary pallor. The soul of Malygris grew sick again with age and despair and the death of his evanescent hope. He could believe no longer in love or youth or beauty; and even the memory of these things was a dubitable mirage, a thing that might or might not have been. There was nothing left but shadow and grayness and dust, nothing but the empty dark and the cold, and a clutching weight of insufferable weariness, of immedicable anguish.

===Chinese===
“你是妮莉莎吗？”他问——“是我曾在香桃木荫蔽的梅罗斯山谷里爱过的那个妮莉莎吗？在那黄金般岁月的深处——如今那些日子已随一切死去的纪元，坠入了无时间的深渊？”

“是，我是妮莉莎。”她的声音，正是那在他记忆中回荡了许久的、清简而潺湲如银的声音……可不知怎的，当他凝视、倾听之际，一缕细小的疑虑生了开来——这疑虑既荒谬又难以忍受，却偏偏执拗不去：这当真全然是他所识的那个妮莉莎吗？难道没有某种难以捉摸的变化，太过幽微而无法命名、无法界定？岁月与坟墓，难道不曾取走某样东西——某样无可名状的东西，而他的法术未能将它尽数唤回？那双眼睛是否还如当初温柔？那头黑发是否还如当初光艳？那身姿是否还如当初纤秀柔韧，一如他所追忆的那个少女？他不能确信了，那愈长愈盛的疑虑，继之以铅一般沉重的沮丧，继之以阴沉的颓败，将他的心堵得如塞满灰烬。他的审视变得苛求、紧迫而残酷，而那幽影，时时刻刻，愈来愈不像妮莉莎完美的形似；时时刻刻，那唇、那额愈发不美，曲线愈发不那么幽微；纤秀的身形变得单薄，发缕成了寻常的黑色，颈项成了寻常的苍白。马利格里斯的灵魂，又因衰老与绝望、因他那转瞬即逝之希望的死亡，而重新病了。他再也不能相信爱情、青春或美貌；甚至连对它们的记忆，亦成了一片可疑的海市蜃楼，一件或有或无之物。所余者，唯暗影、灰败与尘埃，唯空洞的幽暗与寒冷，唯那不可忍受的倦怠、无可疗救的苦痛那攫人心魄的重压。

===Original===
In accents that were thin and quavering, like the ghost of his former voice, he pronounced the incantation that serves to dismiss a summoned phantom. The form of Nylissa melted upon the air like smoke and the lunar gleam that had surrounded her was replaced by the last rays of the sun. Malygris turned to the viper and spoke in a tone of melancholy reproof:

“Why did you not warn me?”

“Would the warning have availed?” was the counter-question. “All knowledge was yours, Malygris, excepting this one thing; and in no other way could you have learned it.”

“What thing?” queried the magician. “I have learned nothing except the vanity of wisdom, the impotence of magic, the nullity of love, and the delusiveness of memory﻿ … Tell me, why could I not recall to life the same Nylissa whom I knew, or thought I knew?”

“It was indeed Nylissa whom you summoned and saw,” replied the viper. “Your necromancy was potent up to this point; but no necromantic spell could recall for you your own lost youth or the fervent and guileless heart that loved Nylissa, or the ardent eyes that beheld her then. This, my master, was the thing that you had to learn.”

===Chinese===
他以细弱而颤抖的声调，如他昔日嗓音的幽魂一般，诵出了那用以遣退所召幽影的咒语。妮莉莎的身形如烟一般消融于空中，环绕着她的那缕月辉，被夕阳最后的余光所取代。马利格里斯转向那蝰蛇，以凄然的责备之色开口道：

“你为何不曾警告我？”

“那警告又有何用？”对方反问道，“一切知识原都是你的，马利格里斯，唯独这一桩除外；除此法之外，你再无别法可以知晓它。”

“哪一桩？”巫师诘问，“我什么也未学到，只学到了智慧的虚妄、法术的无力、爱情的落空、记忆的欺瞒……告诉我，我为何不能将我所识的、或自以为识的那个妮莉莎，原样唤回人间？”

“你所召来、所见的，确是妮莉莎。”蝰蛇答道，“您的死灵之术至此已是登峰造极；然而没有任何一道死灵法术，能为您唤回您自己逝去的青春，唤回那颗曾爱着妮莉莎的、炽烈而不设防的心，唤回当年凝望她的那双灼热的眼。我的主人，这，便是您不得不学的那一桩。”

## The Mahout / 象夫

===Original===
Arthur Merton, British resident at Jizapur, and his cousin, John Hawley, an Agra newspaper editor, who had run down into Central India for a few weeks’ shooting at Merton’s invitation, reined in their horses just outside the gates of Jizapur. The Maharaja’s elephants, a score of the largest and finest “tuskers” in Central India, were being ridden out for their daily exercise. The procession was led by Raja, the great elephant of State, who towered above the rest like a warship amongst merchantmen. He was a magnificent elephant, over twelve feet from his shoulders to the ground, and of a slightly lighter hue than the others, who were of the usual muddy gray. On the ends of his tusks gleamed golden knobs.

===Chinese===
亚瑟·默顿（Arthur Merton）本是英国驻吉扎普尔（Jizapur）的驻扎官，其堂弟约翰·霍利（John Hawley）则是阿格拉（Agra）一家报馆的编辑，应默顿之邀南下中印度，欲作数周狩猎之游。二人于吉扎普尔城门外勒马驻足。那大君（Maharaja）的象群——整整二十头，皆是中印度境内最为硕壮雄骏的“巨牙象”——正被驱策而出，作每日例行的操练。行列由那头名叫拉贾（Raja）的御象领头，它高踞群象之上，宛若一艘战舰凌驾于商船之间。它是一头气象峥嵘的巨象，自肩至地高逾十二英尺，毛色较其余那些寻常泥灰色的同类略浅一线；两根象牙之端，各顶着一只金灿灿的圆球，熠熠生光。

===Original===
“What a kingly animal!” exclaimed Hawley, as Raja passed.

As he spoke, the mahout, or driver, who had been sitting his charge like a bronze image, turned and met Hawley’s eyes. He was a man to attract attention, this mahout, as distinctive a figure among his brother mahouts as was Raja among the elephants. He was apparently very tall, and of a high-caste type, the eyes proud and fearless, the heavy beard carefully trimmed, and the face cast in a handsome, dignified mold.

===Chinese===
“好一头王者之兽！”拉贾经过时，霍利失声赞叹。

话音未落，那象夫（mahout）——亦即驭象之人——原本端坐象背之上纹然不动，恍若一尊铜铸的造像，此时却回过身来，与霍利四目相接。这象夫是个引人瞩目之人，在同行之间超然特立，一如拉贾之于象群。他看去身量颀长，眉宇间是一派高种姓的气度，双目孤傲而无惧，浓密的髯须修剪得一丝不苟，整张脸轮廓俊朗，气度端方凝重。

===Original===
Hawley gave a second exclamation as he met the mahout’s gaze and stared at the man hard. The Hindu, after an impressive glance, turned his head, and the elephant went on.

“I could swear that I have seen that man before,” said Hawley, at his cousin’s interrogatory expression. “It was near Agra, about six years ago, when I was out riding one afternoon. My horse, a nervous, high-strung Waler, bolted at the sight of an umbrella which someone had left by the roadside. It was impossible to stop him, indeed, I had all I could to keep on. Suddenly, the Hindu we have just passed, or his double, stepped out into the road and grabbed the bridle. He was carried quite a distance, but managed to keep his grip, and the Waler finally condescended to stop. After receiving my thanks with a dignified depredation of the service he had done me, the Hindu disappeared, and I have not seen him since.”

===Chinese===
与那象夫目光一接，霍利又惊呼一声，遂定睛将那人细细打量。那印度人投来一道深沉难测的目光，旋即别转头去，巨象继续前行。

“我可以发誓，那人我从前必定见过。”见堂兄一脸探询之色，霍利说道，“那是大约六年以前、阿格拉近郊的事。某日午后我外出骑马，座下是一匹沃勒马（Waler），生性敏感、神经过敏、极易受惊，因瞧见路边有人遗落了一把阳伞，便受惊狂奔起来。那马根本勒之不住，说实话，我能不摔下马背已是拼尽全力了。忽然，方才我们擦身而过的那个印度人——要么便是一个与他生得一模一样的人——步出路面，一把攥住了马缰。他连拖带拽被带出去好一程，却死死抓住不放，那沃勒马总算肯屈尊停下脚步。听我道谢之时，他神态端重，将这一桩义举视若不足挂齿；其后便飘然而逝，自此我再未见过他。”

===Original===
“It is scarcely probable, though, that this mahout is the same,” Hawhey resumed, after a pause. “My rescuer was dressed as a high-caste, and it is not conceivable that such a one would turn elephant driver.”

“I know nothing of the man,” said Merton, as they rode on into the city. “He has been Raja’s mahout ever since I came here a year ago. Of course, as you say, he cannot be the man who stopped your horse. It is merely a chance resemblance.”

===Chinese===
“不过，这象夫断不可能是同一人。”霍利顿了一顿，又道，“那位救我之人衣着是高种姓的打扮，这般身份的人，断不致沦落到去当一名驭象的象夫。”

“此人来历，我一无所知。”默顿说道，此时二人已策马入城，“自一年前我到任此地起，他便一直是拉贾的象夫。当然，诚如你所言，他决不可能是当年拦马那人。不过是形貌偶然相似罢了。”

===Original===
The next afternoon, Hawley was out riding alone. He had left the main road for a smaller one running into the jungle, intending to visit a ruined temple of which Merton had told him. Suddenly he noticed elephant tracks in the dust, exceedingly large ones, which he concluded could have been made only by Raja. A momentary curiosity as to why the elephant had been ridden off into the jungle, and also concerning the mahout, led Hawley to follow the tracks when the road branched and they took the path opposite to the one that he had intended to follow. In a few minutes he came to a spot of open ground in the thick luxuriant jungle, and reined in quickly at what he saw there.

===Chinese===
翌日下午，霍利独自骑马出游。他舍了大路，转入一条蜿蜒伸入林莽的小径，意欲去寻访一座废寺——那是默顿向他提起过的。忽而，他留意到浮土之上印着象踪，硕大无朋，他断定除拉贾之外，再无别象能留下这般蹄印。一时之间好奇心起：这巨象何以被驱入密林深处？那象夫又所为何事？于是当道路分岔、象踪折向与他原定路径相反的一方时，霍利便循着蹄印跟了下去。不数分钟，他便来到密林深处一片开阔之地，一见眼前景象，赶紧勒马。

===Original===
Raja stood in the clearing, holding something in his trunk which Hawley at first glance took to be a man, dressed in a blue and gold native attire, and with a red turban. Another look told him that it was merely a dummy﻿—some old clothes stuffed with straw. As he watched, the mahout gave a low command, reinforced with a jab behind the ear from his ankus, or goad. Raja gave an upward swing with his trunk, and released his hold on the figure, which flew skyward for at least twenty feet, and then dropped limply to earth. The mahout watched its fall with an expression of what seemed to be malevolence upon his face, though Hawley might have been mistaken as to this at the distance. He gave another command, and a jab at the elephant’s cheek﻿—a peculiar, quick thrust, at which Raja picked the dummy up and placed it on his back behind the mahout in the place usually occupied by the howdah. The Hindu directing, the figure was again seized and hurled into the air.

===Chinese===
只见拉贾伫立于林间空地之上，长鼻卷着一件物事，霍利初一眼瞧去，还当是个人——身着蓝底金线的印度衣冠，头缠一顶红色头巾。再一定睛，才看清不过是个草人——几件旧衣里塞满了稻草罢了。正注视间，那象夫低声发了一句口令，手中象钩（ankus），亦即驱象刺棒，顺势在象耳之后狠狠一戳，以壮声威。拉贾长鼻向上一抡，松开了那草人，只见它直飞上天，足有二十英尺之高，而后软塌塌地坠落在地。那象夫凝视着草人坠落，面上一副似是怨毒之色——不过隔着这般距离，霍利或许看走了眼，也未可知。他又发了一声口令，朝象颊上戳了一下——这一下戳法古怪、疾捷异常——拉贾应声将草人拾起，放于象背之上、象夫身后的位置，正是平日象舆（howdah）所在之处。那印度人指令一下，草人复又被长鼻卷起，再度抛上半空。

===Original===
Much mystified, Hawley watched several repetitions of this strange performance, but was unable to puzzle out what it meant. Finally, the mahout caught sight of him, and rode the elephant hastily away into the jungle on the opposite side of the clearing. Evidently he did not wish to be observed or questioned. Hawley continued his journey to the temple, thinking over the curious incident as he went. He did not see the mahout again that day.

===Chinese===
霍利满腹狐疑，将这诡异一幕看了好几遍反复，仍参不透其中玄机。终于，那象夫瞥见了他，忙不迭驱象急行，从空地另一侧的林莽中遁去。显然，他不愿被人撞见、不愿被人盘问。霍利继续赶往那座废寺，一路上仍在回味这桩离奇之事。那一日，他再没见过那象夫。

===Original===
He spoke of what he had seen to Merton that evening, but his cousin paid little attention to the tale, saying that no one could comprehend anything done by natives, and that it wasn’t worth while to wonder at their actions anyway. Even if one could find the explanation, it wouldn’t be worth knowing.

The scene in the jungle recurred to Hawley many times, probably because of the resemblance of the mahout to the man who had stopped his horse at Agra. But he could think of no plausible explanation of what he had seen. At last he dismissed the matter from his mind altogether.

===Chinese===
当晚，他将目击之事说与默顿听，堂兄却并不怎样上心，只道土著之所为本就无人能懂，又何苦去大惊小怪；纵使能寻出个解释，那解释亦不值一哂。

林中那一幕，在霍利脑海中反复浮现，多半是因为那象夫与阿格拉拦马之人容貌相似之故。但他始终想不出一个说得通的解释。终于，他将这事从心头彻底撇开了。

===Original===
At the time of Hawley’s visit, great preparations were being made for the marriage of the Maharaja of Jizapur, Krishna Singh, to the daughter of the neighboring sovereign. There was to be much feasting, firing of guns, and a gorgeous procession. All the Rajahs, Ranas, and Thakurs, etc., for a radius of at least hundred miles, were to be present. The spectacle, indeed, was one of the inducements that had drawn Hawley down into Central India.

===Chinese===
霍利造访之时，正值吉扎普尔大君克里希纳·辛格（Krishna Singh）迎娶邻邦君主之女，盛大婚礼的筹备正紧锣密鼓地进行。届时将有连日的盛宴、隆隆的礼炮，以及一场穷极奢华的仪仗巡行。方圆百里之内的王公、拉纳、塔库尔诸般贵族，悉数将莅临与会。说起来，这番盛景，正是吸引霍利南下中印度的由头之一。

===Original===
After two weeks of unprecedented activity and excitement in the city of Jizapur, the great day came, with incessant thunder of guns from the Maharajab’s palace during all the forenoon, as the royalty of Central Indian arrived with its hordes of picturesque, tattered, dirty retainers and soldiery. Each king or dignitary was punctiliously saluted according to his rank, which in India is determined by the number of guns that may be fired in his honor.

===Chinese===
吉扎普尔城中历经两周前所未有的喧嚣与躁动，那个大日子终于来临。整整一个上午，大君宫殿那边礼炮之声如雷不息，中印度的王公贵胄携着他们那一群群服饰绚烂、却褴褛肮脏的家臣与兵丁，陆续驾临。每一位君王或显贵莅临，皆循其品阶受礼炮致敬——在印度，人之尊卑，正系于其所能享有的礼炮之数。

===Original===
At noon a great procession, the Maharaja heading it, issued from the palace to ride out and meet the bride and her father and attendants, who were to reach Jizapur at that hour.

Hawley and Merton watched the pageant from the large and many-colored crowd that lined the roadside without the city gates. As Raja, the great State elephant emerged, with Krishna Singh in the gold-embroidered howdah, or canopied seat, on his back, a rising cloud of dust in the distance proclaimed the coming of the bride and her relatives.

===Chinese===
正午时分，一支浩浩荡荡的仪仗从宫门涌出，大君亲领其前，出城去迎接新娘及其父亲与随从——他们恰好将于此时抵达吉扎普尔。

霍利与默顿立于城门之外、夹道而立的万头攒动、五色斑斓的人海之中，观看这一场盛况。那头御象拉贾昂然步出，克里希纳·辛格端坐于其背上那金线绣花的象舆——亦即带篷的宝座——之中。恰在此时，远处腾起的一蓬尘烟，宣告着新娘及其亲眷的车驾正姗姗而来。

===Original===
Behind the Maharaja came a number of elephants, bearing the nobles and dignitaries of Jizapur, and the neighboring princes. Then emerged richly caparisoned horses, with prismatically-attired riders﻿—soldiers and attendants. Over this great glare of color and movement was the almost intolerable light of the midday Eastern sun.

===Chinese===
大君之后，跟随大队象群，载着吉扎普尔的贵胄显要与四邻的土邦王子。继而鱼贯而出的是披挂华美鞍辔的骏马，马上骑士——甲兵与扈从——衣饰如棱镜般七彩流转。这一片炫目流转的斑斓色浪之上，正笼罩着中天印度烈日那几乎令人难以忍受的毒辣光芒。

===Original===
The two Englishmen were some distance from the city gates, so that when the Maharaja’s slow, majestic procession passed them, that of the bridge was drawing near﻿—a similar one, and less gorgeous only because it was smaller.

Perhaps fifty yards separated the two when something happened to bring both processesions to a halt. Hawley, who happened at the moment to be idly watching the elephant Raja, and his driver, saw the mahout reach swiftly forward and stab the animal’s cheek with his goad, precisely as he had done on that day in the jungle when Hawley had come unexpectedly upon him. Probably no one else noticed the action, or, if they did, attached any importance to it in the excitement that followed.

===Chinese===
两名英国人立处距城门已有相当一段路，故而当大君那庄重徐缓的仪仗行经他们身前之时，新娘一方的队伍已正逼近——仪仗相类，唯因规模略小，遂稍减其繁华。

两队相距约莫五十码光景，忽有变故陡生，使两队仪仗齐齐停了下来。其时霍利恰正漫不经心地望着那头巨象拉贾及其驭者，只见那象夫倏然探身向前，以刺棒戳向象颊——手法精准，与他那日在密林中、霍利猝然撞见之时所施展的，分毫不差。大约再无第二人留意到这一举动；纵有留意的，在随后那场骚乱之中，也未曾将它放在心上。

===Original===
As he reached with his trunk for the dummy seated on his back, so Raja reached into the howdah and grasped Krishna Singh about the waist. In an instant the astonished, terror-stricken Maharaja was dangling in midair where the elephant held him poised a moment. Then, in spite of the shouts, commands, and blows of his mahout, Raja began to swing Krishna Singh to and fro, slowly at first, but with a gradually increasing speed. It was like watching a gigantic pendulum. The fascinated crowd gazed in a sudden and tense silence for what seemed to them hours, though they were really only seconds, before the elephant, with a last vicious upward impetus of his helpless victim, reeased his hold. Krishna Singh soared skyward, a blot of gold and red against the intense, stark, blazing azure of the Indian sky. To the horror-stricken onlookers he seemed to hang there for hours, before he began to fall back from the height to which the giant elephant had tossed him as one would toss a tennis-ball. Hawley turned away, unable to look any longer, and in an instant heard the hollow, lifeless thud as the body struck the ground.

===Chinese===
正如它曾以长鼻探向背上的草人那样，拉贾此番将长鼻探入象舆，一把攫住了克里希纳·辛格的腰身。刹那之间，那位惊骇欲绝、魂飞魄散的大君已被悬于半空，巨象将他定格在那里、悬了一瞬。而后，任凭那象夫怎样呼喝、下令、捶击，拉贾全然不理，开始将克里希纳·辛格来来回回地摆荡起来——起初徐缓，继而愈摆愈急。直如看着一具硕大无朋的钟摆。那群如着魔般的看客骤然陷入一种紧绷的死寂，凝目而视——于他们恍若数小时之久，实则不过数秒——直到巨象将他那无力挣扎的牺牲品作最后狠狠一向上一送，松开了长鼻。克里希纳·辛格被抛向九霄，在印度穹苍那浓烈、酷厉、如焚的湛蓝之中，化作一抹金红相间的色斑。在那群骇然失色的观者眼中，他仿佛就那样悬在天上、历历数小时之久，方才从巨象将他抛起的高度——恍若人抛网球一般抛起的高度——朝下跌落。霍利别过头去，再不忍卒睹，旋即便听见那沉闷、了无生气的“砰”然一声——身躯着地。

===Original===
The sound broke the spell of horror and amazement that had held the crowd, and a confused babble arose, interspersed with a few wails and cries. One sharp shriek came from the curtained howdah of the bride. The Maharaja’s body guard at once galloped forward and formed a ring about the body. The crowd, to whom the elephant had gone “musth,” or mad, began to retreat and disperse.

Hawley, in a few words, told his cousin of what he has seen the mahout do, and his belief that the elephant’s action had thus been incited.

The two Englishmen went to the captain of the bodyguard, who was standing by the side of the fallen Maharaja. Krishna Singh lay quite dead, his neck broken by the fall. The captain, upon being informed of what Hawley had seen, directed some of his men to go in search of the mahout, who, in the confusion, had slipped from Raja’s neck, disappearing no one knew where. Their search was unsuccessful, nor did a further one, continued for over a week, reveal any trace of the elephant driver.

===Chinese===
这一声响，打破了那镇慑住人群的恐怖与惊骇的魔咒，一时哗然四起，其间夹杂着几声哀号与惊呼。一声尖利的惨叫，发自新娘那垂着帘幕的象舆之内。大君的近卫旋即飞骑上前，围成一圈，将那具尸身护住。众人只道那象发了“musth”——亦即狂象之症——遂纷纷后退溃散。

霍利三言两语，将他所见那象夫的举动告知堂兄，并说自己深信，巨象之行实乃受其煽诱所致。

二人遂走向那位近卫统领——他正伫立于殒命的大君身侧。克里希纳·辛格已然气绝，颈骨折断于那一摔之下。那统领听罢霍利所述，当即遣手下数人前去搜捕那象夫——方才一片混乱之中，他早已从拉贾颈上滑身而下，不知所踪，无人知其去向。此番搜索一无所获；其后又续搜一周有余，仍寻不见那驭象人的半点踪迹。

===Original===
But several days afterward Hawley received a letter, bearing the Agra postmark. It was in a hand unfamiliar to him and was written in rather stiff, though perfectly correct English, such as an educated native would write. It was as follows:

To Hawley Sahib:

I am the man who stopped the Sahib’s horse near Agra one day, six years ago. Because I have seen in the Sahib’s eyes that he recognized and remembers me, I am writing this. He will then understand much that has puzzled him.

===Chinese===
然而数日之后，霍利收到一封信，盖着阿格拉的邮戳。字迹于他全然陌生，通篇英文虽略显生硬，却工整无误，一如一位受过教育的土著所书。信中写道：

致霍利·萨希布（Sahib）：

我便是六年前某日、在阿格拉近郊拦下萨希布坐骑的那个人。因我从萨希布眼中看出，他已将我认出、且记在心里，故而写下此书。读罢，许多令他困惑之事，他便都能了然了。

===Original===
My father was Krishna Singh’s half-brother. Men who bore my father an enmity, invented evidence of a plot on his part to murder Krishna Singh and seize the throne. The Maharaja, bearing him little love and being of an intensely suspicious nature, required little proof to believe this, and caused my father and several others of the family to be seized and thrown into the palace dungeons. A few days later, without trial, they were led out and executed by the “Death of the Elephant.” Perchance the Sahib has not heard of this. The manner of it is thus: The condemned man is made to kneel with his head on a block of stone, and an elephant, at a command from the driver, places one of his feet on the prisoner’s head, killing him, of course, instantly.

===Chinese===
家父本是克里希纳·辛格的异母兄弟。一些与家父怀有旧怨之人，捏造了家父意欲谋害克里希纳·辛格、篡夺君位的罪证。那大君本就对他少有情分，加之秉性多疑成性，几乎不须什么佐证便信以为真，遂将家父并族中数人一并拿下，投入宫中的地牢。数日之后，未经任何审讯，他们便被押出，以“象刑”（Death of the Elephant）处决。萨希布或许未曾听闻此刑。其法如下：受刑之人被迫跪地，将头颅置于一方石块之上，而后一头大象，凭驭者一声令下，抬起一脚踩下、踏于囚犯头上——自然，立时毙命。

===Original===
I, who was but a youth at the time, by some inadvertence was allowed to escape, and made my way to Agra, where I remained several years with distant relatives, learning, in that time, to speak and write English. I was intending to enter the service of the British Raj, when an idea of revenge on Krishna Singh, for my father’s death, suddenly sprang into full conception. I had long plotted, forming many impracticable and futile plans for vengeance, but the one that then occurred to me seemed possible, though extremely difficult. As the Sahib has seen, it proved successful.

===Chinese===
彼时我还不过是个少年，因某种疏漏，竟得以漏网逃脱，辗转逃至阿格拉，在远亲家中寄居数年，于此期间学会了英语的讲读与书写。我本欲投身英属印度政府（British Raj）谋一差事，恰在此时，为家父向克里希纳·辛格复仇之念，忽而在我心中酝酿成形、沛然不可复抑。我早有谋划，曾拟出许多全无用处、徒劳无功的复仇之策，然而彼时浮现于脑际的这一个，似乎可行——纵然难如登天。一如萨希布所亲见，它终告成功。

===Original===
I at once left Agra, disguising myself as a low-caste, and went to Burma, where I learned elephant-driving﻿—a work not easy for one who has not been trained to it from boyhood. In doing this, I sacrificed my caste. In my thirst for revenge, however, it seemed but a little thing.

After four years in the jungle I came to Jizapur and, being a skilled and fully accredited mahout, was given a position in the Maharaja’s stables. Krishna Singh never suspected my identity, for I had changed greatly in the ten years since I had fled from Jizapur, and who would have thought to find Kshatriya in the position of such a low-caste elephant-driver?

===Chinese===
我当即离开阿格拉，乔装成一个低种姓，远赴缅甸（Burma），在那里学起了驭象之术——这门营生，对非自幼习此之人而言，绝非易事。为此，我舍弃了自己的种姓。然则在我复仇的渴念之中，这不过区区小事。

在密林之中历练四年之后，我来到吉扎普尔，以一名身手娴熟、资斧俱全的象夫身份，在大君的象厩中谋得了一个差事。克里希纳·辛格始终未曾疑心我的真实身份——自当年逃离吉扎普尔，十年之间我已面目大改；再者，谁又会想到，一个刹帝利（Kshatriya）种姓之人，竟会沦为一个如此低贱的驭象之夫呢？

===Original===
Gradually, for my skill and trustworthiness, I was advanced in position, and at last was entrusted with the State elephant, Raja. This was what I had long been aiming at, for on my attaining the care of Krishna Singh’s own elephant depended the success or failure of my plan.

This position obtained, my purpose was but half-achieved. It was necessary that the elephant be trained for his part, and this, indeed, was perhaps the most difficult and dangerous pact of my work. It was not easy to avoid observation, and detection was likely to prove fatal to me and to my plan. On that day when the Sahib came upon me in the jungle, I thought my scheme doomed, and prepared to flee. But evidently no idea of the meaning of the performance in the jungle entered the Sahib’s mind.

===Chinese===
渐渐地，因我技艺精熟、行事可靠，位次屡屡升迁，终于，那头御象拉贾被交托到了我的手上。这正是我长久以来苦心谋求的——因为我能否接手照管克里希纳·辛格本人的坐骑，正是我整个谋划成败之所系。

这差事一旦到手，我的目的也不过才达成一半。那巨象尚须为它所当扮演的角色受训，而这，或许正是我整桩营生之中最为艰难凶险的一环。欲避人耳目，殊非易事；一旦败露，于我、于我的谋划，皆将是致命之祸。那一日，萨希布在林中撞见我时，我只当我这计谋已然败露，遂作好了逃遁的准备。然而显然，那林中一幕究竟有何含义，萨希布心中并无所悟。

===Original===
At last came my day of revenge, and after the Maharaja’s death I succeeded in miraculously escaping, though I had fully expected to pay for my vengeance with my own life. I am safe now﻿—not all the police and secret emissaries in India can find me.

The death that my father met has been visited upon his murderer, and the shadow of those dreadful days and of that unavenged crime has at last been lifted from my heart. I go forth content, to face life and fate calmly, and with a mind free and untroubled.

===Chinese===
复仇之日终于到来。大君既殁，我竟奇迹般地脱身得手——尽管我本已打定主意，要以自家性命来为这场复仇付账。如今我已安然无虞——纵使倾尽全印度的巡捕与密探，亦休想将我寻获。

家父所遭的那一死，已原封不动地报应到了凶手身上。那些可怖时日的阴翳、那一桩久未昭雪的罪愆，终于从我心头卸下。我此去心怀安然，将平静地直面人生与命运，了无挂碍、神清气朗。

## The Malay Krise / 马来波形短剑

===Original===
"Sahib," said the sword-dealer, "this blade, which came from far Singapore, has not its equal for sharpness in all Delhi."

He handed me the blade for inspection. It was a long krise, or Malay knife, with a curious boat-shaped hilt, and, as he had said, was very keen.

===Chinese===
"萨希布（Sahib），"兵器商说道，"这柄利刃来自遥远的新加坡，论锋锐，整个德里无可匹敌。"

他把利刃递与我验看。那是一柄长型的波形短剑（krise），亦即马来刀，剑柄形制奇古，状若小舟；果如他所言，锋锐异常。

===Original===
"I bought it of Sidi Hassen, a Singapore dealer into whose possession it came at the sale of Sultan Sujah Ali's weapons and effects after the Sultan's capture by the British. Hast heard the tale, Sahib? No? It runs thus:

===Chinese===
"我是从西迪·哈森（Sidi Hassen）手中购得此剑的，他本是新加坡的一名兵器商。苏丹·苏加·阿里（Sultan Sujah Ali）为英人所擒之后，所遗兵甲器物悉数发付变卖，此剑遂辗转落入哈森之手。萨希布，可曾听闻此中典故？未曾？其事本末如此——

===Original===
"Sujah Ali was the younger son of a great Sultan. There being little chance of his ever coming to the throne, he left his father's dominions, and becoming a pirate, set out to carve for himself a name and an empire. Though having at first but a few *prahus* (boats) and less than a hundred men, he made up this lack by his qualities of leadership, which brought him many victories, much plunder and considerable renown. His fame caused many men to join him, and his booty enabled him to build more *prahus*. Adding continually to his fleets, he soon swept the rivers of the Peninsula, and then began to venture upon the sea. In a few years his ships were held in fear and respect by every Dutch merchantman or Chinese junk whose sails loomed above the waters of the China Sea. Inland he began to overrun the dominions of the other Sultans, conquering, amongst others, that of his older brother, who had succeeded to his father's throne. Sujah Ali's fame reached far, and its shadow lay upon many peoples.

===Chinese===
"苏加·阿里原是一位大苏丹的幼子。因承袭大统之望渺茫，他便舍了父王的疆土，落草为海盗，决意凭一己之力，闯下赫赫威名、立起一方霸业。起初他麾下不过寥寥数艘帆船（prahus）与不足百名的部众，然其统御之才弥补了这等匮乏，使他屡战屡捷，劫得财货无数，博得赫赫声威。他的威名引得四方亡命之徒争相归附，劫掠所得又使他得以添造更多帆船。船队日渐壮大，未几，他便横扫马来半岛诸河川，继而胆气渐壮，竟敢驶入汪洋。不过数载，凡航经中国海面、桅帆赫然浮现的荷兰商船与中国帆船，无不对他的船队闻风丧胆、敬畏有加。在陆上，他亦开始蚕食别位苏丹的疆土，所灭者之中，竟连那位已承袭父王宝座的长兄亦未能幸免。苏加·阿里威名远播，其阴翳笼于万邦之上。

===Original===
"Then the English came to the Peninsula and built Singapore. Sujah Ali despatched ships to prey upon their vessels, many of whom he succeeded in capturing. The English sent big ships after him, bearing many heavy guns and many armed men.

"The Sultan went to meet them in person, with the greater part of his fleet. It was a disastrous day for him. When the red sun sank into the sea, fully fifty of his best *prahus*, and thousands of his men, amongst whom he mourned several of his most noted captains, lay beneath the waters. He fled inland with the shattered remnant of his fleet.

===Chinese===
"后来英人来到马来半岛，辟建新加坡。苏加·阿里遣船舰去劫掠他们的船只，其中不少竟被他截获。英人遂派出大船前来追剿，舰上重炮林立，甲兵满员。

"苏丹亲率麾下大半舰队迎战。那是他劫数临头的一日。待到那轮赤日沉入大海，足足五十艘他最精锐的帆船，连同数以千计的部众——其中尚有数员素负重名的悍将——俱已葬身波涛之下。他只得率领残破舰队的余部，逃入内陆。

===Original===
"The British resolved to crush him decisively, sent boats up the rivers, and in numerous hard-fought battles they sunk most of Sujah Ali's remaining *prahus*, and cleared land and water of the infesting pirates. The Sultan himself, however, they sought in vain. He had fled to a well-nigh inaccessible hiding-place—a small village deep in a network of creeks, swamps, and jungle-covered islands. Here he remained with a few fighting-men while the English hunted unsuccessfully for the narrow, winding entrances.

"Amina, his favorite wife, was among those who had accompanied him to this refuge. She was passionately attached to the Sultan, and, although such was his wish, had positively refused to be left behind.

===Chinese===
"英人决意将其彻底荡平，遂遣舟船溯诸河而上，历数番血战，击沉了苏加·阿里余下大半帆船，将陆上水上为患的海盗扫荡净尽。唯独苏丹本人，他们遍寻不得。他早已遁入一处几乎无路可通的藏身之地——一座隐于港汊、沼泽与林莽丛生诸岛深处的小村。他偕数名亲信武士留居于此，而英人徒然搜寻那狭窄蜿蜒的水道入口，终无所获。

"他的宠妻阿米娜（Amina），亦在随他来此避难之人的行列。她对苏丹情深意笃，纵然苏丹本欲将她留下，她也断然不肯从命。

===Original===
"There was a beautiful girl in the village, with whom Sujah Ali became infatuated. He finally married her, and she exercised so great an influence over him that Amina, who had hitherto considered herself first in her husband's estimation, grew jealous. As time passed, and she perceived more clearly how complete was his infatuation, her jealousy grew more intense and violent, and at last prompted her to leave the village secretly one night, and to go to the captain of a British vessel which had been cruising up and down the river for weeks. To this man, one Rankling Sahib, she revealed the secret of Sujah Ali's hiding place. In thus betraying him, her desire was probably more for revenge upon her rival than upon the Sultan.

===Chinese===
"村里有一名美貌的少女，苏加·阿里对她神魂颠倒。他终于娶她为妻，而此女对他的钳制之力如此之深，竟使阿米娜——此前素来自视为丈夫心头第一人——妒意渐生。日月推移，她愈发看清他痴迷之彻底，那妒火便愈发凶猛难抑，终于驱使她在某个夜晚悄然离村，前去投奔一艘数周以来在河上往返巡弋的英舰之舰长。她向此人——一位兰克林·萨希布（Rankling Sahib）——和盘托出了苏加·阿里藏身之处的秘密。她这一番出卖，所欲报复的，恐怕与其说是苏丹，毋宁说是她的情敌。

===Original===
"Rankling Sahib, guided by Amina, passed at midnight through the network of creeks and jungles. He landed his crew and entered the village. The Malays, taken completely by surprise, offered little or no resistance. Many awoke only to find themselves confronted by loaded rifles, and surrendered without opposition.

===Chinese===
"兰克林·萨希布由阿米娜引路，于午夜穿过那港汊与丛林交织的水网。他率部众登岸，闯入村中。马来人猝不及防，几乎未作多少抵抗。许多人惊醒之时，唯见上膛的步枪直指胸前，便束手就擒了。

===Original===
"Sujah Ali, who had lain awake all evening wondering as to the cause of Amina's absence, rushed out of his hut with half a score of his men, and made a futile attempt at escape. A desperate fight ensued, in which he used his krise, the same that thou seest, with deadly effect. Two of the English he stretched dead, and a third he wounded severely.

"Rankling Sahib had given orders that the Sultan be taken alive, if possible. Finally, wounded, weary and surrounded by his foes on all sides, the Sultan was made prisoner. And the next morning he was taken down the river to Singapore.

===Chinese===
"苏加·阿里彻夜未眠，正自疑心阿米娜何以不见踪影，闻变当即带着十来名部下冲出茅舍，作了一回徒劳的夺路突围。一场殊死血战随之而起，他挥起手中那柄波形短剑——便是你眼下所见的这一柄——招招致命。两名英人被他当场格毙，第三人亦被他重创。

"兰克林·萨希布早有军令：若能办到，务须生擒苏丹。终于，苏丹身负创伤、力竭神疲，又遭仇敌四面合围，终被生擒。次日清晨，他便被押解顺河而下，解往新加坡。

===Original===
"This is the krise you see on the wall."

===Chinese===
"你此刻所见、悬于壁上的那一柄，便是此剑了。"

## The Ninth Skeleton / 第九具骷髅

===Original===
It was beneath the immaculate blue of a morning in April that I set out to keep my appointment with Guenevere. We had agreed to meet on Boulder Ridge, at a spot well known to both of us, a small and circular field surrounded with pines and full of large stones, midway between her parents’ home at Newcastle and my cabin on the northeastern extremity of the Ridge, near Auburn.

===Chinese===
我启程去赴桂妮薇儿（Guenevere）之约，其时正逢四月清晨，穹苍澄碧、纤尘不染。我们相约在巨石岭（Boulder Ridge）碰头——那是一处我俩都熟稔的地方：一方为松林所环抱、满布巨石的小小圆形旷野，恰好坐落于她双亲定居的纽卡斯尔（Newcastle），与我那间在岭之东北端、邻近奥本（Auburn）的木屋之间。

===Original===
Guenevere is my fiancée. It must be explained that at the time of which I write, there was a certain amount of opposition on the part of her parents to the engagement﻿—an opposition since happily withdrawn. In fact, they had gone so far as to forbid me to call, and Guenevere and I could see each other only by stealth, and infrequently,

===Chinese===
桂妮薇儿是我的未婚妻。这里须得说明一笔：在我笔下所叙的那个时辰，她双亲对我们的婚约多少有些反对——不过这层阻力，后来总算欣然撤去了。事实上，他们当时竟至于不准我登门；我与桂妮薇儿只能偷偷相会，且聚少离多。

===Original===
The Ridge is a long and rambling moraine, heavily strewn in places with boulders, as its name implies, and with many outcroppings of black volcanic stone. Fruit-ranches cling to some of its slopes, but scarcely any of the top is under cultivation, and much of the soil, indeed, is too thin and stony to be arable. With its twisted pines, often as fantastic in form as the cypresses of the California coast, and its gnarled and stunted oaks, the landscape has a wild and quaint beauty, with more than a hint of the Japanesque in places.

===Chinese===
这道山岭是一道漫长而逶迤的冰碛（moraine），顾名思义，处处散布着漂砾，并多有黑色火山岩露出地表。果园攀附于它几处山坡之上，但岭顶几乎全无开垦；事实上，许多地方的土层既薄且石砾丛生，根本无可耕作。此处松树盘曲虬结，姿态往往奇诡如加利福尼亚海岸的丝柏，橡树亦多疙瘩节疤、矮小蜷缩，整个景致透着一种荒蛮而古拙的美，有些地方更隐隐流露出几分日本画的意趣。

===Original===
It is perhaps two miles from my cabin to the place where I was to meet Guenevere.
