Since I was born in the very shadow of Boulder Ridge, and have lived upon or near it for most of my thirty-odd years, I am familiar with every rod of its lovely and rugged extent, and, previous to that April morning, would scarcely have refrained from laughing if anyone had told me I could possibly lose my way﻿… Since then﻿—well, I assure you, I should not feel inclined to laugh﻿…

===Chinese===
从我的木屋到与桂妮薇儿相约之处，约莫两英里光景。我既生于巨石岭的浓阴之下，三十余载的岁月里又大半栖息于岭上或岭畔，故而这可爱而崎岖的山岭每一寸土地，我都了如指掌；在那个四月的清晨之前，倘若有人对我说我竟会在岭上迷路，我恐怕难免要哑然失笑……可自那以后——唉，我敢向你保证，我再笑不出来了……

===Original===
Truly, it was a morning made for the trysts of lovers. Wild bees were humming busily in the patches of clover and in the ceanothus bushes with their great masses of white flowers, whose strange and heavy perfume intoxicated the air. Most of the spring blossoms were abroad: cyclamen, yellow violet, poppy, wild hyacinth, and woodland star; and the green of the fields was opalescent with their colouring. Between the emerald of the buckeyes, the grey-green of the pines, the golden and dark and bluish greens of the oaks, I caught glimpses of the snow-white Sierras to the east, and the faint blue of the Coast Range to the west, beyond the pale and lilac levels of the Sacramento valley. Following a vague trail, I went onwards across open fields where I had to thread my way among clustering boulders.

===Chinese===
诚然，那是一个天生为恋人幽会而设的清晨。野蜂在三叶草丛间、在缀满团团白花的美洲茶（ceanothus）丛中忙碌地嗡嗡低吟，那些白花所散发的奇异而馥郁的香气，使整片空气都醺醺欲醉。春日的大部分花朵都已纷纷露面：仙客来、黄色的堇菜、罂粟、野风信子，以及林星花；原野的青绿因它们的色彩而泛出蛋白石般的柔光。在七叶树的翡翠、松树的灰绿、橡树那金黄、幽暗与泛蓝的种种绿意之间，我偶尔窥见东面雪白的内华达山脉（Sierras），以及西面、在萨克拉门托（Sacramento）谷地那苍白而带丁香紫色的平畴之外，海岸山脉（Coast Range）那一抹淡蓝。我循着一条隐约的小径，继续向前穿过开阔的旷野，须在成簇的漂砾之间穿行而过。

===Original===
My thoughts were all of Guenevere, and I looked only with a casual and desultory eye at the picturesqueness and vernal beauty that environed my path. I was halfway between my cabin and the meeting-place, when I became suddenly aware that the sunlight had darkened, and glanced up, thinking, of course, that an April cloud, appearing unobserved from beyond the horizon, had passed across the sun. Imagine, then, my surprise when I saw that the azure of the entire sky had turned to a dun and sinister brown, in the midst of which the sun was clearly visible, burning like an enormous round red ember. Then, something strange and unfamiliar in the nature of my surroundings, which I was momentarily at a loss to define, forced itself upon my attention, and my surprise became a growing consternation. I stopped and looked about me, and realized, incredible as it seemed, that I had lost my way; for the pines on either hand were not those that I had expected to see. They were more gigantic, more gnarled, than the ones I remembered; and their roots writhed in wilder and more serpentine contortions from a soil that was strangely flowerless, and where even the grass grew only in scanty tufts. There were boulders large as druidic monoliths, and the forms of some of them were such as one might see in a nightmare. Thinking, of course, that it must all be a dream, but with a sense of utter bewilderment which seldom if ever attends the absurdities and monstrosities of nightmare, I sought in vain to orient myself and to find some familiar landmark in the bizarre scene that lay before me.

===Chinese===
我满心所想的全是桂妮薇儿，对沿途那如画的风光与春日的明媚，不过走马观花地随意一瞥。走到木屋与会面之处一半路程时，我忽然察觉阳光暗淡了下来，便抬头望去，自然以为是天边不知何时飘来一片四月的云，遮住了日头。那么，请想象我当时是何等惊愕——只见整片穹苍的蔚蓝已化作一种阴沉而不祥的褐色，日头仍清晰可见，却燃烧得如同一枚巨大浑圆的赤红余烬。紧接着，周遭景物之中有某种陌生而诡异之处，我一时竟说不出所以然来，却硬生生撞进我的意识，于是惊愕逐渐化作日益加深的惶恐。我停下脚步，四下张望，终于意识到——尽管这听起来匪夷所思——我竟迷失了方向；因为两旁的松树，已不是我料想中应见的那几株。它们比我记忆中的更为高大、更为盘虬；它们的根须从一片奇异地了无花开的泥土里，以更狂乱、更如蛇形盘曲的姿态扭绞而出，那地上甚至连青草也只是稀稀落落地长成几簇。还有些漂砾大如德鲁伊的独块巨石，其中几尊的形状，简直只有在梦魇之中才能见到。我当然以为这必然只是一场梦，可那种彻底的茫然之感，却绝非梦魇中种种荒诞怪异之物所能带来的（纵然有之，也极为罕见）——我徒然地试图辨清方位，试图在这片展现在我眼前的诡异景象中，寻出某个熟悉的标识。

===Original===
A path, broader than the one I had been following, but running in what I judged to be the same direction, wound on among the trees. It was covered with a grey dust, which, as I went forward, became deeper and displayed footprints of a singular form﻿—footprints that were surely too attenuate, too fantastically slender, to be human, despite their five toe-marks. Something about them, I know not what, something in the nature of their very thinness and elongation, made me shiver. Afterwards, I wondered why I had not recognized them for what they were; but at the time, no suspicion entered my mind﻿—only a vague sense of disquietude, an indefinable trepidation.

===Chinese===
一条小径，比我先前所循的那条更宽，却依我判断朝着同一个方向，在林间蜿蜒而去。它覆着一层灰色的尘土，我愈向前行，那尘土愈深，并显出一串形貌奇特的脚印——这些脚印未免太过细窄、太过纤长得荒诞离奇了，纵然各有五个足趾的印记，也断不可能是人所留下的。它们身上有某种东西，我说不出究竟是什么——那种极度的单薄与修长——令我不寒而栗。事后回想，我不明白自己当时为何竟没认出它们究竟是什么；可在当时，我心里并无半分猜疑，只有一缕隐约的不安，一种难以名状的战栗。

===Original===
As I proceeded, the pines amid which I passed became momentarily more fantastic and more sinister in the contortions of their boughs and boles and roots. Some were like leering hags; others were obscenely crouching gargoyles; some appeared to writhe in an eternity of hellish torture; others were convulsed as with a satanic merriment. All the while, the sky continued to darken slowly, the dun and dismal brown that I had first perceived turning through almost imperceptible changes of tone to a dead funereal purple, wherein the sun smouldered like a moon that had risen from a bath of blood. The trees and the whole landscape were saturated with this macabre purple, were immersed and steeped in its unnatural gloom. Only the rocks, as I went on, grew strangely paler; and their forms were somehow suggestive of headstones, of tombs and monuments. Beside the trail, there was no longer the green of vernal grass﻿—only an earth mottled by drying algae and tiny lichens the colour of verdigris. Also there were patches of evil-looking fungi with stems of a leprous pallor and blackish heads that drooped and nodded loathsomely.

===Chinese===
我一路前行，身旁经过的松树，其枝、其干、其根的扭曲，愈来愈显得奇诡、显得凶险。有的状如挤眉弄眼的老巫婆；有的则是形容猥亵、蹲伏蜷缩的兽形石像；有的仿佛在地狱般的酷刑之中永世扭动挣扎；有的则浑身抽搐，似被一种撒旦式的狂欢所攫住。与此同时，天色持续缓缓地暗下去，我最初所见到的那种阴沉惨淡的褐色，经过几近难以察觉的色调推移，转成一种死寂的、丧礼般的紫色；日头在这片紫色里闷燃，如同一轮自血池之中升起的月亮。树木与整片景致都被这阴森可怖的紫所浸透，都被它那悖逆常理的幽暗所浸泡、所浸渍。唯有岩石，随着我走下去，竟变得异样地苍白；而它们的形状，隐隐叫人联想起墓碑，联想起坟茔与纪念碑。小径两旁，再不见春草的青绿——只有一片被渐干的藻类与铜绿一般的细小地衣所斑驳的泥土。此外还有些形容可憎的菌类，一丛丛地生长着，茎秆透出麻风病般的惨白，黑乎乎的菌盖低垂着、厌嫌地一点一点。

===Original===
The sky had now grown so dark that the whole scene took on a semi-nocturnal aspect, and made me think of a doomed world in the twilight of a dying sun. All was airless and silent; there were no birds, no insects, no sighing of the pines, no lisping of leaves: a baleful and preternatural silence, like the silence of the infinite void.

===Chinese===
此时天色已暗得令整片景象都染上了一种半似夜晚的况味，使我联想起一个命定覆亡的世界，沉沦在一轮垂死日头的暮色之中。万物都了无气息、寂然无声；没有鸟雀，没有虫豸，没有松涛的叹息，没有树叶的窸窣：那是一种邪祟的、超乎自然的寂静，一如无穷虚空之中的寂静。

===Original===
The trees became denser, then dwindled, and I came to a circular field. Here, there was no mistaking the nature of the monolithal boulders﻿—they were headstones and funeral monuments, but so enormously ancient that the letterings or figures upon them were well-nigh effaced; and the few characters that I could distinguish were not of any known language. About them there the hoariness and mystery and terror of incomputable Eld. It was hard to believe that life and death could be as old as they. The trees around them were inconceivably gnarled and bowed as with an almost equal burden of years. The sense of awful antiquity that these stones and pines all served to convey increased the oppression of my bewilderment, confirmed my disquietude. Nor was I reassured when I noticed on the soft earth about the headstones a number of those attenuate footprints of which I have already spoken. They were disposed in a fashion that was truly singular, seeming to depart from and return to the vicinity of each stone.

===Chinese===
树木渐趋茂密，继而又稀疏起来，我终于来到一方圆形的旷野。在这里，那些独石般的漂砾究竟是什么，便再无疑问了——它们乃是墓碑与丧葬的纪念碑，只是古老得太过惊人，上面的刻字或图形几乎已被磨灭殆尽；而我勉强辨认出的寥寥几个字符，又绝非任何已知的语言。它们周身弥漫着一种不可计量的洪荒（Eld）所特有的苍古、神秘与恐怖。简直叫人难以相信，生与死竟能古老至此。环绕它们的树木，则盘虬得难以想象，佝偻低垂，仿佛也扛着与之几乎相等的岁月之重。这些石头与松树所共同传达出的那种可怖的苍古之感，加重了我惶乱之中的窒息，坐实了我的不安。而当我注意到墓碑四周松软的泥土上那一串串我已说过的瘦长脚印时，我心头的疑惧也并未稍减。它们的排列方式实在奇特，似是从每块石头近旁离去、又返回每块石头近旁。

===Original===
Now, for the first time, I heard a sound other than the sound of my own footfalls in the silence of this macabre scene. Behind me, among the trees, there was a faint and evil rattling. I turned and listened; there was something in these sounds that served to complete the demoralization of my unstrung nerves; and monstrous fears, abominable fancies, trooped like the horde of a witches’ sabbat through my brain.

===Chinese===
就在此时，在这片阴森景象的死寂之中，我头一回听见了除自己足音之外的声响。在我身后，林间，传来一阵若有若无、邪气森森的磕碰之声。我回转身去凝神倾听；这些声音里有某种东西，将我那早已紧绷欲断的神经彻底击溃；种种畸形的恐惧、种种可憎的臆想，如巫魔夜会（witches' sabbat）上蜂拥的群魔一般，成群结队地涌入我的脑中。

===Original===
The reality that I was now to confront was no less monstrous! There was a whitish glimmering in the shadow of the trees, and a human skeleton, bearing in its arms the skeleton of an infant, emerged and came towards me! Intent as on some ulterior cryptic purpose, some charnel errand not to be surmised by the living, it went by with a tranquil pace, an effortless and gliding tread in which, despite my terror and stupefaction, I perceived a certain horrible and feminine grace. I followed the apparition with my eyes as it passed among the monuments without pausing and vanished in the darkness of the pines on the opposite side of the field. No sooner had it gone, than a second, also bearing in its arms an infant skeleton, appeared, and passed before me in the same direction and with the same abominable and loathsome grace of movement.

===Chinese===
我此刻所要面对的实情，同样骇人听闻！树影之中隐隐泛着一抹惨白的微光，一具人的骷髅，怀中抱着一具婴孩的骷髅，自阴影中现身，朝我走来！它神态凝定，仿佛专注于某种别有所图、晦暗难明的目的，专注于某种活人所无从揣度的骸骨堂差使，以从容的步调从我面前经过——那步伐轻巧而不费力，恍如滑行；尽管我惊恐万状、目瞪口呆，却仍从中辨出一种可怖的、属于女性的优雅。我目送这幽灵般的身影穿过一座座墓碑而不作片刻停留，隐没在旷野对面的松林深处。它前脚刚走，第二具骷髅便已现身，怀中同样抱着一具婴孩的骷髅，从我面前朝同一方向走过，举手投足之间，是同样的可憎、同样的令人作呕的优雅。

===Original===
A horror that was more than horror, a fear that was beyond fear, petrified all my faculties, and I felt as if I were weighted down by some ineluctable and insupportable burden of nightmare, Before me, skeleton after skeleton, each precisely like the last, with the same macabre lightness and ease of motion, each carrying its pitiful infant, emerged from the shadow of the ancient pines and followed where the first had disappeared, intent as on the same cryptic errand. One by one they came, till I had counted eight! Now I knew the origin of the bizarre footprints whose attenuation had disturbed and troubled me.

===Chinese===
一种甚于恐惧的恐惧，一种凌驾于惊骇之上的惊骇，将我全部的神志都化作了顽石；我只觉得自己仿佛被某种无可逃避、不堪承受的梦魇重负所压伏。在我面前，一具又一具骷髅，每一具都与前一具毫无二致，以同样阴森可怖的轻盈、同样从容不迫的步态，每一具都抱着它那可怜的婴孩，自古松的阴影中现身，循着第一具消失的方向鱼贯而去，神态凝定，仿佛专注于同一桩晦暗难明的差使。它们一个接一个地走来，直至我数出了八个！直到这时，我才明白那些令我心神不宁的怪异脚印，究竟从何而来。

===Original===
When the eighth skeleton had passed from sight, my eyes were drawn as by some irresistible impulsion to one of the nearer headstones, beside which I was amazed to perceive what I had not noticed before: a freshly opened grave, gaping darkly in the soft soil. Then, at my elbow, I heard a low rattling, and the fingers of a fleshless hand plucked lightly at my sleeve. A skeleton was beside me, differing only from the others through the fact that it bore no infant in its arms. With a lipless and ingratiating leer, it plucked again at my sleeve, as if to draw me towards the open grave, and its teeth clicked as if it were trying to speak. My senses and my brain, aswirl with vertiginous terror, could endure no more: I seemed to fall and fall through deeps of infinite eddying blackness with the clutching terror of those fingers upon my arm, till consciousness was left behind in my descent.

===Chinese===
待到第八具骷髅也走出视野，我的目光被某种不可抗拒的冲动所牵引，落到了近旁的一块墓碑上；令我骇然的是，在它旁边，我竟看见一桩先前未曾留意的事物：一座新掘开的坟茔，在松软的泥土里黑沉沉地洞张着口。紧接着，就在我肘畔，我听见一阵低低的磕碰声，一只无肉之手的手指，轻轻地扯了扯我的衣袖。一具骷髅立在我身旁，与其余诸具唯一的不同，便是它怀中并未抱着婴孩。它以一种没有嘴唇的、讨好献媚的狞笑，又扯了扯我的衣袖，似要把我拽向那座敞开的坟茔，它的牙齿嗒嗒作响，仿佛正竭力想要开口说话。我的感官与头脑，已被令人眩晕的恐惧搅得团团乱转，再也承受不住：我仿佛不断地坠落、坠落，穿过无穷回旋的漆黑深渊，而那只手手指的紧攥仍死死扣在我的臂上——直坠到我把意识都抛在了身后。

===Original===
When I came to, Guenevere was holding me by the arm, concern and puzzlement upon her sweet oval face, and I was standing among the boulders of the field appointed for our rendezvous.

“What on earth is the matter with you, Herbert?” she queried anxiously. “Are you ill? You were standing here in a daze when I came, and didn’t seem to hear or see me when I spoke to you. And I really thought you were going to faint when I touched your arm.”

===Chinese===
待我苏醒过来，桂妮薇儿正挽着我的手臂，那张甜美的鹅蛋脸上满是关切与困惑；而我，正站在我们约定相会的那片旷野的漂砾之间。

“你到底怎么了，赫伯特（Herbert）？”她焦急地探问道。“你是病了吗？我来的时候，你就这样恍恍惚惚地站在这里，我跟你说话，你仿佛既听不见、也看不见我。我碰到你手臂的时候，我真以为你就要晕过去了。”

## The Phantoms of the Fire / 火之幻影

===Original===
It was late summer, and the Georgetown road was deep with dust, which had settled like a dun pall on the bordering chaparral and pines. Since he had walked all the way from Auburn without securing a single lift, the man who was trudging along the road with the broiling afternoon sun on his back was hardly less dusty than the trees. He paused now and then to mop his face with a discoloured handkerchief, or to peer rather wistfully at the occasional cars which passed him without offering to stop. His clothing, though not actually ragged, was old and worn, and had the indescribable shapelessness of clothing that has been slept in. He was very thin, stoop-shouldered, and discouraged-looking; his general aspect was almost that of a professional tramp; and the people of the countryside were suspicious of tramps.

===Chinese===
时值夏末，乔治敦（Georgetown）那条路上积了厚厚的尘土，仿佛一层暗褐色的棺衣，覆盖在路旁的灌木丛与松林之上。他从奥本（Auburn）一路走来，不曾搭上一程便车，此刻正顶着灼热得近乎烤人的午后烈日，在道上踽踽跋涉，满身尘灰几乎不比那些树木少半分。他不时停下脚步，用一块褪了色的手帕揩脸，又或带着几分希冀，望一望偶尔驶过的汽车——可那些车没有一辆肯停下来载他。他的衣裳虽还不至于破烂，却已陈旧磨损，带着那种说不清道不明、被睡得走了样的松垮模样。他身形极瘦，双肩微驼，一脸落魄沮丧的神气；通身上下的派头，几乎与一个职业流浪汉无异；而乡间的人家，对流浪汉向来是满怀戒备的。

===Original===
"Well, I guess I’ll have t’ walk all the way," he said to himself, whining a little even in his thoughts. "But it ain’t much further now.﻿… Gosh, but things is hot an’ dry." He looked about him at the familiar landscape of parched grass, brushwood and yellow pines with an appraising eye. "Wonder there ain’t been more fires﻿—there alluz is at this time o’ year."

===Chinese===
"唉，看来我只好一路走到底喽，"他自言自语，连在心里嘀咕时都带着几分抱怨的腔调。"不过也快到了吧。……老天，这天真热得干巴巴的。"他以一种估量的目光，环顾四周那片熟悉的、焦枯的野草、灌木丛与黄松。"奇了，倒没出更多的火灾——往年这时候，可总是有的。"

===Original===
The man was Jonas McGillicuddy, and he was on his way home after a somewhat prolonged absence. His return was unannounced, and would prove as unexpected to his wife and three children as his departure had been. Tired of trying to extort a living from a small vineyard and pear-orchard of rocky El Dorado land, and tired also of the perennial nagging of his frail, sensitive-nerved and sorely disappointed wife, Jonas had left abruptly, three years before, after a quarrel of more than customary bitterness and acerbity with his helpmate. Since then, he had heard nothing from his family, for the good and sufficient reason that he had not sought to communicate with them. His various attempts to earn a livelihood had proved scarcely more successful than the fruit-ranching venture, and he had drifted aimlessly and ineffectually from place to place, from situation to situation﻿—a forlorn and increasingly desperate figure. For a man of such shifting, unstable temperament, when all else had failed him, and he had wearied of the hopeless struggle, it was not unnatural to think of returning. Time had softened his memory of his wife’s undependable temper, of her shrewish outbursts; but he had not forgotten her motherly ways when she was in a more tractable humour, nor her excellent cooking.

===Chinese===
这人正是乔纳斯·麦吉利卡迪（Jonas McGillicuddy），离家相当一段时日之后，如今正在归途之上。他的归来并不曾预先告知，必将令他的妻子和三个孩子始料不及——一如当年的不告而别。乔纳斯早已厌倦了从那片岩石嶙峋的埃尔多拉多（El Dorado）土地上、一小片葡萄园与梨园里勉强榨取生计，也同样厌倦了他那体弱多病、神经敏感、满心失望的妻子那常年不休的絮叨。三年之前，在与他的妻子大吵了一场——其刻毒尖酸远胜于往日寻常的口角——之后，他便骤然弃家而去。自那以后，他没有得到家中半点消息，缘由既简单又充分：他压根不曾设法与他们联系。他谋生的种种尝试，结果并不比当初经营果园强得了多少；他漫无目的地、徒劳无功地从一个地方漂泊到另一个地方，从一份差事漂泊到另一份差事——一个凄惶落寞、日益走投无路的身影。像他这样一个性情游移、心性不定的人，当万事皆已落空、他对那无望的挣扎也已心生厌倦之时，动起回家的念头，原也算不得反常。时光已经冲淡了他对妻子那阴晴不定的脾气、对她那些泼妇般发作的记忆；可他并未忘却她心境较为温顺时所流露的慈母情态，更不曾忘却她那一手好厨艺。

===Original===
Now, with empty pockets, since his last money had sufficed merely to pay his train-fare to Sacramento, Jonas was nearing the hills in which lay his forest-surrounded ranch beyond Georgetown. The country through which he tramped was sparsely peopled, and there were great stretches of softly rolling hills and low valleys that had not known the touch of cultivation. The ranches were often quite isolated. Beyond all, in the hazy blue of the distance, were the vague and spectral snows of the Sierras.

===Chinese===
如今，他囊空如洗——身上最后那点钱仅够付到萨克拉门托（Sacramento）的火车票——而乔纳斯已渐渐行近那片山岭，他那四周环林的农庄便坐落在乔治敦以远的那一带山间。他徒步走过的这片乡野人烟稀少，大片大片柔和起伏的丘陵与低谷，从未沾染过开垦的痕迹。一座座农庄往往彼此孤悬，相隔甚远。而在这一切之外，在那朦胧幽蓝的远方，则是内华达山脉（Sierras）那影影绰绰、如幻似魅的积雪。

===Original===
"Gosh, but one of Matilda’s pear pies’ll taste good," thought the wanderer. His mouth began to water. He was not reflective enough, however, to wonder just what his reception would be, beyond an easy surmise that Matilda might give him a terrific scolding for his absence. "But mebbe she’ll be mighty glad t’see me, after all," he consoled himself. Then he tried to picture his children, the five-year-old boy and the girl-babies of three and two respectively whom he had last seen.

"Guess they’ll have forgotten they had a papa," he mused. The afternoon had been utterly still and airless, with a sultry brooding in its silence. Now, from the northeast, along the road he was travelling, there came a gust of wind, and with it the unmistakable acrid odour of burnt grass and trees.

===Chinese===
"老天，玛蒂尔达（Matilda）做的一只梨馅饼，那滋味该有多美啊，"这浪迹归乡的人心想。他嘴里开始泛起口水。然而他这人并不善于深思，除了轻描淡写地揣测玛蒂尔达或许会因他的离家而狠狠数落他一顿之外，也并未细想自己究竟会受到怎样的迎接。"不过嘛，说不定她到底还是会很高兴见到我的，"他这样宽慰自己。接着他竭力在脑中描摹他的几个孩子——那个五岁的男孩，以及他最后见到时分别是三岁和两岁的两个女婴。

"怕是早就忘了他们还有个爹喽，"他暗自沉吟。整个下午天地间纹丝不动，闷得没有一丝风，那死寂之中酝酿着一层沉闷的郁热。忽然，从东北方——沿着他正走着的这条路——刮来一阵风，随之而来的，是那绝不会错认的、焦草与烧树刺鼻的苦辛气味。

===Original===
"Hell, there has been a fire after all," muttered Jonas, with an uneasy start. He peered anxiously ahead, but could see no smoke above the dun and grey-green hills. "Guess it’s all out now, anyway."

===Chinese===
"该死，到底还是走了火，"乔纳斯嘟囔了一句，不安地一激灵。他焦急地朝前方张望，却看不见那暗褐与灰绿相间的山岭上方有半缕烟。"不过我想，眼下总该灭了吧。"

===Original===
He came to the top of the low slope he was climbing, and saw before him the burnt area, which lay on both sides of the road and was of indeterminable extent. The brown foliage of heatseared oaks and the black skeletons of bushes and pines were everywhere. A few fallen logs and old stumps were still smoking a little, as is their wont for days after the extinguishment of a forest fire. It was a scene of complete and irremediable desolation.

===Chinese===
他登上了正在攀爬的那道低坡坡顶，眼前便赫然现出那片火烧过的地带——它横亘在道路两侧，其广袤无从估量。触目所及，尽是被烈焰灼焦的橡树那焦褐的枝叶，尽是灌木与松树漆黑的骸架。几段倒伏的残木与几个老树桩仍在微微冒烟，一如林火熄灭之后数日间它们惯有的情形。这是一幅彻彻底底、无可挽回的荒败景象。

===Original===
Jonas hurried on, with a sense of growing panic, for he was now little more than a mile from his own property. He thought of the yellow pines that stood so close and tall about his cabin﻿—the pines which he had wished to fell, but had spared at the earnest solicitation of the nature-loving Matilda.

"They’re so pretty, Jonas," she had said, pleadingly. "I just can’t see them go."

"Hope the fire didn’t get into them pines," thought Jonas now. "Gosh, but I wish I’d cut ’em down when I wuz plannin’ to. It would have been a lot safer; and I’d have had the money for the wood, too."

===Chinese===
乔纳斯加快了脚步，心头渐生一阵恐慌，因他此刻离自家的地界已不到一英里之遥。他想起那些紧紧围立、高耸于他木屋四周的黄松——那些他本欲砍倒、却因酷爱自然的玛蒂尔达苦苦相劝而手下留情的松树。

"它们多好看啊，乔纳斯，"当年她曾这样央求着说。"我实在舍不得看着它们倒下。"

"但愿那火没烧进那些松林里去，"乔纳斯此刻心想。"老天，我当初盘算着砍的时候真该把它们全砍了的。那样子要安全得多；我还能把那批木头卖几个钱呢。"

===Original===
The road was strewn in places with ashen leaves, with the charcoal of fallen brands, and several trees had crashed across it, but had now been removed to permit the passage of traffic. It was hotter than ever, in this charred and blackened waste, for the brief gust of wind had fallen. The dust on Jonas’ cheeks was runnelled with sweat which he no longer paused to wipe away. Irresponsible as he was, a strange gravity had come over the wastrel, and he felt an ever-deepening premonition of calamity,

===Chinese===
路上东一处西一处地散落着灰烬般的枯叶、散落着烧落的火炭所成的木炭，还有好几棵树曾轰然倒下横亘路面，如今已被挪开以通行人车辆。在这片焦黑的废墟之中，天热得比先前更甚，因为方才那阵短暂的风已经息了。乔纳斯两颊的尘土被汗水冲出一道道沟痕，他却已不再停下脚步去揩拭。他这人本是个没心没肺的浪荡子，此刻却笼罩上一层异样的凝重，心头那大祸将至的预感，愈发深沉地压迫着他。

===Original===
He came at last to the little byroad which turned off to his ranch from the Georgetown highway. Here, he found with a sinking heart, the fire had also been, and had left nothing but devastation. In spite of his fatigue, he almost ran, with long, shambling steps, and rounding a turn in the byroad, saw that the fire had stopped at the very verge of his own property. The hillside orchard of stunted pear trees, the straggling vines of Mission and Muscat grapes, were quite as he remembered them; and beyond, in the grove of yellow pines, he could see the wreathing smoke that arose from the chimney of his cabin. Panting heavily, he paused, with a sense of relief and thanksgiving as poignant as anything of which his dulled heart was capable.

===Chinese===
他终于来到了那条由乔治敦公路岔向自家农庄的小道。在这里，他心如坠铅地发现，大火也曾来过，留下的唯有一片满目疮痍。他顾不得满身疲惫，几乎是跑了起来，迈着拖沓的大步，绕过小路的一道弯角，却看见那火恰恰停在了自家地界的边缘上。山坡上那片矮小的梨树果园，那几架参差蔓生的弥赛（Mission）与麝香（Muscat）葡萄藤，一如他记忆中的模样；再往里去，在那片黄松林中，他能看见自家木屋烟囱里袅袅升起的炊烟。他气喘如牛地停住脚，心头涌起一阵如释重负与感恩戴德——这已是他那早已钝化的心灵所能感受到的、最为深切的一种情绪了。

===Original===
The sun had almost touched the horizon, as he climbed the winding road through the orchard and entered the grove above. Aisles of light perceptibly tinged with gold lay between the elongated shadows. Even to the sodden, insensitive Jonas, the beauty of the woodland scene, the magic of the sunset, the high, solemn, dark-green pines and the rich glow sifting among them on manzanita-bushes and beds of brown needles, were not without their charm. He drew a long breath, inhaling the clean balsams that the hot sun had drawn from the forest, and feeling as he did so a vague pleasure.

===Chinese===
当他沿着蜿蜒的道路穿过果园、步入上方那片松林时，太阳已几乎触到了地平线。一道道明显染上了金黄的光带，横陈在那些被拉长的阴影之间。即便是乔纳斯这样一个麻木迟钝的人，这林间景致之美、这落日的奇光异彩、这些高耸庄严的墨绿松树，以及那透过枝柯筛落在熊果木（manzanita）灌木丛与褐色松针积成的地床上的浓郁辉光，于他也并非全无动人之处。他长长地吸了一口气，吸进那烈日从林间蒸腾出的清冽的脂香，如此做着时，体味到一种隐隐的愉悦。

===Original===
Now he could see his cabin, a long, four-roomed shack of plain, unpainted boards and weather-darkened shingles. A woman in calico was standing in front of the steps. Two little girls were beside her, and he wondered as to the whereabouts of the boy, who had been a fragile youngster, always ailing and fretful. "Mebbe Bill is sick agin," mused Jonas. He was very glad to be home, but he felt a little doubtful, a trifle tremulous, as to the greeting he would receive from Matilda.

===Chinese===
如今他能看见自家的木屋了——一间长长的、四居室的棚屋，用的是素净未漆的木板和被风雨沤黑的墙板。一个身着印花布衣裙的女人正站在台阶前。两个小女孩依偎在她身旁，他不禁纳闷起那个男孩的去向——那孩子一向身子单薄，总是病病歪歪、焦躁爱哭的。"莫不是比尔（Bill）又病了，"乔纳斯暗想。他很高兴终于到家，可对于玛蒂尔达会给他怎样的迎接，心里却又有些没底，带着几分发虚的忐忑。

===Original===
The woman looked up as he approached, shading her eyes with her hand from the last rays of the sun, which fell horizontally through the wood. He could see her apron, which was quite clean, as always, though worn and faded from many washings, like her dress, She did not seem to perceive him, but was apparently staring with great intentness at something among the trees. The children also stared, and huddled closer to her, clinging to the hem of her gown.

===Chinese===
他走近时，那女人抬起头来，用手遮在眉上挡住最后一缕阳光——那光正横斜斜地穿过林子照来。他看得见她的围裙，一如既往地干干净净，虽已因洗过太多次而磨损褪色，一如她的衣裳。她似乎并没有察觉到他，而是显然正全神贯注地盯着林间某处看。孩子们也直盯着那边，更紧地挤到她身边，紧紧攥着她衣袍的下摆。

===Original===
Jonas tried to call out: "Hello, Matilda," but his throat was so dry and dusty that the words were no more than a hoarse whisper. He started to clear his throat, but the simple act was never finished, for at that moment, the whole scene before him, the trees, the cabin, the woman and the children, were lost in a roaring sheet of ruddy flame that seemed to come from all sides at once and blot out the entire world and the very sky as it towered full-grown in what could have been no more than the fraction of a second. A blast of intolerable heat, fierce as the breath of a thousand furnaces, blew in Jonas’ face and swept him backwards like a hurricane. The mighty roaring pounded in his ears like a sea, and was mingled with human screams, as he went down into pitch-black gulfs of unconsciousness.

===Chinese===
乔纳斯想要喊一声："喂，玛蒂尔达，"可他的喉咙又干又涩又满是尘土，话到嘴边不过成了一声沙哑的低语。他清了清嗓子，可这个简单的动作却再也没能做完——因为就在那一刻，他眼前整个景象，那些树木、那间木屋、那女人和孩子们，统统没入了一片咆哮的、赤红的烈焰之中；那火焰仿佛从四面八方同时涌来，在不过一瞬的须臾之内便拔地而起、直冲云霄，将整个世界、连整片天空都一并吞没。一股令人无法忍受的灼热，猛烈得如同千座熔炉同时吐出的气息，扑在乔纳斯脸上，又如飓风一般将他向后掀倒。那震天动地的轰鸣在他耳畔如大海般擂响，其中还夹杂着人的惨叫，他便这样坠入了漆黑一片的、不省人事的深渊。

===Original===
It was day when Jonas awoke, but he was too confused for a few instants to realize that the light was slanting through the treetops from a different direction, or that there was more of it than seemed normal in an evergreen forest. When his wits returned sufficiently to permit the comprehension of the fact that it was morning, he began to notice other things that were equally singular. He found that he was lying on his back among burnt needles, and above him towered the dark boles of fire-swept trees with the pitiful stumps of their cauterized branches. Darkly, indistinctly, in a sort of dull astonishment, he began to remember the events of the previous day, his return at sunset to the cabin, his glimpse of Matilda and the two children, and the all-engulfing sheet of flame. He looked instinctively at his clothes, with the feeling that he must have been badly burnt; but there was no trace of fire on his raiment, and the black ashes about him were cold. Nor, when he reared himself on his elbow and peered around, was there the faintest thread of smoke to indicate a recent conflagration.

===Chinese===
乔纳斯醒来时已是白天，可起初有好一阵子他神志混乱，竟没意识到光线正从另一个方向斜斜穿过树梢，也没留意到这光线对于一个常青松林而言未免太多了些。待他的神志稍稍清醒，足以明白此刻乃是清晨，他这才开始注意到其余几桩同样古怪的事。他发现自己正仰面躺在烧焦的松针之间，头顶上方矗立着那些被烈火席卷过的树木漆黑的树干，上面只剩些被烧焦断落的、可怜的枝桠残桩。他在一种迟钝的惊愕之中，昏昏沉沉、影影绰绰地，开始忆起昨日种种——日落时分回到木屋、瞥见玛蒂尔达和两个孩子、以及那吞没一切的火幕。他下意识地去看自己的衣裳，心想自己必定已被烧得不成样子；可他的衣物上竟无半点火烧的痕迹，身旁那些黑色的灰烬也是冷的。待他用胳膊肘撑起身子、四下张望时，更找不出一缕最细微的轻烟，足以表明此处刚刚有过一场大火。

===Original===
He arose and stepped towards the place where the cabin had stood. It was a heap of ashes, from which protruded the ends of charred beams.

"My God!" muttered Jonas. He felt utterly dazed, and his thoughts refused to align themselves, failing to form any sort of intelligible order.

===Chinese===
他站起身，朝木屋原先矗立之处走去。那里只剩一堆灰烬，几根烧焦的房梁的端头从灰堆里翘了出来。

"我的天！"乔纳斯喃喃道。他只觉天旋地转，满脑子思绪乱作一团，怎么也理不出半点清楚的条理。

===Original===
As Jonas spoke, a man arose from where he had been stooping behind the wreckage of the cabin, furtively dropping some object which he held in his hands. Seeing Jonas, the man came forward hastily. He was a gaunt individual in dirty overalls, with the profile and the general air of a somewhat elderly and dilapidated buzzard. Jonas recognized him as Samuel Slocum, one of his neighbours.

===Chinese===
乔纳斯正说着，一个男人从木屋残骸后面、他方才弯腰俯身的地方站了起来，鬼鬼祟祟地丢下了手中攥着的什么东西。看见乔纳斯，那人急急迎上前来。他是个瘦骨嶙峋的家伙，穿着肮脏的工装裤，侧影和通身的气派，活像一只上了年纪、羽毛破败的秃鹫。乔纳斯认出他来——这是他的一个邻居，塞缪尔·斯洛克姆（Samuel Slocum）。

===Original===
"Wal, Jonas McGillicuddy, so you’ve come back," exclaimed this individual in raucous tones of unfeigned surprise. "Ye’re a little too late, though," he went on, without pausing to let Jonas speak. "Everythin’ burnt up clean, four days ago."

===Chinese===
"嚯，乔纳斯·麦吉利卡迪，你到底是回来啦，"这人嚷道，粗嘎的嗓音里满是毫不作假的惊讶。"不过你来晚了一步，"他紧接着又说，全不给乔纳斯插嘴的工夫。"四天前，啥都烧得干干净净了。"

===Original===
"But the cabin wuz here las’ night," stammered Jonas. "I came through the woods ’bout sunset, an’ I saw Matilda an’ the children in front o’ the steps, jus’ as plain as I see you. Then everythin’ seemed to go up in a burst o’ flame, an’ I didn’t know nothin’ till I woke up jus’ now."

===Chinese===
"可昨晚那木屋还在啊，"乔纳斯结结巴巴地说。"我是日落前后穿过林子来的，我亲眼看见玛蒂尔达跟孩子们在台阶前头，看得就跟瞧见你一样清清楚楚。后来就好像一切轰地一下全着了火，我就啥也不知道了，一直到这会儿才醒。"

===Original===
"Ye’re crazy, Jonas," assured the neighbour. "Them weren’t no cabin here las’ night, an’ no Matildy an’ no children, neither. They wuz all burnt up, along with the rest o’ the countery hereabouts. We heerd yer wife an’ babies a-screamin’, but the fire wuzall aroun’ before ye could say Jack Robinson, an’ the trees fell across yer road, an’ no one could git in an’ no one could git out.﻿… I alluz told ye, Jonas, t’ cut them yeller pines down."

===Chinese===
"你疯魔了吧，乔纳斯，"那邻居一口咬定。"昨晚哪儿还有啥木屋，也没啥玛蒂尔达，更没啥孩子。早都烧没了，跟这一带别处一道烧没了。我们亲耳听见你媳妇跟娃娃们尖声惨叫，可那火快得你连一句话都来不及说便四面合围，树木横七竖八地倒在你那条道上，谁也进不来，谁也出不去。……乔纳斯，我可一直跟你说，把那些黄松砍了吧。"

===Original===
"My folks wuz all burnt up?" faltered Jonas.

"Wal, yer little boy died a year ago, so they wuz jus’ Matildy an’ the two gals."

===Chinese===
"我一家老小全烧死了？"乔纳斯颤着声问。

"唉，你家那个小男孩一年前就没了，所以当时就只有玛蒂尔达跟两个闺女在。"

## The Raja and the Tiger / 王公与猛虎

===Original===
There was more than one reason why Bently did not view his appointment as British Resident at Shaitanabad with enthusiasm. The climate was reported to be particularly hot even for India, the population largely composed of snakes, tigers, and wild boars, and the attitude of the natives from the Raja down unfriendly. The last Resident had died of sunstroke, so it was said, and the one before him departed suddenly for an unknown destination without taking the trouble to apply for leave of absence. But as somebody had to occupy the position, Bently went to Shaitanabad; from the nearest railway station one hundred miles by camel and bullock cart over parched hills and sandy desert.

===Chinese===
本特利（Bently）之所以对出任谢塔纳巴德（Shaitanabad）的英国驻扎官（British Resident）一职毫无热忱，原因不止一端。据说那地方气候即便以印度而论也灼热得格外难耐，居民则大抵由蛇、虎与野猪组成，土人从王公（Raja）以下皆不怀善意。上一任驻扎官据说死于中暑，再上一任则不辞而别，忽地动身去了某处不明之地，连请假的手续也懒得办。可总得有人来坐这个位子，于是本特利还是前往谢塔纳巴德：从最近的火车站起，乘骆驼与牛车，越焦渴的丘陵与沙碛荒漠，整整一百英里。

===Original===
His early impressions of the place were hardly reassuring. His first glimpse of it was from the summit of a cactus-covered hill through a red haze of dust-laden heat. The principal feature which caught his eye was the Raja’s fortress-palace perched on a high rock on the northeast side and grimly overlooking the flat-roofed city. It was known as the Nahargarh, or Tiger Fort. For the rest Shaitanabad may be summed up as a place of narrow, irregular alleys, bazaars with shops little larger than dry-goods boxes, bad smells, a perpetual plague of insects, gaily clothed people, and a general Arabian Nights atmosphere. A thousand years ago it was the same, and so it will be a thousand years hence. The local temperature was 120° in the shade, sometimes more. Except the Resident, there were no other Englishmen in the place, not even a missionary. That is sufficient testimony as to Shaitanabad’s character.

===Chinese===
他对这地方的初印象，几乎谈不上令人安心。他头一眼瞥见它，是从一座仙人掌丛生的山丘之巅，透过那弥漫着飞沙与燥热的一片红色薄霭望过去。最惹眼的景物，是王公那座堡垒式的宫殿——它高踞于东北方一块巨岩之上，阴沉沉地俯瞰着这片平顶屋构成的城市。此堡名为纳哈加尔堡（Nahargarh），意即虎堡。至于其余，谢塔纳巴德大抵可概括为这样一处地方：狭窄曲折的小巷，店铺不比干货箱大多少的集市，难闻的气味，终年不绝的虫害，衣着鲜丽的人们，以及一种笼统的、《天方夜谭》般的氛围。一千年前它是这般模样，一千年后想必仍是这般。当地荫处的气温高达华氏一百二十度，有时还更高。除驻扎官外，当地再无别的英国人，连一个传教士也没有。仅此一点，便足以说明谢塔纳巴德的脾性了。

===Original===
Bently regarded it as fortunate that the Residency was situated outside the city, and that his predecessor’s staff of Bengali and Rajput servants were waiting to receive him. A bath, a fairly well-cooked meal, and a good night’s rest, in spite of the heat, removed the exhaustion of the journey and made the outlook appear more satisfactory.

===Chinese===
本特利觉得还算幸运的是：驻扎官官邸坐落在城外，而且前任留下的那班孟加拉与拉杰普特人（Rajput）仆从正等着迎接他。一场沐浴、一顿烹制得颇为可口的饭菜、一夜酣眠——尽管酷热难当——已将旅途的疲惫驱散，使前景显得令人满意了些。

===Original===
His first duty being to call on the Raja, he early proceeded to the palace accompanied by his servant, Lal Das. Ascending a flight of steps cut in the towering sandstone rock, which was the only means of access to the fort, Bently passed through a great gate into a courtyard. There he was left to stand in the full rays of the Indian sun while the Raja’s attendants went in to announce the Resident’s arrival. Finally they returned and conducted him through a deep veranda into a hall, from which another room opened. This room, carpeted with Persian rugs and hung with rare kinkhab draperies, seemed cool and pleasant after the heat without.

The Raja, Chumbu Singh, was seated on a cushioned *gadi*, surrounded by several attendants. He was a tall, slender man of about forty, and wore the peculiar Rajput side whiskers. His attire consisted of a pearl-embroidered coat, trousers of white tussah silk, and an elaborately embroidered turban. One hand toyed with the gem-encrusted hilt of a short sword stuck in a broad silk cummerbund.

===Chinese===
他的首要职责是拜会王公，于是大清早便由仆从拉尔·达斯（Lal Das）陪同，前往王宫。那座巍峨的砂岩巨岩上凿有一道石阶，乃是进入城堡的唯一通路。本特利拾级而上，穿过一道大门，进入一处庭院。他被留在那儿，在印度烈日的直射下立着，等候王公的侍从进去通报驻扎官驾临。末了他们终于回来，引他穿过一道深廊进入一座厅堂，厅堂里又开出一间屋子。此室铺着波斯地毯，悬着稀有的金卡布（kinkhab，金银线织锦）帷幔，与外头的酷热相较，显得清凉宜人。

王公琼布·辛格（Chumbu Singh）端坐于铺了软垫的*加迪*（gadi，宝座）之上，几名侍从簇拥在侧。他是个四十来岁、身形瘦高的人，留着拉杰普特人特有的那种连鬓鬓角。他身穿一袭绣珍珠的外衣，着白柞丝绸长裤，头戴一顶绣工繁复的头巾。一只手把玩着一柄短剑——剑插在宽大的丝质腰带（cummerbund）里，剑柄上镶满了宝石。

===Original===
At this first meeting conversation was short and formal. The Raja asked after Bently’s health, and requested his opinion of such matters as the climate. He spoke fluent English, and seemed well educated and intelligent.

“I hope you will like Shaitanabad,” he said, finally. “Sport here is good. If at any time you care to hunt tigers, I shall be glad to place all the facilities in my power at your disposal.”

===Chinese===
初次会面，交谈简短而拘于礼数。王公问候了本特利的身体，又就气候之类的事务征询他的看法。他英语流利，看得出受过良好教育，人也聪敏。

“但愿你会喜欢谢塔纳巴德。”他最后说道，“此地的猎物甚佳。你若何时想猎虎，我很乐意将我力所能及的一切便利悉数供你驱使。”

===Original===
Bently retired on the whole rather favorably impressed with the Raja and inclined to treat certain adverse reports of his conduct as exaggerated. Native princes are always more or less prone to irritation at the ways of British Residents. Probably such was the basis of Chumbu Singh’s offense in British official quarters.

===Chinese===
本特利告退时，对王公的印象总体上相当不错，倒倾向于认为关于其行止的某些不利传闻未免言过其实。土邦王公对于英国驻扎官的做派，总不免或多或少有些恼怒。琼布·辛格在英方官场中所招致的非议，多半便源于此。

===Original===
During the next two or three weeks Bently thought he had reason to be pleased at his judgment of native character. Chumbu Singh fell so readily into certain administrative reforms proposed by Bently that there appeared little doubt of his earnestness to walk in the path of modern progress. So far things looked much better than he had been led to anticipate, even the temperature dropping to 98° at midnight. It was after the settlement of a land ownership case, in which Bently’s assistance had been requested, that the Raja made a proposal.

===Chinese===
此后两三周间，本特利觉得自己有理由庆幸当初对土人性情的判断没错。琼布·辛格对本特利提出的几项行政改革欣然从命，其锐意走上近代进步之路的热忱，看来殆无疑义。事态至此，远比旁人预先让他料想的要好，就连气温到午夜也降到了华氏九十八度。直到一桩地权纠纷案的尘埃落定——此案中曾请本特利从旁协助——王公方才提出了一个建议。

===Original===
“I have arranged for a tiger hunt tonight,” he said. “Would you like to go?”

Bently eagerly responded in the affirmative.

“This is a terrible animal, Sahib,” continued the Raja. “He has killed many people. His den is in the hills—an old cave temple, haunted, my people, say, by ghosts and devils. However that may be, the tiger is many devils in himself. He stalks both cattle and villagers in broad daylight, and kills not only when hungry, but out of the devilishness of his heart. We have planned to get him at the cave.”

===Chinese===
“今夜我安排了一场猎虎，”他说，“你可愿同去？”

本特利热切地一口应允。

“这是一头可怕的畜生，萨希布（Sahib，先生），”王公接着说，“它已咬死了许多人。它的巢穴在山里——一座古老的石窟神庙，据我的人说，里头闹鬼，有妖魔作祟。无论真假，这猛虎本身便集了众魔于一身。它竟敢光天化日之下潜袭牲畜与村民，杀人不为充饥，只为心头那份恶毒。我们已筹划好，要去那洞穴里收拾它。”

===Original===
When the last rays of the sun had faded from the hot red sandstone of the Nahargarh, and the gray veil of dusk had fallen over Shaitanabad, Chumbu Singh and several followers came to the Residency to announce that all was ready. They were armed and mounted on wiry Baluchi ponies. Bently joined them, accompanied by Lal Das, and the party set off across the rapidly darkening plain. Their destination, as indicated by Chumbu Singh, was a mass of low-lying, jungle-clad hills two miles to the northeast. The plain, or rather desert, between was barren with scarce a tree or shrub, and its monotony was broken only by a series of nasty mullahs or gulleys, which gave much trouble, necessitating careful horsemanship and slow traveling.

===Chinese===
待夕阳的余晖从纳哈加尔堡那滚烫的红色砂岩上褪尽，黄昏的灰色帷幔已笼罩了谢塔纳巴德，琼布·辛格偕数名随从来至官邸，禀报一切齐备。他们全副武装，骑着结实耐劳的俾路支（Baluchi）矮种马。本特利带着拉尔·达斯加入其中，一行人便横越那迅速暗沉下去的平原出发了。据琼布·辛格所指，他们的目的地是东北方两英里外一丛低矮、丛林密覆的山冈。中间这片平原——或者说是荒漠——寸草不生，难得见到一株树或一丛灌木，其单调唯有一道道恼人的干沟与冲沟（nullah 或 gulley）加以打破，给人添了不少麻烦，不得不小心驭马、缓辔而行。

===Original===
Reaching the hills without mishaps, the horses were left near an old tomb in charge of the servants. The Raja, Bently, Lal Das, and two Rajputs continued afoot. They first followed a bullock trail, and then a narrow footpath, one of the Rajputs acting as guide. The path, winding up and down, through cactus jungle, deep ravines, and among great boulders, led well into the hills.

The moon had risen, and as they emerged from a patch of jungle, Bently saw the cave temple of which Chumbu Singh had spoken. It was in a steep hillside, where the formation changed from sandstone to light granite. In front was a level space overgrown with cactus, jungle plants, and a few larger trees. There were three entrances, the central one being about fifteen feet high, and the other two smaller. The larger one was open, but the others were choked with debris.

===Chinese===
一行人安然无恙地到了山前，便将马匹留在古墓近旁交仆从看管。王公、本特利、拉尔·达斯与两名拉杰普特人弃马步行。先沿一条牛踪而行，继而转入一条狭窄的小径，由其中一名拉杰普特人充作向导。小径盘曲起伏，穿仙人掌丛林、过深壑、行于巨石之间，一路深入山腹。

月亮已升起。众人从一片丛林中钻出时，本特利看见了琼布·辛格所说的那座石窟神庙。它嵌在一面陡峭的山坡上，此处岩层由砂岩变为浅色花岗岩。庙前有一片平地，上长满了仙人掌、丛林草木及几棵稍大的树。庙有三个入口，居中的约十五英尺高，另外两个较小。那大些的洞口敞开着，其余两个则被碎石瓦砾堵死。

===Original===
The hunters toiled up the hillside, scrambling over boulders and through the thick scrub. There was no path, and it was not pleasant travelling. A handful of cactus spines, even on a moonlit night in the presence of ancient and interesting ruins, is more productive of profanity than enthusiasm.

“This is the ancient temple of Jains,” said the Raja when they at last came panting to the entrance.

Bently peered within to behold the moonlight shining on huge indistinct figures, old forgotten gods carved in the solid granite. There were also great footprints in the thick dust, evidently those of the tiger. Undoubtedly he was a monster animal, for Bently had never seen pads to equal them.

===Chinese===
猎人们艰难地攀上山坡，在巨石与浓密灌木间手脚并用地钻爬。没有路，走起来并不惬意。一把仙人掌的刺——哪怕是在月夜、面对古老而有趣的废墟时——所催生的也多是咒骂，而非兴致。

“这是一座古老的耆那教（Jain）神庙，”待众人终于气喘吁吁地攀到洞口时，王公说道。

本特利向里张望，只见月光照在几尊巨大而影影绰绰的形体上——那是些被遗忘的古老神祇，雕刻在整块花岗岩之中。厚厚的积尘里还有硕大的足印，分明是那猛虎所留。这畜生无疑是个庞然大物，因为本特利从未见过能与之比肩的掌垫。

===Original===
The two Rajputs examined the pads carefully, and gave it as their opinion that the tiger had crept forth, bent on stalking about nightfall, and would probably not return until morning. They were sure he was not in the cave. The Raja seemed annoyed at the prospect of a long wait, and abused the Rajputs for not arranging matters so that they might have arrived at the cave earlier and so intercepted the tiger.

===Chinese===
两名拉杰普特人仔细查看那些掌垫，断言猛虎已潜行出洞，意在黄昏前后四处窥伺，多半要到清晨方才归返。他们确信它此刻不在洞中。王公似因要久候而面有愠色，斥责那两名拉杰普特人办事不力，没安排好让众人早些抵达洞口，好截住那头猛虎。

===Original===
“I owe you many apologies,” he said, turning Bently. “You see what comes of trusting to these fellows. But since it is such an effort to get here, I suggest that we wait for the tiger.”

“Certainly,” agreed Bently. “I am willing to wait as long as you like for a shot at that beast.”

“Very well,” the Raja nodded. “In the meantime suppose we take a look at the cave temple. It is an interesting place, of its kind without equal in India.”

===Chinese===
“实在对不住得很，”他转向本特利说道，“你也瞧见了，信这些家伙会是什么下场。不过既已费这么大劲到了这里，我提议咱们就守着，等那猛虎回来。”

“那是自然，”本特利应道，“为了一枪撂倒那畜生，你要等多久我都奉陪。”

“很好，”王公点头，“那等候之际，不妨去看看这座石窟神庙吧。这地方很有看头，在印度同类之中可谓无出其右。”

===Original===
To this Bently readily assented. Thereupon the Raja sent off one of the Rajputs and Lal Das with an order for the rest of the retainers to keep watch in case the tiger returned unexpectedly. The other Rajput then produced a torch, and the party of three entered the cave. First they passed through a sort of peristyle, or antechamber, which, thirty yards from the entrance, opened into a vast grotto. This was the main excavation. Huge stone pillars, elaborately sculptured, supported the roof, and around the sides great gods and goddesses of the Jain mythology, called Arhats, glared downward. The torch illuminated dimly, leaving much in shadow, and in the shadow imagination created strange fantasies. A narrow passage from the grotto ended in a smaller chamber littered with fallen fragments. It was more than once necessary to climb over some god whose face was in the dust. Another short passage led to an arched entrance two-thirds blocked with debris.

===Chinese===
对此本特利欣然应允。于是王公遣走其中一名拉杰普特人和拉尔·达斯，传令余下的扈从在外守望，以防猛虎出乎意料地折返。另一名拉杰普特人随即取出一支火把，三人一行便进了洞穴。他们先是穿过一种列柱廊（peristyle）式的前室，前室在距洞口三十码处，豁然通入一座巨大的石窟。这便是主窟了。一根根雕工繁复的巨大石柱撑起窟顶，四面则是耆那教神话中的男女大神——称作阿罗汉（Arhat）——居高临下地怒目俯视。火把照得昏昏暗暗，许多地方都隐在阴影里，而在阴影之中，想象便造出种种奇形怪状。从石窟引出的一条窄道尽头，是一间较小的洞室，里头散落着崩塌的碎石。途中不止一次要攀过某尊面朝尘土的神像。再经一条短通道，便来到一道拱门入口，已被碎石堵去三分之二。

===Original===
“We cannot go any further,” said the Raja, “but if you take the torch and climb up on that pile, you will be able to see into a greater cave beyond. My superstitious retainers believe that it is the abode of ghosts and devils, the guardians of the temple.”

Bently’s curiosity was stimulated. Torch in hand he surmounted the obstruction, and peered into a gulf of black darkness. He seemed on the verge of a great precipice, the limits and bottom of which the torchlight failed to reach. From far beneath he fancied he caught a splash of water tumbling over a rocky bed, and strange echoes floated upward, but he could see nothing. It was an appalling abyss, which, for all he knew, might sink into the foundations of the earth.

===Chinese===
“我们没法再往前了，”王公说，“不过你若举着火把爬上那堆乱石，便能望见里头一个更大的石窟。我那些迷信的扈从相信，那里是鬼魅妖魔——也就是神庙的守护神——的居所。”

本特利的好奇心被勾了起来。他举着火把翻过那道障碍，向一团漆黑的深渊中张望。他仿佛立于一道绝壁的边缘，火光既照不到它的边际，也探不到它的底。他隐约听见极深处有水花溅落在岩石河床上的声响，还有古怪的回声自下飘来，可他什么也看不见。那是一道骇人的深渊，据他所知，说不定一直通到地底的根基。

===Original===
Suddenly he received a violent push from behind, accompanied by a muttered curse hurled from the Raja’s lips. Bently tumbled forward, and, in doing so, threw out an arm wildly to save himself. It caught the barrel of the Raja’s rifle, swept it from his grasp, and hurled it clattering into the chasm beneath. Bently promptly followed the Raja’s rifle down a steep crumbling slope to what would have been certain death had his own rifle not brought him up with a jerk by becoming lodged to half its length between two rocks. As it were, there he hung in midair with the buttress of his rifle for his only support. A shower of following pebbles swept on down into nothingness.

===Chinese===
冷不防，他背后遭了重重一推，伴着王公嘴里嘟囔出的一句咒骂。本特利身子朝前一栽，慌乱中狂伸出手臂想要自救。那只手恰好抓住了王公步枪的枪管，一拽之下把它从对方手中夺脱，哐啷一声甩进了下方的深渊。本特利随即紧随那支步枪，沿一道陡峭而松塌的斜坡滚落下去——要不是他自己的步枪卡进两块岩石之间、卡进去足足半截，猛地将他拽住，他必死无疑。就这样，他悬在了半空，那支步枪犹如一根撑柱，成了他唯一的凭借。一串随他坠下的碎石纷纷滚落，消失在虚无之中。

===Original===
For some moments he remained almost stunned by the peril of the situation, but presently his mind began to gather in the slender chances of escape. He had apparently been brought up with his back against a sidewall of rock and with one foot resting on a narrow projection. Reaching out a hand, and groping with it, he discovered that the narrow projection was one of a flight of irregular steps cut in the rock and leading upward. If a hazardous foothold, he presumed it had been used at some period, and decided to tempt its course.

===Chinese===
有那么一阵子，他几乎因处境之险而吓懵了，但很快他的脑子便开始搜寻那一丝逃生的微茫生机。他显然是被卡停在这儿：背靠着岩壁，一只脚踩在一处窄窄的凸出物上。他伸手去摸索，发现那处窄小的凸起，竟是在岩壁上凿出的一道参差石阶中的一级，阶阶向上。落脚处固然险，但他揣度这石阶从前总有被人用过的时候，遂决意冒险一试。

===Original===
He balanced himself carefully, and disengaging his rifle, crept slowly upward step by step. Once his foot slipped, and he almost fell, but throwing himself inward he found he had stumbled into the entrance of a narrow passage. That meant safety from the chasm at any rate, and he gave vent to a huge breath of relief. His next act was to test the springs of his rifle, and so far as he was able to judge in the darkness he was further gratified to find that it was uninjured. Then he went cautiously forward, guiding his progress by a hand on the sidewall. Presently he came to a broad flight of steps partly choked up with fallen debris. Climbing up this, he emerged into the grotto of the temple.

===Chinese===
他小心翼翼地稳住身子，卸下步枪，一级一级缓缓向上爬去。有一回脚下一滑，险些跌落，但他朝里一侧身，竟撞进了窄道的一个入口。无论如何，总算脱离了那深渊，他长长地舒了一口气。他接着检查步枪的机簧，在黑暗中凭感觉判断，竟欣慰地发现枪并未受损。随后他小心前行，一手扶着侧壁探路。不久便来到一道宽阔的石阶前，部分已被坠落的碎石堵塞。攀过这道石阶，他钻出了洞口，回到了神庙的主窟。

===Original===
Then he drew back suddenly. A coughing snarl echoed through the cavern. Bently softly moved behind the stone image of a god, and looked out from its shadow. From a cleft in the roof of the temple a stream of moonlight fell within, and toned with silver the yellow body and velvet stripes of a monster tiger. It also shone upon the prostrate forms of the Raja and his Rajput retainer, held beneath the huge paws of the Lord of the Jungle. Again the coughing snarl echoed through the temple. The eyes of the beast flashed with savage thirst for blood as it lowered its head to plunge its fangs into the throat of one of its victims.

Bently raised his rifle to the shoulder, took steady aim, and fired. A terrific roar shook the stone gods, a gigantic convulsion seized upon the body of the tiger as it rolled over. Bently fired again, and then strode from his place of concealment. Another shot at closer range finished the death struggle of the tiger. Its last breath went forth in a choking growl of defiance.

===Chinese===
就在这时他猛地缩回了身。一声咳嗽般的咆哮回荡在石窟之中。本特利悄然闪到一尊神像的石像背后，从它的阴影里向外窥看。一缕月光自神庙窟顶的裂隙倾泻而下，为那头猛虎焦黄的虎身与丝绒般的斑纹镀上一层银色。月光同时也照在王公与其拉杰普特侍从那俯卧的躯体上——他们被丛林之主那双硕大的虎爪按在地上。那声咳嗽般的咆哮再度在神庙中回荡。猛兽双眼闪着嗜血的凶光，正低下头去，要将獠牙刺入某一具猎物的喉管。

本特利将步枪抵上肩头，稳稳瞄准，开了火。一声骇人的虎啸震得众石神发颤，猛虎的身子被一阵剧烈的抽搐攫住，翻倒在地。本特利又补了一枪，随即大步从藏身处走出。近处再补一枪，便了结了那猛虎的垂死挣扎。它吐出最后一口气，化作一声哽咽的、桀骜不驯的低吼。

===Original===
It took but a cursory examination to convince Bently that both the Raja and the Rajput were past rendering any account of their treachery on this earth, and a lack of response to his shouts made it plain that the Raja’s retainers had promptly bolted when the tiger unexpectedly returned. The Raja and the Rajput has thus been left to encounter the powerful beast unarmed.

How Bently regained the Residency was a matter he was unable to explain except by instinct, but daylight had already broken when he reached the compound. Then he acted with swift decision.

He sent orders for the Raja’s retainers to appear at the Residency for an investigation, which eventually led to a thorough exploration of the temple. By another entrance the bottom of the abyss was gained, and sundry relics discovered there proved how the Raja had relieved himself of the undesirable presence of those who had interfered with his dubious proceedings.

===Chinese===
稍一查看，本特利便确信王公与那名拉杰普特人都已无法再在人间为自己的叛逆作任何交代了；而他的呼喊无人回应，可见猛虎出乎意料地折返时，王公的扈从们早已脚底抹油、溜之大吉。如此一来，王公与那拉杰普特人便落得手无寸铁，独自面对这头猛兽。

本特利究竟怎样回到官邸，除了凭本能之外他自己也说不清，但等他赶进院子时，天已大亮。于是他当机立断。

他传令王公的扈从们到官邸受审，这场调查最终引向了对神庙的一次彻底勘查。众人经由另一入口下到了深渊底部，在那里发现的一些零碎遗物，恰好证实了王公是如何“摆脱”那些碍手碍脚、干扰其可疑勾当之人——他所用的，正是这种不留活口的方法。

## The Uncharted Isle / 海图未载之岛

===Original===
I do not know how long I had been drifting in the boat. There are several days and nights that I remember only as alternate blanks of greyness and darkness; and, after these, there came a phantasmagoric eternity of delirium and an indeterminate lapse into pitch-black oblivion. The seawater I had swallowed must have revived me; for when I came to myself, I was lying at the bottom of the boat with my head lifted a little in the stern, and six inches of brine lapping at my lips. I was gasping and strangling with the mouthfuls I had taken; the boat was tossing roughly, with more water coming over the sides at each toss; and I could hear the sound of breakers not far away.

I tried to sit up and succeeded, after a prodigious effort. My thoughts and sensations were curiously confused, and I found it difficult to orient myself in any manner. The physical sensation of extreme thirst was dominant over all else—my mouth was lined with running, throbbing fire—and I felt lightheaded, and the rest of my body was strangely limp and hollow. It was hard to remember just what had happened; and, for a moment, I was not even puzzled by the fact that I was alone in the boat. But, even to my dazed, uncertain senses, the roar of those breakers had conveyed a distinct warning of peril; and, sitting up, I reached for the oars.

===Chinese===
我不知道自己已在小艇中漂流了多久。有好几个日日夜夜，在我记忆中不过是一连串灰白与漆黑交替的空白；此后，便是一段恍如幻灯走马般漫长的谵妄，以及一场漫无定限的、坠入漆黑忘却之深渊的沉落。我所吞下的海水想必将我激醒——因为当我恢复知觉时，我正仰卧在艇底，头在船尾微微抬起，六英寸深的咸水正一下一下舔舐着我的嘴唇。我呛咳着、窒息着，为那几口灌入喉中的海水所苦；小艇剧烈地颠簸着，每颠一次便有更多的水越过舷沿灌进来；而我已能听见不远处理涛拍岸的轰鸣。

我挣扎着想坐起来，使尽了浑身力气，总算做到了。我的思绪与感官异样地混乱，发现自己无论如何也难以辨认方向。极度干渴的肉体感觉压倒了一切——我的口腔里仿佛敷满了一层奔涌跳动的烈火——我头昏眼花，周身上下有一种古怪的绵软与空虚。要记清究竟发生了什么，实在困难；有一瞬间，我甚至对自己竟孤身一人在艇上这一事实也不觉困惑。然而，即便在我那昏沉、恍惚的感官看来，那碎浪的轰鸣已分明传达出一个危险的警告；于是，我坐起身来，伸手去摸船桨。

===Original===
The oars were gone, but in my enfeebled state, it was not likely that I could have made much use of them anyway. I looked around, and saw that the boat was drifting rapidly in the wash of a shoreward current, between two low-lying darkish reefs half-hidden by flying veils of foam. A steep and barren cliff loomed before me; but, as the boat neared it, the cliff seemed to divide miraculously, revealing a narrow chasm through which I floated into the mirror-like waters of a still lagoon. The passage from the rough sea without, to a realm of sheltered silence and seclusion, was no less abrupt than the transition of events and scenery which often occurs in a dream.

The lagoon was long and narrow, and ran sinuously away between level shores that were fringed with an ultra-tropical vegetation. There were many fern-palms, of a type I had never seen, and many stiff, gigantic cycads, and wide-leaved grasses taller than young trees. I wondered a little about them even then; though, as the boat drifted slowly toward the nearest beach, I was mainly preoccupied with the clarifying and assorting of my recollections. These gave me more trouble than one would think.

===Chinese===
船桨已不知去向，不过以我此刻衰弱之状，即便有桨，怕也使不上几分力气。我四下张望，只见小艇正顺着一股扑向海岸的潮流急速漂去，两侧是两道低矮的暗色礁石，半隐在飞溅的浪幕之后。一道陡峻而荒秃的悬崖矗立在我前方；然而，当小艇驶近时，那悬崖竟仿佛奇迹般从中裂开，露出一条狭窄的罅隙，我便由此漂入了一片静潟湖那明镜般的水域。从外面波涛汹涌的大海，骤然转入这一片荫蔽、静谧、与世隔绝的境域，这转换之突兀，恰如梦境中常常出现的那种事件与场景的骤然更替。

这潟湖狭而长，在两岸平坦的边缘之间蜿蜒远去，岸上密密地覆盖着一片过分繁茂的热带植被。那里有许多我从未见过的蕨棕榈（fern-palm），许多挺拔而巨大的苏铁（cycad），还有比幼树还高的阔叶草。即便在彼时彼地，我便已对这些植物心生些许疑惑；不过，当小艇缓缓漂向最近的那片海滩时，我心头所盘桓的，主要还是如何理清并归拢自己的回忆。这事比人们所想象的要棘手得多。

===Original===
I must have been a trifle lightheaded still; and the seawater I had drunk couldn’t have been very good for me either, even though it had helped to revive me. I remembered, of course, that I was Mark Irwin, first mate of the freighter Auckland, plying between Callao and Wellington; and I recalled only too well the night when Captain Melville had wrenched me bodily from my bunk, from the dreamless undersea of a dog-tired slumber, shouting that the ship was on fire. I recalled the roaring hell of flame and smoke through which we had fought our way to the deck, to find that the vessel was already past retrieving, since the fire had reached the oil that formed part of her cargo; and then the swift launching of boats in the lurid glare of the conflagration. Half the crew had been caught in the blazing forecastle; and those of us who escaped were compelled to put off without water or provisions. We had rowed for days in a dead calm, without sighting any vessel, and were suffering the tortures of the damned, when a storm had arisen. In this storm, two of the boats were lost; and the third, which was manned by Captain Melville, the second mate, the boatswain, and myself, had survived. But sometime during the storm, or during the days and nights of delirium that followed, my companions must have gone overboard.… This much I recalled; but all of it was somehow unreal and remote, and seemed to pertain only to another person than the one who was floating shoreward on the waters of a still lagoon. I felt very dreamy and detached; and even my thirst didn’t trouble me half as much now as it had on awakening.

===Chinese===
我想必仍有些头昏眼花；而且我所饮下的那点海水，纵然有助于令我苏醒，对我恐怕也没有什么好处。我当然记得，我是马克·欧文（Mark Irwin），货轮奥克兰号（Auckland）的大副，这艘船往返于卡亚俄（Callao）与惠灵顿（Wellington）之间；我也再清楚不过地记得，梅尔维尔船长（Captain Melville）把我从铺位上生生拽起、将我从那累极之后无梦的深海般的沉睡中拖出来的那个夜晚——他高喊着船起火了。我记得我们一路拼死从烈焰与浓烟那咆哮的地狱中杀上甲板，却发现船只早已无可挽救，因为火势已蔓延到她所载货物中那部分燃油；随后，便是大火那惨烈的红光之中救生艇的仓促下水。半数船员被烈焰封死在起火的船首楼里；而我们这些侥幸逃出的人，被迫在没有饮水、没有给养的情况下离船下海。我们在死一般的无风状态中划了好几天桨，连一条船的影子也没见着，正受着地狱般的煎熬，这时一场风暴骤然而起。在这场风暴中，两条救生艇失踪了；而第三条——由梅尔维尔船长、二副、水手长和我本人所乘的这一条——侥幸存活了下来。但在风暴之中，抑或在其后那些谵妄的日日夜夜里，我的同伴们想必都已落海。……这便是我的回忆；然而这一切，不知怎的，都显得虚幻而遥远，仿佛只属于另一个人，而不属于此刻正漂浮在那片静潟湖的水面上、向岸边漂去的这个我。我感到一种如坠梦中的恍惚与抽离；甚至连干渴，此刻也不及我初醒时那般折磨我了。

===Original===
The boat touched a beach of fine, pearly sand, before I began to wonder where I was and to speculate concerning the shores I had reached. I knew that we had been hundreds of miles southwest of Easter Island on the night of the fire, in a part of the Pacific where there is no other land; and certainly this couldn’t be Easter Island. What, then, could it be? I realized with a sort of shock that I must have found something not on any charted course or geological map. Of course, it was an isle of some kind; but I could form no idea of its possible extent; and I had no way of deciding offhand whether it was peopled or unpeopled. Except for the lush vegetation, and a few queer-looking birds and butterflies, and some equally queer-looking fish in the lagoon, there was no visible life anywhere.

===Chinese===
小艇已触上一片细腻、珠母般的沙滩，我才开始思忖自己究竟身在何处，揣度我所抵达的这片海岸究竟是什么地方。我知道，失火的那一夜，我们正在复活节岛（Easter Island）西南方数百英里之处，那一带太平洋水域再无别的陆地；而这里，断不可能是复活节岛。那么，这里又能是什么地方呢？我几乎是带着一阵震惊地意识到：我必然是撞上了某一处从未标注在任何航线或地质地图上的所在。当然，这是一座岛屿——某种意义上的岛屿；但我无从揣想它可能的幅员；也无从当即判断它上面究竟有没有人烟。除了那葱茏茂密的植被、几种模样古怪的鸟与蝴蝶，以及潟湖中一些同样古怪的鱼之外，四下里看不见丝毫生灵的迹象。

---

===Original===
I got out of the boat, feeling very weak and wobbly in the hot white sunshine that poured down upon everything like a motionless universal cataract. My first thought was to find fresh water; and I plunged at random among the mighty fern-trees, parting their enormous leaves with extreme effort, and sometimes reeling against their boles to save myself from falling. Twenty or thirty paces, however, and then I came to a tiny rill that sprang in shattered crystal from a low ledge, to collect in a placid pool where ten-inch mosses and broad, anemone-like blossoms mirrored themselves. The water was cool and sweet: I drank profoundly, and felt the benison of its freshness permeate all my parched tissues.

===Chinese===
我下了小艇，在倾泻于万物之上、宛若一道凝然不动的普天之瀑的那灼白发烫的阳光里，只觉浑身酸软、脚下虚浮。我头一个念头便是寻找淡水；我胡乱地一头扎进那些巨大的蕨树之间，拼尽全力拨开它们硕大的叶片，有时踉跄着靠上树干，才勉强稳住身形不致跌倒。然而不过二三十步，我便寻到了一缕细小的溪流，它以碎裂的水晶之姿自一道低矮的石坎涌出，汇入一个静谧的水潭，潭中倒映着十来英寸高的苔藓和宽展的、银莲花般的花朵。水清凉而甘甜：我痛饮了一番，只觉那清冽的甘霖渗入了我每一寸焦渴的肌理。

===Original===
Now I began to look around for some sort of edible fruit. Close to the stream, I found a shrub that was trailing its burden of salmon-yellow drupes on the giant mosses. I couldn’t identify the fruit; but its aspect was delicious, and I decided to take a chance. It was full of a sugary pulp; and strength returned to me even as I ate. My brain cleared, and I recovered many, if not all, of the faculties that had been in a state of partial abeyance.

I went back to the boat, and bailed out all the seawater; then I tried to drag the boat as far up on the sand as I could, in case I might need it again at any future time. My strength was inadequate to the task; and still fearing that the tide might carry it away, I cut some of the high grasses with my clasp-knife and wove them into a long rope, with which I moored the boat to the nearest palm-tree.

===Chinese===
接着我便开始四下寻觅某种可食的果实。离溪流不远，我发现一丛灌木，正将它那满枝鲑鱼黄色的核果垂坠在巨大的苔藓之上。我认不出这是什么果子；但它外表诱人，我遂决意冒险一试。果肉饱含着糖分的浆质；我边吃，气力便边回到身上。我的头脑清明起来，恢复了先前陷入半停滞状态的许多官能——虽未必全部。

我折回小艇，将舱中的海水尽数舀出；随后我试着把小艇在沙滩上尽可能往高处拖，以防日后万一还要再用到它。我的气力却不足以胜任此事；我仍担心潮水会将它卷走，便用折刀割下一些高草，编成一条长绳，用它将小艇拴系在最近的一棵棕榈树上。

===Original===
Now, for the first time, I surveyed my situation with an analytic eye, and became aware of much that I had hitherto failed to observe or realize. A medley of queer impressions thronged upon me, some of which could not have arrived through the avenues of the known senses. To begin with, I saw more clearly the abnormal oddity of the plant-forms about me: they were not the palm-ferns, grasses and shrubs that are native to South sea islands: their leaves, their stems, their frondage, were mainly of uncouth archaic types, such as might have existed in former aeons, on the sea-lost littorals of Mu. They differed from anything I had seen in Australia or New Guinea, those asylums of a primeval flora; and, gazing upon them, I was overwhelmed with intimations of a dark and prehistoric antiquity. And the silence around me seemed to become the silence of dead ages and of things that have gone down beneath oblivion’s tide. From that moment, I felt that there was something wrong about the island. But somehow I couldn’t tell just what it was, or seize definitely upon everything that contributed to this impression.

===Chinese===
直到此刻，我才头一次以审视的目光打量自己的处境，并察觉到许多先前未曾留意、未曾体悟到的情形。种种古怪的印象纷至沓来，其中有几重，绝非经由我所熟悉的那些感官通道所能抵达。首先，我更清楚地看出了周遭那些植物形态的异常怪诞：它们并非南海诸岛土生土长的棕蕨、野草和灌木：它们的叶、它们的茎、它们的蕨叶，大多属于某种拙陋古拙的类型，仿佛曾在往昔的万古中、在姆大陆（Mu）那没入汪洋的滨海之地生存过。它们与我曾在澳大利亚或新几内亚——那些原始植物的避难之所——所见过的任何东西都截然不同；凝望着它们，我心中涌起一种幽暗而太古的洪荒之感，几将我淹没。而周遭的寂静，似乎也化作了已死年代万物沉没于忘却之潮下后的那种死寂。自那一刻起，我便感到这座岛屿有什么不对劲。但我不知怎的，就是说不出究竟是什么，也无法确切地抓住所有构成这一印象的端倪。

===Original===
Aside from the bizarre-looking vegetation, I noticed that there was a queerness about the very sun. It was too high in the heavens for any latitude to which I could conceivably have drifted; and it was too large anyway; and the sky was unnaturally bright, with a dazzling incandescence. There was a spell of perpetual quietude upon the air, and never the slightest rippling of leaves or water; and the whole landscape hung before me like a monstrous vision of unbelievable realms apart from time and space. According to all the maps, that island couldn’t exist, anyhow.… More and more decisively I knew that there was something wrong: I felt an eerie confusion, a weird bewilderment, like one who has been cast away on the shores of an alien planet; and it seemed to me that I was separated from my former life, and from everything I had ever known, by an interval of distance more irremeable than all the blue leagues of sea and sky; that, like the island itself, I was lost to all possible reorientation. For a few instants, this feeling became a nervous panic, a paralyzing horror.

===Chinese===
除了那些模样诡谲的植被之外，我还注意到连那轮太阳都透着一种古怪。它悬在天穹中的位置，对我所能漂抵的任何纬度而言都太高了些；而且无论如何它也太大了些；天空则亮得不近情理，泛着一种令人目眩的炽白。空气之上笼罩着一道永恒静寂的符咒，枝叶与水面从无丝毫涟漪；整片风光就那样悬在我眼前，宛若一幅出自时空之外的不可置信之境的可怖幻象。无论依照哪一张地图，这座岛屿都断无存在之理。……我愈发笃定地意识到有什么地方不对：我感到一种令人毛骨悚然的迷乱，一种诡异的惘然，仿佛一个被抛上异星海岸的遇险者；我觉得自己与从前的生活、与我所熟知的一切之间，隔开了一段比那所有的、蔚蓝的若干里格的海与天更无可挽回的距离；仿佛连同这座岛屿本身一道，我已永远丧失了一切重新辨明方位的可能。有那么几个瞬间，这种感觉化作一阵神经质的惊恐、一种令人瘫痪的恐惧。

===Original===
In an effort to overcome my agitation, I set off along the shore of the lagoon, pacing with feverish rapidity. It occurred to me that I might as well explore the island; and perhaps, after all, I might find some clue to the mystery, might stumble on something of explanation or reassurance.

After several serpent-like turns of the winding water, I reached the end of the lagoon. Here the country began to slope upward toward a high ridge, heavily wooded with the same vegetation I had already met, to which a long-leaved araucaria was now added. This ridge was apparently the crest of the island; and, after a half-hour of groping among the ferns, the stiff archaic shrubs and araucarias, I managed to surmount it.

===Chinese===
为了压下心中的激荡，我沿着潟湖的岸边迈步走去，步频急促得近乎狂躁。我转念一想，索性把这岛屿探查一番也罢；说不定，我终归能寻到这谜团的些许线索，说不定能撞见某种足以解释或令我安心的端倪。

顺着蜿蜒的水道转过几道蛇形的弯，我走到了潟湖的尽头。由此开始，地势向上抬升，趋向一道高峻的山脊，山脊上密密匝匝覆盖着我已见过的那些植被，如今又添了一种长叶的南洋杉（araucaria）。这道山脊显然便是全岛的最高处；在蕨丛、挺拔的古拙灌木和南洋杉之间摸索了约莫半个时辰之后，我总算攀上了它的顶端。

===Original===
Here, through a rift in the foliage, I looked down upon a scene no less incredible than unexpected. The further shore of the island was visible below me; and all along the curving beach of a landlocked harbor were the stone roofs and towers of a town! Even at that distance, I could see that the architecture was of an unfamiliar type; and I was not sure at first glance whether the buildings were ancient ruins or the homes of a living people. Then, beyond the roofs, I saw that several strange-looking vessels were moored at a sort of mole, flaunting their orange sails in the sunlight.

===Chinese===
在这里，透过枝叶的一道缝隙，我俯瞰到了一幅既不可思议又出乎意料的景象。岛屿另一侧的彼岸在我脚下清晰可见；而沿着一个陆围海港那弧形海滩的整条边线，竟分布着一座城镇的石顶与塔楼！即便隔着那样的距离，我也能看出那是一种我所陌生的建筑式样；乍一瞥之下，我竟分辨不清那些屋宇究竟是古时的废墟，还是活人居住的家宅。随后，越过那些屋顶，我看到几艘模样奇特的船只停泊在某种防波堤旁，它们那橘红色的风帆在日光下招展。

===Original===
My excitement was indescribable: at most (if the island were peopled at all) I had thought to find a few savage huts; and here below me were edifices that betokened a considerable degree of civilization! What they were, or who had builded them, were problems beyond surmise; but, as I hastened down the slope toward the harbor, a very human eagerness was mingled with the dumbfoundment and stupefaction I had been experiencing. At least, there were people on the island; and, at the realization of this, the horror that had been a part of my bewilderment was dissipated for the nonce.

===Chinese===
我心中的激荡难以言表：充其量（倘若这岛上当真有人居住的话），我原以为至多不过会找到几间蛮人的茅棚；可眼下，在我脚下的，竟是一群昭示着相当文明程度的建筑！它们究竟是什么，又是何人所造，皆是无法揣度的问题；然而，当我急匆匆奔下山坡、朝那海港赶去时，一种极其人之常情的渴盼，已与我一路所感受的目瞪口呆、瞠目结舌交织在了一起。至少，岛上有人；而意识到这一点之后，先前作为我那迷惘之一部分的恐惧，便暂且烟消云散了。

===Original===
When I drew nearer to the houses, I saw that they were indeed strange. But the strangeness was not wholly inherent in their architectural forms; nor was I able to trace its every source, or define it in any way, by word or image. The houses were built of a stone whose precise color I cannot recall, since it was neither brown nor red nor grey, but a hue that seemed to combine, yet differ from, all these; and I remember only that the general type of construction was low and square, with square towers. The strangeness lay in more than this—in the sense of a remote and stupefying antiquity that emanated from them like an odor: I knew at once that they were old as the uncouth primordial trees and grasses, and, like these, were parcel of a long-forgotten world.

===Chinese===
待我走近那些屋宇，才看出它们确乎古怪。然而这种古怪并非全然内在于它们的建筑形制；我也无法穷究它的每一重根源，更无法以言辞或意象对它加以界说。那些房屋以某种石料筑成，那石料的确切颜色我已记不真切，因为它既非棕、亦非红、又非灰，而是一种仿佛糅合了这三种颜色却又与之皆异的色调；我只记得那建筑的大致样式低矮方正，配有方形的塔楼。那种古怪远不止于此——更在于一种悠远而令人错愕的太古之感，如同一缕气息从它们身上散发出来：我当即明白，它们与那些拙陋的原始草木一般古老，并且一如那些草木，同属于一个早已被遗忘的世界。

===Original===
Then I saw the people—those people before whom not only my ethnic knowledge, but my very reason, were to own themselves baffled. There were scores of them in sight among the buildings, and all of them appeared to be intensely preoccupied with something or other. At first I couldn’t make out what they were doing, or trying to do; but plainly they were much in earnest about it. Some were looking at the sea or the sun, and then at long scrolls of a paper-like material which they held in their hands; and many were grouped on a stone platform around a large, intricate metal apparatus resembling an armillary. All of these people were dressed in tunic-like garments of unusual amber and azure and Tyrian shades, cut in a fashion that was unfamiliar to history; and when I came close, I saw that their faces were broad and flat, with a vague fore-omening of the Mongolian in their oblique eyes. But, in an unspecifiable way, the character of their features was not that of any race that has seen the sun for a million years; and the low, liquid, many-vowelled words which they spoke to each other were not denotive of any recorded language.

===Chinese===
接着我便看到了那些人——面对他们，不仅我所有的族类学知识，连我的理性本身，都不得不甘拜下风。那些屋宇之间，眼中所见者不下数十，他们似乎全都极其专注地忙于某事。起初我辨不清他们究竟在做什么、或试图做什么；但他们显然对此极为认真。有的在眺望大海或太阳，而后又低头察看手中所执的长卷——一种纸质的东西；还有许多人聚集在一个石台上，围着一件庞大而繁复、形似浑天仪（armillary）的金属器物。这些人都身着一种束腰外衣式的衣袍，颜色是不同寻常的琥珀色、天青色与推罗紫色调，剪裁的式样为史所未见；待我走近，我看清他们的面孔宽阔扁平，在那双斜眼中隐约预示着某种蒙古人种的特征。可是，以某种难以言传的方式，他们面容的特质却不像任何一个曾沐于阳光下百万年之久的种族；而他们彼此交谈时所吐出的那些低缓、流丽、元音繁多的字音，亦不与任何见于记载的语言相对应。

===Original===
None of them appeared to notice me; and I went up to a group of three who were studying one of the long scrolls I have mentioned, and addressed them. For all answer, they bent closer above the scroll; and even when I plucked one of them by the sleeve, it was evident that he did not observe me. Much amazed, I peered into their faces, and was struck by the mingling of supreme perplexity and monomaniacal intentness which their expression displayed. There was much of the madman, and more of the scientist, absorbed in some irresoluble problem. Their eyes were fixed and fiery, their lips moved and mumbled in a fever of perpetual disquiet; and, following their gaze, I saw that the thing they were studying was a sort of chart or map, whose yellowing paper and faded inks were manifestly of past ages. The continents and seas and isles on this map were not those of the world I knew; and their names were written in heteroclitic runes of a lost alphabet. There was one immense continent in particular, with a tiny isle close to its southern shore; and ever and anon, one of the beings who pored above the map would touch this isle with his fingertip, and then would stare toward the empty horizon, as if he were seeking to recover a vanished shoreline. I received a distinct impression that these people were as irretrievably lost as I myself; that they too were disturbed and baffled by a situation not to be solved or redeemed.

===Chinese===
他们当中似乎没有一个人留意到我；我走向其中一组——三个人，正在研读我方才所提到的那种长卷之一——向他们搭话。作为全部的回应，他们只是更凑近了些俯身于那卷册之上；甚至当我拽住其中一人的衣袖时，他显然也没有察觉到我。我大为惊异，细看他们的面容，被他们神情中所交织的那种极致的迷惘与近乎偏执的专注所打动。那神色里，既有一个疯子的影子，更有一个科学家的影子——一个沉溺于某种无解难题中的科学家。他们的眼神凝定而灼热，嘴唇翕动、喃喃低语，处于一种无休无止的不安的狂热之中；顺着他们的目光望去，我看出他们所研读的，乃是一幅图或者说地图，那发黄的纸张和褪色的墨迹分明出自往昔的年代。这幅地图上的大陆、海洋与岛屿，并非我所认识的那个世界的面貌；而它们的名字，是用一种失传字母的乖异符文所书就的。其中尤有一片广袤的大陆，紧贴其南岸有一座极小的岛屿；时不时地，那俯身于地图上的人之一，便会用指尖去触碰这座小岛，而后便向那空空荡荡的地平线眺望，仿佛要重新寻回一段业已消逝的海岸线。我得到了一种鲜明的印象：这些人正与我一样，陷入了无可挽回的迷失；他们亦同样为一种无从解答、无从救赎的处境所困扰、所挫败。

===Original===
I went on toward the stone platform, which stood in a broad open space among the foremost houses. It was perhaps ten feet high, and access to it was given by a flight of winding steps. I mounted the steps, and tried to accost the people were crowding about the armillary-like instrument. But they too were utterly oblivious of me, and intent upon the observations they were making. Some of them were turning the great celestial sphere; some were consulting various geographical and celestial maps; and, from my nautical knowledge, I could see that certain of their companions were taking the height of the sun with a kind of astrolabe. All of them wore the same look of perplexity and savant-like preoccupation which I had observed in the others.

===Chinese===
我继续朝那石台走去，它矗立在最前面几排屋宇之间的一片开阔空地上。它约莫有十英尺来高，一道盘旋的石阶可供登临。我拾级而上，试着向围聚在那件浑天仪式器物旁的人们搭话。可他们也一样对我浑然不觉，全副心神都贯注在他们所进行的观测之上。其中几人正在转动那只硕大的天球；另几人则在查阅各种各样的地理与天文地图；而凭我航海的学识，我能看出他们的同伴中有几位正用一种星盘（astrolabe）在测量太阳的高度。他们所有人的脸上，都带着我在旁人身上所观察到的那种迷惘与学者式的专注。

===Original===
Seeing that my efforts to attract their attention were fruitless, I left the platform and wandered along the streets toward the harbor. The strangeness and inexplicability of it all were too much for me: more and more, I felt that I was being alienated from the realms of all rational experience or conjecture; that I had fallen into some unearthly limbo of confoundment and unreason, into the cul-de-sac of an ultra-terrestrial dimension. These beings were so palpably astray and bewildered; it was so obvious that they knew as well as I that there was something wrong with the geography, and perhaps with the chronology, of their island.

===Chinese===
见自己想引起他们注意的努力皆属徒劳，我便离开了石台，沿着街道朝海港方向踱去。这一切的古怪与不可索解，已超出我所能承受：我愈发感到自己正被排斥于一切合乎理性的经验与揣测的领域之外；我已坠入某种非尘世所有的迷惘与非理性之林勃狱，坠入了一个超乎尘世之维度的死胡同。这些生灵是那样分明地迷失、那样茫然无措；再明显不过了，他们与我一样心知肚明：这座岛屿的地理——或许还包括它的年代——出了什么岔子。

===Original===
I spent the rest of the day roaming around; but nowhere could I find anyone who was able to perceive my presence; and nowhere was there anything to reassure me, or resolve my ever-growing confusion of mind and spirit. Everywhere there were men, and also women; and though comparatively few of them were grey and wrinkled, they all conveyed to my apprehension a feeling of immemorial eld, of years and cycles beyond all record or computation. And all were troubled, all were feverously intent, and were perusing maps or reading ancient pells and volumes, or staring at the sea and sky, or studying the brazen tablets of astronomical parapegms along the streets, as if by so doing they could somehow find the flaw in their reckonings. There were men and women of mature years, and some with the fresh, unlined visages of youth; but in all the place I saw but one child; and the face of the child was no less perplexed and troubled than those of its elders. If anyone ate or drank or carried on the normal occupations of life, it was not done within my scope of vision; and I conceived the idea that they had lived in this manner, obsessed with the same problem, through a period of time which would have been practically eternal in any other world than theirs.

===Chinese===
那一天的余下时光，我都在四处游荡；然而无论何处，我都找不到一个能察觉我存在的人；也无论何处，都没有任何东西能令我安心，或化解我那与日俱增的心神迷乱。到处都是男人，也有女人；尽管他们当中白发苍苍、满面皱纹者相对寥寥，但他们无不给我一种太古洪荒之感，一种超越了所有记载与推算的岁月与纪元之感。所有人都焦灼难安，所有人都狂热地专注于某事，或在研读地图，或在翻阅古老的羊皮卷与典籍，或在对海与天凝望，或在研读街旁那些黄铜所制、铭刻天文历法的历象碑（parapegm），仿佛借由这些举动，他们便能从自己的推算中找出破绽。其中有上了年纪的男女，也有几张容颜鲜润、未生皱纹的青春面孔；然而整个地方，我只看见过一个孩子；而那孩子的脸，与他的长辈们一样写满了迷惘与忧愁。假如有什么人在饮食，或在操持生活里的寻常营生，那也从未落入我的视野之内；我遂生出这样一种念头：他们便是这样生活着的，被同一个难题所攫住，所历经的那段时光，放在他们这个世界之外的任何世界，都几乎可以称之为永恒了。

===Original===
I came to a large building, whose open door was dark with the shadows of the interior. Peering in, I found that it was a temple; for across the deserted twilight, heavy with the stale fumes of burnt-out incense, the slant eyes of a baleful and monstrous image glared upon me. The thing was seemingly of stone or wood, with gorilla-like arms and the malignant features of a subhuman race. From what little I could see in the gloom, it was not pleasant to look upon; and I left the temple, and continued my perambulations.

===Chinese===
我来到一座高大的建筑前，它敞开的门洞因内里的阴影而显得幽暗。朝里窥望，我发觉这是一座神庙；因为隔着那空寂的昏暝——那昏暝中沉甸甸地弥漫着香烬余留的陈腐烟气——一尊凶厉可怖的神像，正以它那双斜睨的眼睛怒视着我。那东西看去似为石质或木质，生着大猩猩般的手臂，以及一副亚人类种族的恶毒面相。凭着昏暗中那点有限的能见度，它实在不是一副赏心悦目的形貌；我遂离开了神庙，继续我的游荡。

===Original===
Now I came to the waterfront, where the vessels with orange sails were moored at a stone mole. There were five or six of them in all: they were small galleys, with single banks of oars, and figureheads of metal that were graven with the likeness of primordial gods. They were indescribably worn by the waves of untold years; their sails were rotting rags; and no less than all else on the island, they bore the imprint of a dread antiquity. It was easy to believe that their grotesquely carven prows had touched the aeon-sunken wharves of Lemuria.

===Chinese===
接着我来到了滨水之处，那些张着橘红帆的船只正系泊在一道石砌的防波堤旁。它们总共有五六艘：都是些小型划桨大船，单层桨座，船首的金属像上镌刻着太古神祇的形貌。它们被不知多少岁月的浪潮侵蚀得难以言状地残旧；它们的风帆已是朽烂的破布；而与岛上的一切别无二致，它们也烙印着一种可怖的古老。要相信它们那雕饰怪诞的船头曾泊靠过利莫里亚（Lemuria）那没入万古的码头，是再容易不过的事。

===Original===
I returned to the town; and once again I sought to make my presence known to the inhabitants, but all in vain. And after a while, as I trudged from street to street, the sun went down behind the island, and the stars came swiftly out in a heaven of purpureal velvet. The stars were large and lustrous and were innumerably thick: with the eye of a practised mariner, I studied them eagerly; but I could not trace the wonted constellations, though here and there I thought that I perceived a distortion or elongation of some familiar grouping. All was hopelessly askew, and disorder crept into my very brain, as I tried once more to orient myself, and noticed that the inhabitants of the town were still busied with a similar endeavor.…

===Chinese===
我回到镇上；再次试图让居民们知道我的存在，却仍是一场徒劳。过了一阵，当我在一条条街道间跋涉时，太阳落到了岛屿的背后，繁星迅即在一片紫红天鹅绒般的天穹上涌现出来。那些星辰又大又亮，密得数不胜数：我以一个老练水手的眼光，热切地研读着它们；可我无论如何也辨认不出惯见的那些星座，尽管偶尔我似乎觉出某一组熟悉的星象有了某种扭曲或拉长。一切都无可救药地歪斜了，当我再次试图辨认方向、并注意到镇上的居民仍在忙于同样的努力时，混乱悄然爬进了我的脑海深处。……

---

===Original===
I have no way of computing the length of my sojourn on the island. Time didn’t seem to have any proper meaning there; and, even if it had, my mental state was not one to admit of precise reckoning. It was all so impossible and unreal, so much like an absurd and troublesome hallucination; and half the time, I thought that it was merely a continuation of my delirium—that probably I was still drifting in the boat. After all, this was the most reasonable supposition; and I don’t wonder that those who have heard my story refuse to entertain any other. I’d agree with them, if it weren’t for one or two quite material details.…

===Chinese===
我无从推算自己在岛上逗留的时日。在那里，时间似乎并无确切的意义可言；即便有，以我当时的心神状态，也无法容许任何精确的计算。这一切是那样的不可能、那样的虚幻，那样像一场荒谬而又恼人的幻觉；有大半的时候，我以为这不过是我的谵妄的延续——多半我仍旧在小艇里漂流着。毕竟，这是最合乎情理的设想；那些听过我这故事的人执意不肯接受任何别的说法，我并不觉得奇怪。若不是为了那么一两桩颇为实在的细节……我也会同他们一般想法。

===Original===
The manner in which I lived is pretty vague to me, also. I remember sleeping under the stars, outside the town; I remember eating and drinking, and watching those people day after day, as they pursued their hopeless calculations. Sometimes I went into the houses and helped myself to food; and once or twice, if I remember rightly, I slept on a couch in one of them, without being disputed or heeded by the owners. There was nothing that could break the spell of their obsession or force them to notice me; and I soon gave up the attempt. And it seemed to me, as time went on, that I myself was no less unreal, no less doubtful and insubstantial, than their disregard would appear to indicate.

===Chinese===
我在那岛上究竟是怎样过活的，在我记忆里也相当模糊。我记得自己在星空下、在镇子外面露宿；我记得自己吃喝，记得日复一日地观察着那些人，看他们如何执拗地进行着那毫无指望的计算。有时我走进那些屋宇，自行取用食物；还有一两次，如果我没记错的话，我睡在其中一户人家的卧榻上，既未受到主人的诘问，也未被他们理会。没有任何东西能打破他们那魔咒般的痴迷，或迫使他们留意到我；我不久便放弃了这番尝试。而且随着时日推移，我渐渐觉得，我自己也并不比他们的无视所显示的那样更真实几分——我也同样虚幻、同样可疑、同样无足轻重。

===Original===
In the midst of my bewilderment, however, I found myself wondering if it would be possible to get away from the island. I remembered my boat, and remembered also that I had no oars. And forthwith I made tentative preparations for departure. In broad daylight, before the eyes of the townspeople, I took two oars from one of the galleys in the harbor, and carried them across the ridge to where my boat was hidden. The oars were very heavy, their blades were broad as fans, and their handles were fretted with hieroglyphs of silver. Also, I appropriated from one of the houses two earthen jars, painted with barbaric figures, and bore them away to the lagoon, intending to fill them with fresh water when I left. And also I collected a supply of food. But somehow the brain-muddling mystery of it all had paralyzed my initiative; and even when everything was ready, I delayed my departure. I felt, too, that the inhabitants must have tried innumerable times to get away in their galleys, and had always failed. And so I lingered on, like a man in the grip of some ridiculous nightmare.

===Chinese===
然而，就在我这般迷惘之中，我却发觉自己竟在琢磨能否离开这座岛屿。我想起了我的小艇，也想起了我并无船桨。于是我便着手为离去做些试探性的准备。在光天化日之下、在镇民众目睽睽之中，我从港口的一艘桨船上取下两把船桨，扛过山脊，送到我藏艇的地方。那两把桨十分沉重，桨叶宽如葵扇，桨柄上镂饰着银色的象形文字。此外，我又从一户人家中取了两只陶罐，罐上绘着蛮荒的图样，我把它们搬到潟湖边，打算临行时装满淡水。我还备下了一应干粮。但不知怎的，这整桩搅乱神思的秘事，已瘫痪了我的决断；即便万事俱备，我却一再拖延着不肯动身。我也觉得，那些居民想必已无数次试着驾着他们的桨船逃离，却总是铩羽而归。于是我就这样踟蹰着、滞留着，活像一个被某种荒诞噩梦所攫住的人。

===Original===
One evening, when those distorted stars had all come out, I became aware that unusual things were going on. The people were no longer standing about in groups, with their customary porings and discussions, but were all hastening toward the temple-like edifice. I followed them, and peered in at the door.

===Chinese===
一天傍晚，当那些扭曲变形的星辰尽数升起之后，我察觉到异乎寻常的事情正在发生。人们不再像往常那样三五成群地站着，俯身研读、彼此讨论，而是全都匆匆朝那座神庙般的建筑赶去。我随着他们，在门口向里张望。

===Original===
The place was lit with flaring torches that flung demoniac shadows on the crowd and on the idol before whom they were bowing. Perfumes were burnt, and chants were sung in the myriad-vowelled language with which my ear had become familiarized. They were invoking that frightful image with gorilla-like arms and half-human, half-animal face; and it was not hard for me to surmise the purpose of the invocation. Then the voices died to a sorrowful whisper, the smoke of the censers thinned, and the little child I had once seen was thrust forward in a vacant space between the congregation and the idol.

===Chinese===
殿内以熊熊燃烧的火把照明，火把将恶魔般的阴影投在人群身上，也投在他们所顶礼膜拜的那尊偶像之上。香料焚烧着，人们以那种元音繁复、我的耳朵已然熟稔的语言唱诵起颂歌。他们正在向那尊形貌骇人、生着大猩猩般手臂与半人半兽面孔的偶像祈求；而这番祈祝的目的，我不难揣度。随后，歌声衰弱成一缕哀伤的低语，香炉的烟气渐渐稀薄，而那个我曾见过的孩童，被推到了会众与偶像之间一片空旷的地带。

===Original===
I had thought, of course, that the god was of wood or stone; but now, in a flash of terror and consternation, I wondered if I had been mistaken. For the oblique eyes opened more widely, and glowered upon the child, and the long arms, ending in knife-taloned fingers, lifted slowly and reached forward. And arrow-sharp fangs were displayed in the bestial grin of the leaning face. The child was still as a bird beneath the hypnotic eyes of a serpent; and there was no movement, and no longer even a whisper, from the waiting throng.…

===Chinese===
我原以为，那神祇不过是木石所制；可此刻，在一阵惊恐与骇然的闪电之中，我不禁怀疑起自己是否看错了。因为那双斜睨的眼睛睁得更大了，凶光毕露地瞪视着那孩子，那两条长长的手臂——末端是利刃般的爪指——缓缓抬起，向前伸去。在那张俯倾下来的脸上，野兽般的狞笑间，露出了箭镞般锋利的獠牙。那孩子动也不动，如同一只被蛇的催眠之眼所镇住的鸟；而那屏息等候的人群，再无半点动静，连一声低语也没有了。……

---

===Original===
I can not recall what happened then: whenever I try to recall it, there is a cloud of horror and darkness in my brain. I must have left the temple and fled across the island by starlight; but of this, too, I remember nothing. My first recollection is of rowing seaward through the narrow chasm by which I had entered the lagoon, and of trying to steer a course by the wried and twisted constellations. After that, there were days and days on a bland, unrippling sea, beneath a heaven of dazzling incandescence; and more nights below the crazy stars; till the days and nights became an eternity of tortured weariness and my food and water were all consumed; and hunger and thirst and a feverous calenture with tossing, seething hallucinations, were all that I knew.

===Chinese===
接下来发生了什么，我已无从回忆：每当我试图回想，脑海中便升起一团恐怖与黑暗的阴云。我必然是离开了神庙、在星光下横穿过整座岛屿逃走了；但连这，我也全无记忆。我最初的印象，便是划着船穿过我当初进入潟湖的那道狭窄罅隙、向海面驶去，并试图凭借那些歪扭畸形的星座来辨别航向。此后的日子里，便是在一片温和、不起一丝涟漪的大海上漂流，头顶是一片灼目炽白的天穹；又有更多的夜晚，在那疯狂的星辰之下度过；直到日日夜夜化作一段饱受煎熬的倦怠的永恒，直到我的食物与淡水消耗殆尽；饥饿、干渴，以及一种伴着翻涌、沸腾的幻觉的发热性谵妄（calenture），便成了我所知的全部。

===Original===
One night, I came to myself for a little while, and lay staring up at the sky. And once more the stars were those of the rightful heavens; and I gave thanks to God for my sight of the Southern Cross, ere I slid back into coma and delirium. And when I recovered consciousness again, I was lying in a ship’s cabin, and the ship’s doctor was bending over me.

===Chinese===
一天夜里，我短暂地清醒了一阵，躺着仰望天空。这一次，繁星终于又恢复了它们本该在天穹上的模样；我在重新滑入昏迷与谵妄之前，为能重见南十字座（Southern Cross）而感谢了上苍。待我再次恢复知觉时，我正躺在一条船的舱室里，船上的医生正俯身在我上方。

===Original===
They were all very kind to me on that ship. But when I tried to tell them my tale, they smiled pityingly; and after a few attempts, I learned to keep my silence. They were very curious about the two oars with silver-fretted handles, and the painted jars which they found with me in the boat; but they were all too frank in refusing to accept my explanation. No such island and no such people could possibly exist, they said: it was contrary to all the maps that had ever been made, and gave the direct lie to all the ethnologists and geographers.

===Chinese===
那条船上的人待我都十分和善。可当我试着把我的经历讲给他们听时，他们只是带着怜悯的微笑；试了几次之后，我便学会了缄口不言。他们对那两把柄上镂有银色花纹的船桨、对那些和我一同在小艇上被发现的彩绘陶罐，都十分好奇；但他们拒绝采信我的解释，且拒绝得直言不讳。他们说，世上绝不可能存在那样的岛屿、那样的人种：这与所有曾经绘制过的地图都相悖，直接戳穿了所有族类学家与地理学家的脸面。

===Original===
Often I wonder about it, myself, for there are so many things I can’t explain. Is there a part of the Pacific that extends beyond time and space—an oceanic limbo into which, by some unknowable cataclysm, that island passed in a bygone period, even as Lemuria sank beneath the wave? And if so, by what abrogation of dimensional laws was I enabled to reach the island and depart from it? These things are beyond speculation. But often in my dream, I see again the incognizably distorted stars, and share the confusion and bafflement of a lost people, as they pore above their useless charts, and take the altitude of a deviated sun.

===Chinese===
我自己也常对此暗自纳罕，因为有太多的事情我无从解释。太平洋是否有一片延伸到时空之外的所在——一片汪洋的林勃狱，那座岛屿便是在某个往昔的年代，因某种不可知的灾劫而遁入其中的，一如利莫里亚沉没于波涛之下？倘若如此，又是凭借怎样的维度法则的废止，我得以抵达那座岛屿、又得以从中离去？这些事已非揣度所能企及。但在我的梦里，我常常重新看见那些无可辨认地扭曲变形的星辰，重新分担一个迷失族群的那份迷惘与挫败——他们俯身在那些徒劳无用的海图之上，测量着一轮偏离正轨的太阳的高度。
